# utxo-partial-order: design notes

## State of the series, and how to read this document

The series is 45 commits on master dd96035e03, tip 3b19733898 (2026-11-28, the block bins folded in, the store's cell pools added, a prune and a batch folded in), listed in 9.3: 138 files, 21301 insertions and 1052 deletions against master. It stands on the branch `utxo-partial-order`. The notes do not follow the history of the branch: a passage that gives a tip says which tree it describes, and a commit is named by what it does.

The state of the series:

- The design: the store knows no block before it is valid and keeps no entry lists; a window block is validated read-only against the store and the outputs of the lower blocks in flight, and committed by the connector in the hold that publishes it, one commit job at a time (4.2, 4.5, 4.6). Received blocks are parsed and stored by a receive pool of five threads of its own (4.7), and every block is freed by a task of the connect pool (4.3).
- Built and tested (9.2): on the night of 27 to 28 November, after the fixes of the ralph review (4.11), every one of the 44 commits built on its own (RelWithDebInfo, clang, `DEBUG_LOCKORDER` and `ABORT_ON_FAILED_ASSUME`, every target but the kernel library, the fuzz binary included) and passed the unit suite; on the tip's tree the full functional suite (503 passed, 23 skipped), `feature_dbcrash.py`, ThreadSanitizer (the unit suite and the window's functional tests, no report) and the TLA+ models passed. The kernel library and `test_kernel` built and passed on the tip, on the maintainer's word that day (clang, RelWithDebInfo, `build-kernel`); `bitcoinkernel.cpp` changed twice (the block cache default, an undo failure failing `process_block`).
- The maintainer's runs (7.1): a mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` of 10:07.16 on 26 November ("I think that is an ok de-tune."); the full functional suite and `feature_dbcrash.py` passed on the trees of 24 and 25 November, and the mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` took 9:44.91 on the tree of 25 November. Not run on the tip: the full functional suite, `feature_dbcrash.py`, ThreadSanitizer, a network sync on the maintainer's machine (not planned for now), and a compile of the kernel library on the harness host.
- File work: the I/O at the edges of 4.14; the rule is written in `validation.h` under "File I/O".
- Untested and unmeasured: the paths of 5.5; no slow-disk measurement exists for any of the file work.

Reading rules:

- A commit is named by what it does. Where a number stands for a commit it is a position in the list of 9.3.
- Sections 4.2, 4.5, 4.6, 5.5 and 9.3 were written against the code at the tip on 2026-11-24; the present-tense claims of sections 1 to 3, the rest of 4 except 4.11 to 4.13, and 5.2 to 5.7 and 6 were checked against it the same day by a reader who saw only the notes and the code. Sections 3, 4 and 9.3 were brought to the tip f77760e49a on 2026-11-26 where the removals of that day (5.9) touched them. Section 2 is quoted as it stands; on 2026-11-26 the state lines of C1, C7, C8, C15 and C16 and the "Never checked" line were brought to the tip, and nothing else in it.
- Sections 4.11 to 4.13, 5.1, 5.2a, 5.2b, 7 and 8 are dated records: each entry names its date or its tip and describes the tree of that day. Before 24 November the window applied a block's changes into the store while the block was validated, and those entries name its mechanisms (a block's record in the store, deferred transactions and their continuations, the spent mark, the retry serial). A mechanism that 4.2 and 4.5 do not describe is not in the series.
- Figures were measured on the tree their entry names. The harness figures of the tip are in 4.5, the maintainer's runs in 7.1.

This document is the design record. The working records it points to are local files and are not part of it:

- Plans and their records: `/work/utxo-partial-order-safety-plan.md`; `/work/parallel-validation/review-plan-full-series.md` with the round files beside it, and `performance-suggestions.md`; `/work/parallel-validation/consolidation-plan.md`; `/work/parallel-validation/overlay-consolidation-plan.md`.
- Patches: `/work/patches/utxo-partial-order.mbox` is the series; `utxo-partial-order-v1.mbox` is an older export, kept for reference.
- The harness: `/work/utxo-partial-order-harness`, with chain DX kept in `chains/chainDX`.
- Worktree: `/work/bitcoin/.claude/worktrees/parallel-validation-machines` holds the series.
- Branches: `utxo-partial-order` (the series), `utxo-partial-order-overlay-trial` (the read-only window as it was built, before the fixes of 9.2 and the last commit) and `utxo-partial-order-baseline` (master e211dd0186 plus the harness probe, 6.3). A branch of another name in the text may not exist.
- The TLA+ specifications in `/work/utxo-partial-order-tla` (9.2): `WindowCommit` (the commit, its take-back, a run and a crash after it), `WindowOutputs` (the window's outputs entered, read and freed by the workers) and `LagRuns` (lag runs against reorganisations and crashes); beside them `WindowCommitSeq`, `Durability` and `ReceivePool` (the receive pool, block I/O and the connect pool against the connector's waits under `cs_main`).

The design document of the series: its state by topic. Each fact stands in one place; other places point to it by section number. A new result is entered at its topic and replaces what it supersedes; no dated section is appended. The state of the series is at the head of the document; a commit is best found by its subject.

Where a statement is measured it says so; the rest is read from the code. Marks on figures: R a real sync or the maintainer's machine, H harness (direction only), I inferred, "none" never measured alone. Standing caveat: harness ratios landed at about a third on the real machine.

## 1. Goal

Three ideas went in. From sipa's post in the libbitcoin thread: a missing input is an obligation to settle later, not a failure, so applying blocks stops being a total order. From libbitcoin: validation split from confirmation, only confirmation ordered. From Hornet and the research's section 9.5: a store written by age at a steady rate, with nothing emptied, and a compact entry. The window, its outputs table and the ordered commit deliver the first two and the window tests pin them; the third was delivered in mechanism at first and in effect only through the work of sections 4.4 and 4.8.

Ruling of the maintainer (2026-11-13): the series exists to remove every limitation on the validation side that does not scale with more hardware, so that the p2p part can then be optimised freely. Lines of code are not a criterion against it. That the connect outruns the delivery on a real sync is the intended result; the master reindex figure is of interest but decides nothing. The yardstick for each component is whether it removes or leaves a non-scaling limit in validation.

Phases: speed work on the connect; then, on the maintainer's word, consolidation and low-resource defaults (tuning instrumentation removed); the connect work paused at the 17:39 run and the receive side taken up; the design review of 2026-11-13 (sections 2, 4.1, 5, 7.6).

The commits are listed in 9.3; the gates of the consolidation of the state-machine work into them are in `/work/parallel-validation/consolidation-plan.md` section 8.

## 2. Constraints, each with its state

The text is the plan's section 1 (stage references left out), the state is the design review's, read at 10588 lines over 72 files and carried forward to the tip.

- **Consensus results identical.** Holds. Shown by the store oracle against a cache (`same_as_cache_under_random_operations`), the window cases of `validation_chainstate_tests` (`window_step_block_by_block`, `invalid_window_block_is_judged_alone`, `double_spend_in_the_window_is_taken_back`), `feature_connect_window.py`, `validation_core_tests` and the unchanged functional suite; `src/script` is untouched, and `src/consensus` has refactors with the same rules (the sequence-lock overloads). The one recorded gap (BIP30, 4.6) is closed. Open: no fuzz target drives the store.
- **Crash safety equal to master.** Shown in part: `feature_async_undo` aborts before the first undo write, `feature_coins_lag` aborts inside write-out runs with reorgs mixed in and warns itself that the hook may not fire. Never exercised: crash recovery under load, a crash of a pruned node.
- **C1, the four architecture goals.** Pure validation functions (the core reads only its arguments and the view: no member, global, clock, log, file or lock); pure state machines (named transitions, asserted phases); asynchronous I/O (every write and sync is an effect on an executor, never under a validation lock); a radical reduction of `cs_main` scope. Oracles: `validation_core_tests` running the core with no `TestingSetup`, the `StagedConnect` phase assertions, the thread-safety annotations on a clang build. State: partly. Purity amended 15 November (the maintainer, "fine for now"): the core reads nothing of the node's (no member, global, file or lock; policy and time through BlockValidationContext); logging is allowed in the core and the consensus functions (amended 15 November, round 11 X7/V4, the maintainer: "Logging in the consensus functions is obviously fine!"; e.g. master's LogError lines in the free DisconnectBlock), and the one other exception is master's bench timing, a steady-clock read moved with the code it times; the purity script is dropped as an oracle. "No I/O under a validation lock" has one exception left: the per-acceptance `FlushStateToDisk` under cs_main that waits for the effects thread (4.7), since the pruning waits only when pruning takes files (the prune event's block-index write and unlink stay under the lock, as in master), and since the IO-4 fix of 5.1 not at all while a step that uses the window runs (`m_window_steps_running`: that step's periodic write checks for pruning instead; the external block loader keeps the check); the full write once forced before the first disconnect of a reorg is gone since 14 November (4.4).
- **C2, default architecture, no new IBD mode.** No hints, no second validation path, no per-mode configuration. The window and the lag are run-time policies of one code path: the window is one when one block is available and larger when blocks are queued; the lag follows the memory budget. `-connectwindow=1` was meant as the same code with the window pinned; in the code it takes the serial path (`use_window` requires `ConnectWindow() > 1`). Known deviation, kept on the maintainer's word (15 November, round 11 X1/W1/T1/V1): the window, the receive hand-off and the request rule are chosen by `IsInitialBlockDownload()`, against this constraint and C17; a node that falls behind after the latch catches up serially; the historical chainstate connecting serially is wanted (it leaves the pool and memory to the active chain). Candidate for later (B): the active chainstate takes the window when more than one block is ready, the historical chainstate stays serial; it needs a catch-up functional test with a live mempool, an IBD re-measurement, and C17's reading written down (its text forbids asking whether a block is the only one in flight). C (the window always, also for one tip block) belongs to 5.2a. Also accepted (15 November, round 12 X2): outside initial block download each block's undo write and completions are awaited under cs_main, as master writes undo synchronously in ConnectBlock; inside it they stay asynchronous. The wait is kept for notification order (BlockConnected and UpdatedBlockTip leave with the block, announcements by headers, a transaction announced after the block it builds on), which does not matter in initial block download; no code change. Added later by the maintainer: no further special-case rules for when the window opens. State: holds. Two new options, `-blockcache` and `-connectwindow`; `-prevoutfetchthreads` sizes the connect pool.
- **C3, the tip as fast or faster than today, with everything the tip already has.** The block at the tip is reconstructed from the mempool through compact blocks, its transactions arrive hashed, its inputs are warm, and its scripts are mostly not run because the script execution and signature caches hold the mempool's verification. Each must survive: no re-hashing of reconstructed transactions, a store whose lookups warm the coins the mempool touches, the `ValidationCache` passed into the core unchanged, parallel prevout fetching for cold inputs. A block connected alone is validated as today: a missing coin fails. Oracles: a `ConnectBlock` bench on a mainnet block with prevouts in a warm store and scripts off, and a regtest bench that accepts a block's transactions into a mempool first and then connects the block; both against master. State: the tip bench through the store exists since 15 November (ConnectBlockMainnetTipStore against TipOverlay, 5.2a): the store's round trip 1.14x master's shape, the commit of the block's changes the difference (measured on the tree of that day); the regtest mempool bench has no figure at a recent tip.
- **C4, low memory and few cores.** IBD must work on a node with 4 GB of RAM and a system-on-chip CPU. Every in-memory structure has a budget and the budgets sum to one stated figure. (The plan's "worker default not from the core count" is superseded by one thread a core, 4.9.) State: open, see 7.6; the byte budget per structure does not exist as one table.
- **C5, ultraprune.** Nothing requires a block older than the prune depth: a pruned node prunes only below the written head (the lag cap is gone, amended 15 November, 5.7), so a replay after a crash reads only kept blocks, pruning never removes a file inside the lag, and undo writes are settled before a block file is pruned. The stored UTXO set never exceeds what a client running today's code has at the same height: what is on disk is a subset of the set at the lag's lower head, with no history beside it; the write-out may issue an erase for a coin that was never written (accepted, 5.5), which leaves nothing on disk. At shutdown the lag is drained so the stored set is exactly the tip's. No on-disk representation that keeps deleted entries past compaction. State: mechanism present (4.10). No new functional test with `-prune`; no pruned sync measured in isolation.
- **C6, optional indexes unchanged.** `BlockConnected` fires per block in height order after the block's undo data is written; the indexes see exactly what they see today. State: shown for one case (`feature_async_undo` with `-txindex` and `-coinstatsindex`).
- **C7, blocks kept in memory are bounded.** The `-blockcache` budget bounds the parsed blocks in flight; nothing holds a block past acceptance outside that budget, the window's blocks included. State: partly; blocks held by receive tasks (at most five, 4.7) and the window lie outside it (7.6); every block is freed by the thread that built it (`ChainstateManager::NewBlock()`, the block bins, 4.3).
- **C8, no additional thread pools; asynchrony through effects.** One pool runs the apply tasks and the prevout fetch tasks; the script check queue remains for the tip. A block waiting for lower blocks holds no thread. All I/O stays on the effects executors and the block I/O pool, except one blocking read issued by the apply workers: database reads for old coins. State: amended on 22 November (the maintainer: I/O to the edges is a goal of the series, a pool of blocking threads is fine, as little OS-specific code as possible): block and undo file I/O is made on the block manager's pool of four blocking threads (`RunBlockIO()`) or on the ordered effects executor, never by an apply worker (4.14); the received block's write in initial block download and the read of a window block that is not in memory are made on the pool; the database reads for old coins are the one blocking I/O the workers still issue. amended 15 November (the maintainer: the helper threads are fine, the short-lived flush threads not): the connect pool, the effects executor thread and the connector thread are accepted beside master's script check queue; a forced flush's further workers are tasks of the connect pool (claimed ranges, so a flush holding cs_main never waits for a queued task). Amended 26 November (the maintainer: "Ok, go.", 5.9): received blocks are deserialised, checked and stored by a pool of their own, the receive pool (`RECEIVE_THREADS` = 5 threads, none when the connect pool has fewer than two workers; 4.7), so that no task of the connect pool takes cs_main (I9).
- **C9, optimise for mainnet.** Every measurement and bench uses mainnet data or mainnet blocks where one can be used. Empty blocks and signet are not design inputs. **C10, measure, don't guess; gains hardware-independent.** No knob is estimated. Expected gains are ratios derived from a measured share, never times on a machine class. Measurements on real data are the maintainer's to run. **C11, no time or effort estimates.** Sequencing and gates only. State of the three: process rules, kept.
- **C12, per-block fixed costs are multiplied by the block count.** Each stage states how many hand-offs per block it adds, keeps that number fixed and small, has a bench for it, and the tip path has none. State: counted 15 November from the code: a block from a peer in initial block download crosses threads about five times (handler to receive task, task to connector, connector to window task, the worker's finished block back to the connector, undo write to the effects thread with its BlockConnected riding the same queue); a tip block three times (handler to connector, undo write and notification, the block freed by a pool task). The tip departs from "none": both tip hand-offs were measured at no cost (tip latency parity, the connector decision); the pool's wake-up has its bench (threadpool bench, with the pause as baseline).
- **C13, the flush is a symptom.** The cause is removed, the total order and the burst write-out, not the symptom. A change whose only effect is to make the flush less visible does not count toward a gate. State: amended 15 November (the maintainer): the cause is removed by the lag (runs of the oldest changes); the write path's own efficiency work (key-range shards, sorted whole-shard batches, the flush workers) is accepted on its own merits and counts toward no gate, since the full write still happens at shutdown, a cache resize and the periodic write.
- **C14, trust unchanged.** Nothing is assumed beyond assumevalid. Every consensus rule is evaluated; a window block's failure decides nothing until the block has been connected alone (I7), and a check whose input is unknown fails rather than defaulting. State: holds.
- **C15, deliverable form.** An mbox of per-commit patches that applies to master with `git am --3way`; nothing is pushed; no code until the maintainer triggers it. The kernel library is not built during development; every new source file that belongs to it is added to `src/kernel/CMakeLists.txt` in the commit that introduces it. Commits carry no assistant trailers (the maintainer's later word, superseding the plan). State: holds; the kernel source list is complete; the maintainer built the kernel library and `test_kernel` passed on 25 November (9.2); it was never compiled on the harness host.
- **C16, existing tests are the oracle.** Functional tests are not changed except where an on-disk file or a log line they read has changed, each edit named in the commit message. Unit tests change only where the interface they call changed, minimally. New tests may be added freely. A design that would need an existing functional test rewritten is a finding against the design. State: two existing assertions change, each following from a deliberate behaviour change: `p2p_segwit.py` (one log text, `TxSegmentRecorder::read()` for `DataStream::read()`, the reader the series reads a transaction with, 9.2) and `chainstatemanager_args` in `validation_chainstatemanager_tests` (the default is `node::DefaultPrevoutFetchThreads()` and the clamp is tried at 2000). `chainstate_write_tests` was a third and is master's file again since 22 November (5.3). Every other change to an existing test file adds: new cases, the one-line markers of 4.14 (`blockmanager_tests`, `interfaces_tests`, `validation_chainstate_tests`, `util/blockfilter.cpp`), test utilities, small additions to the fuzz targets, and the runner and framework registrations. `feature_coinstatsindex` is master's again since the forced write before a reorg went (14 November). `p2p_ibd_stalling` passes with the request rule.

Given by the maintainer after the plan, without a number:

- **The low-end write-out logic stays** (lag runs, eviction, the owed erase). Holds, with unit cases (`streamed_run_records_the_settled_head`, `evict_clean_and_bound_for_bytes`, `owed_erase_survives_a_create`).
- **Network work changes the receiving side only, no protocol change.** Holds: `net.cpp`, `net.h` and `protocol` untouched. A peer can observe two things: a ping is answered only after that peer's handed-off blocks are processed, and a slow peer is asked for fewer blocks at a time.
- **C17, performance work treats every block alike (15 November, the maintainer).** No code asks whether the chain is at the tip, or whether a block is the only one in flight, to choose a cheaper path; and a gain for connecting a single block applies to blocks connected during initial block download too. Decisions may follow from the data (how many of a block's inputs miss the tier), never from where the chain is. Rules out a solo-block confirm and an inline connect when the connector is idle; allows a prefetch sized by its misses. An unwind is driven by the block's undo data: the take-back of a commit (4.5).
- **No LevelDB tuning.** Holds. There is no LevelDB LOG file in the chainstate directory to ask for; the direction is the pipeline and our side of the flush.
- **Allocation failures terminate and logic errors stay uncaught.** Holds (4.5 for the pool; the broad handler around the compaction names `std::system_error`).

Never checked: crash recovery under load; C3's benches; the historical chainstate under a snapshot, which gets neither window nor connector.

The four wordings once open here (C8, C13, C1's purity, C16) were ruled on 15 November (4.11). The list as yardstick was taken as accepted with "Continue".

## 3. Invariants

From the behaviour-only specification of 2026-11-09 (the specification and its 149 checks were used up by a review round and removed). Re-read against the code on 2026-11-23: I1 to I5, I10 and I12 hold as worded; I9, I11 and I13 are worded here for what the code does now. I6, I7 and I8 were worded anew on 2026-11-24 for the window that commits a block once it is valid, I9 on 2026-11-26 for the receive pool.

- I1 Readers under the main lock see a chain tip and a UTXO set of the same height; a block's changes appear atomically with the tip update.
- I2 Every consensus verdict (accept or reject, and the reason), the undo data and the fee total equal in-order validation's.
- I3 Before a run the database holds the state at the marker's old head; each write is the outpoint's value at the new head; replay repairs any prefix. The settled head after a run is a block whose state the database holds.
- I4 A fresh entry is never in the database; an entry collected by a run is not fresh; a spent dirty entry is kept until its erase lands; a coin created and spent inside the lag is never written. The entry machine's table (`coins_entry_machine.h`, `IsStored()`) carries this now.
- I5 Change tags: the block height for connects; the fork height for a reorg's inverse changes, except restored coins created above the settled height; every change tagged at or below a run's bound is carried by the run, or holds the settled height down.
- I6 No entry of the store belongs to a block that is not published in the same hold of `cs_main`: a window block's changes stay in its view until its commit, every block of a commit is published in the hold that made it, and between two of them nothing writes the coins (`Chainstate::m_commits_ahead`); `EvictClean` takes clean entries only (4.4).
- I7 Conflicts are charged to the block at fault. A window block's failure is provisional: the window drains, a commit that met a conflict is taken back from the block at fault up (`TakeBackCommit()`), the block is connected alone, and that verdict decides (4.5). A block's own verdict is never altered by another block's actions on the view.
- I8 A block above a failed or abandoned block leaves nothing in the store. The window drains and each such block's view is given back uncommitted (`DrainWindow()`); of a commit that met a conflict, the blocks from the one at fault up are taken back with their undo data before anything else happens (`TakeBackCommit()`).
- I9 No pool task waits for a task of its own pool, and work that is waited for while a listed lock is held takes none of those locks (`WAITED_FOR_UNDER_LOCK`, `AssertMayWait`, `NO_WAIT_LOCK` in sync.h). Receive tasks, the one kind of pool task that takes `cs_main`, run on a pool of their own (`RECEIVE_THREADS`, 4.7), so that no task of the connect pool takes it.
- I10 Block index batches commit in preparation order; the marker never names a head whose block, undo and index data are not durable.
- I11 The window never makes the node accept a block it would reject alone, or the reverse. Outside IBD the verdicts are master's; the path is not: activation runs on the connector thread, a block connected alone queues its undo write before its commit (`CommitAlone()`) and takes the coins its commit misses from its undo data, and the previous block's transactions are freed off the new block's path (the three tip commits of 9.3).
- I12 A crash at any point (inside a run, inside a block, at a reorg, at shutdown) is followed by a start-up replay that yields a UTXO set equal to that of a node that never crashed.
- I13 Memory: the store's usage is bounded by `-dbcache` as the cache's was (a write-out run's target is one half of the budget, 4.4). What lies outside it is counted by its holder (the block cache's account, `ChainstateManager::m_window_bytes` and `m_pinned_bytes`, the effects executor's undo backlog; `util::ByteAccount`) or listed in 4.9.

Stated in the code and not repeated in this list: the ordering preconditions O1 to O15 (validation.h), the machines with their tables (entry, window block, handoff, block store, the flush order; `util::Lifecycle`), and transaction acceptance waiting for blocks that are still with a task (`WaitForPendingBlocks`).

## 4. The design as it stands

### 4.1 Inventory

The table is the inventory of the design review of 2026-11-13 (7.6): lines are a commit's insertions with its tests at the review tip of that day (they sum to 11288 against the net 10588). The series is 136 files, 20651 insertions and 1002 deletions against master at the tip (the head) and was not split by component again; rows 10 to 12 describe the store's commit and the window as they are, and their lines were not counted. Cells are corrected where today's mechanism differs. Not in the table because they came later: the never-written marker and the window below the BIP34 height (4.6), the prune rule (4.10), the block cache default (4.9) and the removal of the hash pool (row 5); and everything added since (the state machines, the compact store, the tip fixes, the file work; 9.3). The contribution column is the one place a component's headline ratio stands; the sections named give the conditions.

| 11 | The connect window: blocks validated read-only, committed in order | connect pool; a block waits for the lower blocks to be entered, the publisher for the lowest block | window cap (4.9), outside `-dbcache` | not recounted | the first two ideas of section 1 | the series' main gain: reindex against master 2.3 to 8.6x by core count on the tree of 13 November (H, 4.9); the tip against cecbce8958 level on the harness and 10:36.16 against 10:32.54 on mainnet (4.5, 7.1); no master run beside a real one |

| 14 | Receive pool with the request at the hand-off | a pool of its own, `RECEIVE_THREADS` (5) threads, none where the connect pool has fewer than two workers; the handler waits for a free task slot | messages and blocks of at most five tasks, outside every budget | 435 | deserialisation and acceptance off the one handler | 2.15 to 2.2x on a local sync at 12 cores, 1.15x at 4, 1.0x at two, measured while the tasks ran on the connect pool (H); on five threads of their own level with that (5.9); benchcoin at the level of before (R: that benchmark waits for its peer, 7.5) |

| 16 | Kept-node lists | per thread | a list of 1024 cells of 88 bytes, up to two lists a thread, outside the accounting | 58 | cross-thread free of map nodes | 1.12 to 1.19x (H), 1.043x on the connect (R) |
