### 4.14 File I/O at the edges

Written on 2026-11-23 against the code at tip b5fc79e546 and brought to the tip on 2026-11-26 (commits 11 to 14 and 27 to 29 of 9.3). The rule itself stands in `validation.h` under "File I/O", after O15, and the code is the authority where the two differ; this section gives the reasons, follows one block through the files, and says which component owns which piece. The harness figures are under "I/O work on the harness" in 5.2a.

**The philosophy.** The validation side is a set of state machines whose transitions run under `cs_main`. A transition should only decide and record: it reads the block index, picks a position, flips a status, and is over. Everything that touches a block or undo file (reading, writing, allocating space, trimming, syncing, unlinking) is work that takes as long as the disk takes, so it is made between two states, with `cs_main` free, and the only thing that waits for it is the thing it is made for: the one block being stored, the one window block being read, the one reorganisation step being prepared. The maintainer set this as a goal of the series on 22 November ("pushing IO to the edges is something I would very much like to achieve") and confirmed that it is meant strictly ("Yes, it is meant strictly."). The instruction for the work was to put all block and undo writes and reads where they would ideally belong and to iterate on the design with the benchmark harness. Four choices follow from it:

- Blocking threads, not asynchronous I/O interfaces. The maintainer's words: "A pool of blocking threads seems fine" and "as little OS-specific hacking as possible". A read or a write blocks the thread it is made on, so it is given threads that do nothing else, and the connect workers stay on the processor. There is no io_uring, no AIO, no direct I/O.
- Two lanes, split by whether order matters. File work that must happen in a fixed order with other things (an undo write before the notification of its block, a sync before the index batch that records the bytes) goes through the ordered effects executor, one thread, `BlockManager::m_effects`. File work that has no order with anything else (a block's bytes read or written, a pruned file removed) goes to the block I/O pool, `BlockManager::RunBlockIO()`, four threads named `blockio` (`BLOCK_IO_THREADS`). A task of the pool never takes `cs_main`, because a thread that holds it may be waiting for another task queued behind this one, and it lets no exception leave it.
- Under the lock, arithmetic; off the lock, the system calls. Claiming a position is a sum and a note of which chunks the position reaches (`ClaimChunks()`); whoever writes there allocates the space first (`Preallocate()`, under a leaf mutex of the file sequence). A full disk is therefore found by the write and no longer by the claim; the fatal error is the same.
- The rule is checked, and its exceptions are named in the code. A rule of this kind erodes silently, so with DEBUG_LOCKORDER the block manager's lock-free file functions stop the node when their thread holds `cs_main`, and every place that does file work under the lock on purpose is a scope that says why (below).

What the rule does not try to do: it does not reorder the block write behind the connect (asked and withdrawn by the maintainer, "No, don't do that": O2 and O11 rest on a block being written before it is recorded as stored), and it does not optimise the writes and reads of a tip block or an RPC (the maintainer: "little point in optimizing those"); those stay on their callers' threads, already outside `cs_main`.

**The lifetime of a block, by its file work.** The path is that of a block received during the initial block download; where another path differs it is said at the step.

1. *Received.* The message handler hands the block's bytes to a receive task on the receive pool (4.7). The task deserialises and hashes, and runs `ChainstateManager::AcceptNewBlock()`: the block's own checks without the lock, a hold of `cs_main` that accepts the header, the contextual checks without the lock again, and a second hold that claims the block's place on disk. The claim is arithmetic. It also pins the file (`PinBlockFile()`), so that pruning leaves a file alone while a write is in flight for it. No file is touched.
2. *Written.* `WriteNewBlock()` runs on a block I/O thread: it allocates the chunks the bytes need and writes them. This is the stretch between "being stored" and stored of the block store machine (O2). No lock is held and nothing waits for it except this block. Outside the initial block download, and for a block the handler keeps, the same three functions run one after another on the caller's thread (`ProcessNewBlock()`), the write again with `cs_main` released.
3. *Stored.* `FinishNewBlock()` runs on a receive pool thread again, queued at the front since the block is nearly done: one hold records the block as stored, unpins the file, puts the block into the block cache and signals the connector. If the write position left a file while a write was still in flight for it, the file's trim is queued when its last write is in (`m_blockfiles_to_trim`, `UnpinBlockFile()`); otherwise it was queued at the file switch (`QueueBlockFileTrim()`). The trim is the executor's and comes without a sync: the file stays marked unsynced until whatever records its blocks in the block index syncs it.
4. *Staged for the window.* The connector stages the block. A block that is in memory (passed in, or in the block cache) needs no file. One that is not, which is every block of a `-reindex-chainstate` and any block the cache let go, has its bytes read on a block I/O thread (`Chainstate::ReadForConnectTask()`, `ReadBlockBytes()`), and the connect task is submitted with the bytes; the worker parses them (`ParseBlock()`). The split of `ReadBlock()` into its I/O half and its parsing half exists for this hand-over. With a pool without workers the connector reads the block itself, and so it does for a block connected alone that is not in memory; in both cases it holds no `cs_main` over the read.
5. *Connected and published.* The workers validate against the store and read no file; the one blocking I/O left to them is a coin database read for an old coin. At publication, under `cs_main`, the block's undo position is claimed (`ClaimUndoPos()`, arithmetic again) and the undo write is queued as an effect. From here on the availability checks count the undo data as present (`HasStatus()`), because the write is in order before anything that may read or prune it.
6. *Undo written.* The executor writes the undo data with no lock held (`WriteBlockUndoAt()`, which allocates like the block write) and its completion records the position under `cs_main` (`OnUndoWritten()`); the block's `BlockConnected` rides the same queue behind it (C6). When this write is the last of its undo file, the same effect trims and syncs the file (`FinishUndoFile()`), ahead of any write-out run queued after it. A block connected alone queues its undo write before its commit (`CommitAlone()`; the tip commits, 9.3).
7. *Made durable.* A write-out run's effect makes the syncs its block index batch owes (`SyncBatchFiles()`) on the executor before it commits the batch, so the index never records bytes a crash could lose (I10; `CommitBlockIndexBatch()` asserts that a batch owes no sync). A forced flush syncs under `cs_main`, by a caller that waits for exactly that; it is one of the named exceptions. Which files owe a sync is kept in the block manager's sets `m_unsynced_block_files` and `m_unsynced_undo_files`, whose documentation states the rule; `FlushUndoFile()` asserts that no undo write is queued for the file it syncs, since its callers settle the queue first.
8. *Pruned.* The files to prune are chosen and the block index that no longer records them is written under `cs_main` as before (4.10); the files themselves are removed on a block I/O thread afterwards. `pruneblockchain` removes them inline, since its caller waits for the result. A write position that is in a file being pruned moves to an unclaimed file number first (`LeaveCursorFile()`), so a pruned file's number is never claimed again, which both the late removal and the chunk map rely on. This is what lets a node whose background validation has finished prune the last file of the validated chain, as master does (`wallet_assumeutxo.py`).
9. *Disconnected, if a reorganisation reaches it.* A step that will disconnect is planned in one hold (`Chainstate::PlanReorgReads()`): up to `REORG_READ_AHEAD` = 16 blocks with their undo data from the tip down. They are read on the block I/O threads with no lock held (`ReadForReorg()`), and the next hold checks that tip and target are still the planned ones and takes each block by its index entry. `InvalidateBlock()` reads each block it disconnects before that block's hold. What was not read ahead is read under the hold as on master: the blocks of the new branch that are not in memory (passed in, or in the block cache), the disconnected blocks past the first 16, a read that failed, and a step that follows one whose branch was invalid, where the locks are not released in between.

**Who is responsible for what.**

| Component | Owns | Does not do |
|---|---|---|
| A transition under `cs_main` (acceptance, publication, the flush's decisions) | deciding and recording: positions claimed by arithmetic, pins, the queueing of trims and effects, the block index in memory | open, read, write, allocate, trim, sync or unlink a file |
| Block I/O pool, `RunBlockIO()`, four `blockio` threads | unordered, blocking file work: the write of a block received in the initial block download, the read of a window block that is not in memory, a reorganisation's read-ahead, the removal of automatically pruned files | take `cs_main` or any listed lock; keep an order; parse or validate |
| Effects executor, `m_effects`, one thread | ordered file work and what must follow it: undo writes with their completions and notifications, the trim of a file that is finished, the trim and sync of a finished undo file, write-out runs with the syncs their index batch owes | anything that needs no order; its completions take `cs_main` only by `TRY_LOCK` or on a thread that already holds it |
| Receive pool, `RECEIVE_THREADS` (5) threads | a received block's deserialisation, hashing, checks, acceptance and finish step (4.7) | block or undo file I/O |
| Connect pool workers | parsing, hashing, validation; coin database reads | block or undo file I/O |
| Connector thread | staging: it decides whether a block needs a read and queues it; plans a reorganisation's reads and waits for them between two holds | read files itself, except with a pool without workers |
| Block manager | the mechanism: claim (`ClaimChunks()`), allocation (`Preallocate()`), pins, the deferred trim, `ReadBlockBytes()`/`ParseBlock()`, and the check | policy: it does not decide when or on which thread a caller does its work, only refuses the work under `cs_main` |
| Block cache | keeping a received block in memory between its write and its connection, so that step 4 needs no file on a network sync | bound the window's blocks (4.9) |
| A caller that waits for the result itself | its own file work, on its own thread: tip and RPC block writes, getdata and RPC reads | hold `cs_main` over it, unless it is one of the named exceptions |

**The check and the exceptions.** `AssertFileWorkOffMain()` sits in the six block manager functions that take no lock themselves: `OpenBlockFile()` and `OpenUndoFile()`, which every read and write passes through, `Preallocate()`, `SyncBatchFiles()`, `FinishUndoFile()` and `UnlinkPrunedFiles()`. With DEBUG_LOCKORDER they stop the node when their thread holds `cs_main`; without it nothing is checked. A caller that works under the lock on purpose opens a scope `BLOCK_FILE_WORK_UNDER_CS_MAIN(why)`, and the scopes are the complete list of the rule's exceptions, thirteen in production code: at the start, the opening of every block file, the removal of files pruned before the stop, verifying (also for `verifychain`) and the replay of an interrupted write-out; a block accepted in one call (the external block loader and tests); the block write and the undo write in one call (the genesis block, tests and benchmarks); the undo data of a block disconnected under the lock where it was not read before, the block to disconnect that was not read before the hold, and a reorganisation's block to connect that is not in memory (a scope opened through `std::optional<node::BlockFileWorkUnderMain>`); the undo data of a window block taken back under the publish hold, written before the block was staged; `pruneblockchain`, which waits for its files to be removed; and the block read in `SendMessages` for a compact block announcement of a tip that is not the most recent block in memory. The prose list in `validation.h` names the same set by what the caller is doing. The functions that require `cs_main` by annotation, the syncs of a forced flush, are exceptions by that annotation and carry no check. Seven tests that read or write under a hold of their own carry a one-line marker (`blockmanager_tests`, `util/blockfilter.cpp`, `interfaces_tests`, `validation_chainstate_tests`; the fuzz target `connect_block` has one too and was not compiled here).

**Is it pure?** Not strictly, and the gaps are the list above. The initial block download path is: accept, write, store, read, undo write, trim, sync and prune each run with `cs_main` free, and the check holds on that path. The reorganisation path is pure for the first 16 blocks it disconnects and falls back to master's reads under the hold beyond that and for the blocks of the new branch that are not in memory. The fallback was kept on purpose: it is the correct behaviour for a failed or missing read, it bounds the memory a reorganisation holds, and a deeper reorganisation is rare enough that nothing was built for it. The start-up work and the RPC exceptions hold the lock over file work as master does, with a caller that wants exactly that result.

**Beside #36000** ("validation: prefetch blocks while connecting", l0rinc, open on 2026-11-23; read, not built). It goes in the same direction for the read side only: a `node::BlockFetcher` per `Chainstate` with a pool of its own (`blockread`, two threads by default, `-blockfetchthreads`), which reads the next blocks ahead while one connects and, on a reorganisation, the first blocks of the new branch. It is looser than the rule here: `ConnectTip()` waits for the read under `cs_main`, and a speculative read that failed is retried synchronously under the lock. Our fallback is therefore compatible with it; both keep master's read under the hold as the last resort. If it lands first and the series is rebased on it: its retry needs one `BLOCK_FILE_WORK_UNDER_CS_MAIN` marker, the reorganisation read-ahead here reads only the blocks a reorganisation disconnects, which it has nothing for, so the two do not overlap, the two pools fold into one, and its `catch (std::exception&)` in the read task is against the rule of 4.5. It edits `ConnectTip()`, `ActivateBestChainStep()` and `ActivateBestChain()` of master; in this series the counterparts are `StageConnectTip()`, `PlanConnectStep()` with `FinishConnectStep()`, and `ActivateBestChain()`, so the conflicts would be there.

**What is not known.** No measurement on a slow disk exists for any of this; the harness's block files sit in the page cache, so its figures (5.2) show that the moves cost nothing there and say little about what they gain where a read or a sync takes milliseconds. The paths no test exercises are listed in 5.5. The maintainer's full functional suite and `feature_dbcrash.py` last passed on the trees of 24 and 25 November (9.2), which have the file work of this section.

## 5. What is open

### 5.1 The design review's answer

No different design in view reaches equal or better performance with every constraint intact. The simpler alternatives drop the low-end write-out logic or a constraint; the one idea both simpler and inside the constraints (inputs ordered by shard) would replace the centre of the series. The series' open points are at its edges: memory accounting, threads in question, figures never measured. A second comparison round with the outside designs and a second independent design gave the same answer (7.7). The passes behind this are in 7.6.

The verdicts that needed no measurement are settled (the pool exception defect and the BIP30 gap with the serial stretch below BIP34 were fixed, 4.5 and 4.6; everything not named here or in 5.2 is "keep"): one byte budget for the blocks in memory was dropped (8.1; the block cache default follows the coins cache, 4.9 and 7.7; receive tasks stay uncounted, at most five, 4.7; the bins are gone, 8.1); the undo backlog had its byte bound already, 64 MiB (row 3 of 4.1); the block-index states were dropped on 21 November (8.4); the prune ceiling was removed (4.10).

**The outside audit of 2026-11-23** (two gists by willcl-ark, 2afb59da8dbee549d6093574658b0948 the finding list and f0b4dee3e8d61bc603953fdb35447879 the retrospective; written against 88076a427d2, 32 commits on e211dd0186, an older state). Read at tip 31a9b7b0d9 and put to the maintainer; the fixes that followed are below.

- The finding list (about 60 ids), each checked by reading at the tip: no critical item is live as stated. Fixed since that head: CS-1, CS-2, CS-7, IO-2, IO-3, VAL-1, NET-1, NET-2, NET-5, the truncating open. Refuted by the callers: CS-4 (every run is settled before a confirm or unwind), CS-9, the consensus part of CS-3 (a window failure is never a verdict). Live and low, each fixed or closed below: IO-5, VAL-3, KRN-2 part 3, IO-8b, NET-4, CS-10 and the CS-3 remainder. Performance only: IO-4, IO-10, VAL-4, NET-3. Master's own code: IO-1, IO-7, KRN-1. Not settled by reading: CS-5, CS-6, CS-8 part 1. Against a ruling: CS-11 and the other bad_alloc paths.
- Redesign 1 (validation read-only against the store plus the outputs of the window's blocks, an ordered commit by the connector) is the design of the series since 24 November, on the maintainer's judgement that it is the simpler one to review at a speed inside the spread (10:36.16 against 10:32.54, 7.1). It is described in 4.2, 4.5 and 4.6; what was measured and read while it was built is in 7.13.
- The live findings fixed (23 November, on the maintainer's word "fix the last few items that were reported by this last reviewer"), made in their commits, tip 57b3595014: IO-5, a write started while a block is published (its head one above the tip, O8) names the tip in its ChainStateFlushed, not the block whose BlockConnected leaves after it (`FlushedSignalBlock()`, both the run and the synchronous write); VAL-3, `FlushStateToDisk()` fails when a write-out run's failure lands during the call; KRN-2 part 3, `btck_chainstate_manager_create` settles the undo writes as the other two entry points do; IO-8b, `dumptxoutset` refuses a tip without its undo data instead of reaching `GetFirstBlock()`'s assert; NET-4, a getdata waiting for its block to be stored is no more work for the message handler, which the receive task wakes when the store ends (a block stored by submitblock waits for the handler's next wake, within 100 ms); CS-10, a store cell is constructed and destroyed whole. No new test: the index ordering needs a write-out run started inside a publication with a synced index, the others a failed write or a timing the tests cannot make. Gates: `build-clang` and the fuzz build without a warning, unit suite 403 passed, 29 functional entries passed (`feature_index_prune.py` on a rerun after a port another process held for a moment); the kernel library not built. The rest was decided in the next item.
- The items left after those fixes, decided without the maintainer (23 November: "Usually I just follow your recommendations, so maybe just interrogate yourself a bit more?", then "also fix the performance only suggestions"), fixed in their commits: IO-4, in initial block download a receive task no longer runs the pruning check of `FlushStateToDisk(NONE)` under cs_main after accepting a block while a step that uses the window runs (`ChainstateManager::m_window_steps_running`; every block that step publishes checks at its periodic write, which waits for no run under cs_main); with no such step, the connector waiting for a block, the check is made at acceptance as before, so that blocks received ahead of a stalled tip keep the block files at the target (a first form skipped it throughout initial block download, which a check of the fixes found to let the files grow by up to the download window behind a stalled peer); the commit message says so; IO-10 (its page-cache half went with the page-cache release on 26 November, 5.9): the block cache measures a block at or below a snapshot's base against the historical tip (`BlockCache::Tips`, `ChainstateManager::BlockCacheTips()`), so an AssumeUTXO background sync uses it, and over budget the historical chainstate's highest block goes before any of the active chain's (`FirstToGo()`; with highest-first alone the active chain, the one synced to the tip, lost its blocks to the background chain); NET-3, a pending pong and the stalling verdict wait for the active chainstate's activation (DoneActive), not the historical one's: they wait for at most the historical pass in progress, since a block handed over during it is attempted by the next pass, and in initial block download the historical pass does not give way (giving way to every block there would starve it; master's message handler waits for the same pass); CS-5 (the reviewer's mode A, settled by a fresh reading of the store), a confirmed change (made with no block open) keeps a dirty entry that no run in flight has collected under its filed height (`Height::Oldest`; a collected change is the run's to write, which `run_settles_only_unchanged_entries` caught in a first form): a dirty spent entry without OWED owes an erase too, and a disconnect's restore or spend, tagged with the block's or the fork's height, would have filed it higher, so that a run bounded between the two heights, then a crash, brought a spent coin back (reachable only with a coinbase made twice after its first copy was spent, a reorganisation over 100 blocks deep, a partial run in a narrow band and an unclean stop; on mainnet not before 1,983,702); CS-6, `ConfirmBlock()`'s owed-spends loop runs after the slot is freed, and a higher block that made the outpoint again and was spent by a still higher one meanwhile recorded this block's height as the one the erase is owed since: that live spend's item now takes the earlier height; CS-8 part 1, `PeekCoinFor()` looks at the map again under the lock before it reads the count of open blocks (no production caller reads with no block; hardening). Left, with the reason: CS-3's remainder is C2, closed on 22 November (5.6); VAL-4, outside initial block download each block's undo write is settled under the hold by design (O7, so that the mempool's removals follow the earlier blocks' BlockConnected), as master writes undo data synchronously, and only the blocks published after the latch flips pay it; the second sync of a finished undo file is an fsync of a file with no dirty pages (the finish runs off cs_main and leaves the file to the next batch on purpose); the effects thread's 10 ms poll runs only while completions wait for a busy cs_main (a wake on cs_main's release does not exist); a second find per spend does not pay (5.2b), a dirty-list walk saves nothing (nearly every entry of a block's view is dirty), the validation cache's fast path is master's code; CS-12's destructor joins a thread that never takes cs_main; CS-10's parts 2 and 3 (the node's offset is fixed by static asserts, and the free list's two pointers in a freed cell are implicitly created objects in storage from an allocation function); VAL-3's leftover check in `PublishConnectTip()` was removed with its stale comment; CS-7 item 3, IO-1, IO-7 and KRN-1 are master's code (KRN-1, a null manager given to `btck_chainstate_manager_destroy`, is an undocumented precondition, for upstream). The section 3 redesigns (3.1 to 3.4, 3.6) are answered by the redesign reading and the trial above and by the rulings named there. Every fix was then checked against the reviewer's text by a fresh reader: ten addressed as they were, the three above (IO-4, the block cache, NET-3) corrected, and the stale comments named there fixed. Measured once, a pruned sync of chain DX (`-prune=550 -dbcache=450`, 14 cores, three master peers on two, four alternating rounds, `cpu/prune_ab.sh`): 181.8 against 180.7 blocks/s before the round (57b3595014), ranges 180.1 to 187.7 and 163.2 to 184.0 overlapping, 11 prune events and 529 MiB of block files in every run: no change on the harness, and no overshoot of the target. The AssumeUTXO paths (the block cache, the pong) have no harness and are not measured.
- Redesign 2 (flat open-addressed tables, no node allocator): closed by the ruling on bytes per coin (a flat table of 92-byte slots is at 108 bytes only at its fullest and at twice that after a doubling). Redesign 6.1 (window regardless of initial block download): candidate B, closed (C17). Redesign 3 (pools that never take `cs_main`), 4 (`FlushOptions`), 5 (state per chainstate) and 7 (a `ConnectWindowRunner`): structure, no defect behind them at the tip beyond the live items above; the 10 ms poll of the effects thread still exists (`effects.cpp`).

### 5.2 What needs a measurement first

Ran, and so left this table: on the harness (7.7) the effects executor's removal (lost, it stays), the hash pool's removal (done), the memory batch and the pruned sync of 7.6, the profile of the low-end corner; on the maintainer's machine master's connect rate (2:10:38 on 15 November, 9.4x the series' 13:52.88 of that day, 7.1), the pool size (on the harness, 14 November: 15 workers on 16 cores 536.2 blocks/s against 537.4 at 16, 14 workers 516.1, 0.96x, no lever there; on the maintainer's machine, 7.1: at 30 workers the wall of 32 for 2.4% less CPU, 10:32.54; at 24 and 16 workers 1.014x and 1.074x slower, 18 November) and the CPU per block at mainnet's store size (14 November: the late 36.5 ms of worker CPU a block, read 8.55, core 12.4, batch 6.75, cont 6.34, fetch 2.46 between 800,000 and 950,000 in the 14:00.95 run; the reading in 7.3; the glibc and page-cache runs it named next are set aside by ruling, 5.6); the shard lock retry, T3, was cut and taken on 22 November after the maintainer's 10:33.33 ("Looks reasonably fast"; 7.1, 7.3); the one final write path, M1, was closed on 17 November, the parallel flush stays (8.4). The rest did not run.

| Component | Question | Measurement | Lines if taken |
|---|---|---|---|
| Workers below the connector's priority | does the publisher get its core sooner | harness, 14 Nov, a throwaway switch on a `ThreadPool::Start()` worker function: batch policy 537.9, nice 10 540.2, both 516.7 against 540.0 blocks/s, all inside the spread. Not on the series; whether the maintainer's machine (the publisher 23 to 30% late for a core there) differs is not measured | small |
| Eviction in one piece | spread over blocks | the 450 MiB pruned sync of 7.6 | small, in the store |
| Stalled front | second request against earlier disconnect | the crawl profile on the network harness | small; code first |
| Thread maximum 1024 | memory per thread | above 16 threads; up to 16 resident memory follows the thread count, about 400 to 460 MiB above master at 16 (7.7) | 0 |
| Low-end corner | the two halves the profile found (7.7): the pipeline stops for every run, and a worker's block costs 3.2x | the low-end phase: runs overlapping publication (the spend hints, P7, were built and dropped, 8.1) | unknown |
| Full reindex | 16 workers are no faster than 8; neither the loader nor the connect pool is saturated | which thread or wait bounds it; never measured | unknown |

### 5.2a Tip connect performance

The maintainer's question: why is the tip no faster than master when initial block download is, given the allocator, depot, table and huge-page work? What measurement showed (19 to 20 November, clean build `build-bench`; the bench figures first taken in `build-clang`, which has `-DDEBUG_LOCKORDER`, are void wherever several threads take locks): that work recovered costs the store itself brought (cross-thread frees, contention, misses in a large table), which matter with many workers and not for one block on one thread against a warm cache; a real tip block is rebuilt from the mempool and hits the script cache (WarmScripts is only about 1.5x NoScripts), so the UTXO side is a large share of a realistic tip connect; the store's tip round trip is 1.14x master's overlay (11.1 against 9.5 to 9.9 ms on block 413567), `Confirm()` about 1.8x master's flush, the hand-offs (connector, effects) cost nothing measurable.

Levers, under C17 (no tip or solo-block introspection; a single-block gain applies in initial block download too, section 2):
- An unwind driven by the block's undo data and its own outputs, guarded per entry by the block's identity, instead of a per-change record built on the apply path. The probe below found it worth well under 2% of worker CPU: not worth its risk. Since the read-only window there is no per-change record: the take-back of a commit is driven by the block's undo data (4.5).
- A prefetch sized by its misses, decided by the data and the same for every block. Its plain form was tried and dropped (below).
- Ruled out by C17: a solo-block confirm, and connecting inline when the connector is idle.

The node at the tip, as measured: a client from a snapshot one block below, a test peer delivering each block as a compact block after its transactions went to the mempool (all, 80%, 50%) or as a whole block with an empty mempool; latency from the send to `getbestblockhash`; scripts checked, 8 cores, `-dbcache=1024`; chain DX heavy blocks (1.13 MB, 3,000 transactions) and mainnet blocks 240,000 to 240,029 as whole blocks.

- Before: heavy full mempool and 80% equal to the baseline, 50% 3% slower, whole block 5.5% slower. Causes, by probes: `ApplyChanges` 19.5 ms, nearly all of it the apply reading the database again for every spend of an input the tip view had read without keeping it; `ActiveTipChange` waiting 5.9 ms under `cs_main` for the transaction download lock; 2.7 ms after `UpdateTip` waiting for an undo write queued only after the apply.
- Built: a block connected alone queues its undo write before its apply, and takes the coins its apply misses from its undo data (looked up only on a miss). After: heavy full mempool equal (about 52 ms), 80% and 50% not slower, heavy whole block equal (about 1.5 ms lower), mainnet whole blocks equal, empty compact blocks about 0.3 ms above the baseline (ranges overlap), initial block download unchanged. A first form of the second change, keeping the tip's database reads in the store, was faster (whole block 115 against 119 ms) but broke master's `connect_tip_does_not_cache_inputs_on_failed_connect` and was dropped.
- The previous block freed off the path (c600e4b1f9): the previous block, its compact form and its map are taken out under the lock as before and freed on a worker of the connect pool. Against the baseline: heavy full mempool about 17% faster, 80% about 10%, 50% about 3.5%, all beyond the spread. Not called in initial block download.
- Steady state, heavy block with a full mempool: receipt to `UpdateTip` about 50 ms on both; the connect about 27 ms (core 8.4, mempool cleanup about 11, no script queue work at all: Verify equals Connect); before the connect, `NewPoWValidBlock` took 8.4 ms under `cs_main`, 7.2 of it replacing the most recent block map (the destruction freed the previous block's transactions), which the change above moved to the pool. Empty blocks are equal on both: the series has no fixed extra cost per block from receipt to tip.
- Where the rest is (whole heavy block): the core 4 ms above master (the parallel fetch), apply and confirm 11.9 against master's 7.9 ms flush; the undo write and the flush are where the series saves. Not reached: a whole block faster than the baseline beyond the spread. Considered and not taken: validating a large block's transactions in chunks (8.1), the script cache checks on the check queue (at most 1 to 2 ms of the 8.4 ms core), the mempool cleanup (about 11 ms, set aside by the maintainer).
- The store's apply, by a sampled probe (20 November, clean build, `-reindex-chainstate` of chain DX at 8 cores, 19.8 million changes, each component reading about 20 ns high from the timer): 446 ns a change: prefetch phase 95, record insertion 46, the per-change loop about 300 (map find 23, insert 73, list detach 24 and attach 25, state transition 21, undo push 38; about 240 ns of the sampled 462 unattributed, probably the accounting atomics, `Resized()`, `See()` and the coin move). Records, list maintenance and transitions together are 25 to 40 ns, under 10%: the visible costs are the memory latency of the first touch (the prefetch phase, 21%) and the insert (about 11%).
- The read-only window at the tip (25 November, the tip replay of `tip/`, heavy blocks of chain DX from 3,500, 30 a run, three alternating rounds, 8 cores; binaries from `build-bench`: master with the BIP30 switch 326374edc0, the series before the read-only window cecbce8958, the series cdc90d5b22). Medians in ms, master / before / then: full mempool 51.9, 52.1, 51.4 / 43.9, 43.5, 43.5 / 45.8, 45.5, 46.2 (2.0 to 2.7 ms slower than before, beyond its spread of 0.4); 80% 97.8, 98.6, 91.7 / 84.4, 86.6, 85.0 / 85.3, 84.1, 85.4; 50% 164.5, 160.5, 165.5 / 156.1, 154.2, 154.7 / 156.9, 171.6, 157.3; whole block 117.6, 117.6, 117.8 / 117.8, 119.7, 119.0 / 119.7, 115.6, 117.3. The bench lines placed the full-mempool gap in the connect: Confirm 0.10 against 0.44 ms, the core 8.2 to 9.0 against 9.3 to 9.9 ms. Cause, by reading: before the read-only window every block's apply ended by clearing the view's map, making its pool again and reserving it for as many entries; since, only a window block's commit did (`ChangesCommitted()`), and a block connected alone kept its map until the reset after publication, a plain clear in the timed Confirm with no new pool, so the next block's map grew step by step on nodes scattered in recycled memory. Fixed (a6580ee751, `CommitAlone()` ends with `ChangesCommitted()`). After, before / then / fixed: full mempool 44.9, 43.5, 44.0 / 44.7, 46.9, 44.8 / 44.3, 45.1, 43.2; 80%, 50% and the whole block equal between the three within their spreads; the fixed binary's bench lines Confirm 0.09 ms, core 8.16 ms. Mainnet whole blocks not run this time.
- Will's reduced stack against the series and master (26 November, the maintainer: "Do you think this branch will have similarly faster performance at tip?", then "Can you measure it?"): `utxo-clean/5-async-p2p-handoff` of willcl-ark (2f009a23a4, 16 commits on master edcac28380; it frees the previous block on the new block's path as master does, and has the undo-sourced apply), built with the baseline's `-test=bip30_off` probe added (branch `will-bench`, its compiler warnings ignored on the maintainer's word), against the baseline 326374edc0 and the series f77760e49a; the tip replay of chain DX heavy blocks from 3,500, 30 a run, three alternating rounds, 8 cores, all three from the same master snapshot, load average 0.5 to 2.4. Medians in ms, master / series / Will's, by round: full mempool 51.5, 51.0, 50.0 / 42.7, 42.1, 41.6 / 53.2, 54.2, 54.6 (series 17.1% faster than master, Will's 6.3% slower); 80% 93.3, 91.9, 92.0 / 80.7, 82.3, 83.6 / 92.3, 95.8, 94.3 (11.0% faster, 1.9% slower); 50% 156.7, 156.7, 159.2 / 154.3, 152.4, 151.6 / 164.0, 161.2, 160.9 (3.0% faster, 2.9% slower); whole block 115.8, 117.8, 117.8 / 116.7, 115.8, 115.1 / 127.6, 120.7, 119.0 (1.1% faster, 4.5% slower). The series' lead follows the freed previous block, as measured when it was built; Will's branch is at or a little behind master in every case.
- Will's utxo-v4-series against the same figures (27 November, the maintainer: "test its at tip performance again", master and the series not re-run, yesterday's numbers above used): `utxo-v4-series` of willcl-ark (f2fbb6de86, 20 commits on master 0958604118, two upstream commits past the series' base), built from an exported tree with the `-test=bip30_off` probe applied at his two BIP30 decision sites (serial `ConnectBlock()` and the parallel IBD window; script `will4_probe.py` in the job tmp, no branch), build-bench settings, start state a copy of the same master snapshot; the same replay, three rounds, 8 cores, load average 0.5 to 1.5 (6.2 before the first run, the build's tail; that run matches the other two). Medians in ms by round, and the mean of the three against master / the series of 26 November: full mempool 47.87, 47.96, 47.48 (6.0% faster than master, 13.3% slower than the series); 80% 85.95, 85.24, 85.73 (7.3% faster, 4.2% slower); 50% 156.92, 155.05, 154.59 (1.3% faster, 1.8% slower); whole block 115.14, 115.59, 114.88 (1.6% faster, 0.6% faster, inside the series' spread). Against his reduced stack of the day before: 11.6%, 9.0%, 4.0% and 5.9% faster. Out of initial block download his parallel window is off, so this measures his serial path on the sharded tip cache with the handoff cache and the off-`cs_main` block store. Across two days on the shared host, not alternated with the other binaries.
- Will's utxo-v4-series at the floor (27 November, the maintainer: "check the blocks/s on the chain DX harness with minimal dbcache"): the same binary (`bin-will4`) against the series tip f77760e49a (`bin-tipf777`), chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers on 16 cores, `-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5`, `-test=bip30_off -par=16`, three alternating rounds (`cpu/will4_floor.sh`, log `cpu/will4_floor.log`), load average 1.5 to 2.8. Heavy section (2,781 to 4,281), series / Will's: 58.3, 58.5, 57.4 / 60.6, 60.5, 59.3 blocks/s (1.035x, under the 5% a harness gain needs to predict anything); whole run 27.8, 27.6, 28.2 / 31.2, 31.2, 31.7 s (Will's 1.13x slower); CPU 116.4, 116.0, 118.3 / 45.3, 43.0, 43.4 s (0.38x); peak RSS 391, 411, 398 / 313, 314, 314 MiB. On a reindex his window builds no undo data where the block's undo is on disk (`build_undo`), which a sync cannot skip, so part of his CPU saving is the reindex's; his empty-database lookup skip lasts only to the first write at this cache. Master not run: its connect is one thread, 15 to 19 blocks/s at 30/5 (18 November, above), and does not fit the harness's minute.
- Two ports from Will's branch tried on the series (27 November, the maintainer: "I would try out the keyed sigphash tag and the entries for spent coins"; the POSIX reader left out for good: "no more platform specific tweaks"). Branch `utxo-partial-order-keyed-spent-trial` on f77760e49a: 0de7b09208 (the three per-block outpoint tables take a keyed SipHash-1-3 tag of the txid, one random key for the process, `TxidTag()`; ConnectBlock() finds an input's slot once) and 1d7ec87b3c (a spent entry the store keeps, dirty and neither fresh nor owed, lies in a 44-byte spent cell: link, key and word, a cell's first bytes; the word moves before the coin and gives a bit of the filed height, now 2^24, to `spent_cell`; a second node allocator numbers spent cells with the top bit, each allocator 2^12 regions; shrink at a confirmed spend, grow where a coin is written over, between Detach() and Attach(), never under a run). Branch `utxo-partial-order-spent-trial` is the spent cells alone on the tip (5cff15c176). Harness, chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, BIP30 off, two alternating rounds each (`cpu/keyed_ab.sh`, `cpu/spent_ab.sh`). Keyed tag at `-dbcache=1024`: 607.8, 607.3 against the series' 618.3, 642.7 blocks/s (about 3.5% slower in both rounds, 1 to 2 s more CPU; under the 5% a harness difference needs). Spent cells at the floor (`-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5`), against the keyed binary: 385.7, 387.7 against 55.7, 56.9 blocks/s, 5.9 against 28.9 s wall, CPU 45 against 124 s, peak RSS 334 against 411 MiB. A cliff of this chain: the store falls from about 100 to about 84 bytes an entry (21.5 MiB for 268k entries at 3,500 against 18.7 MiB for 195k), the whole set stays under the floor's write-out threshold, and one write-out run lands against 93 (`-debug=coindb`). On mainnet, where the set is far above any floor cache, the gain is fewer write-outs in proportion to the spent entries, not none; at 450 and above chain DX writes nothing out, so the harness cannot show it there. Checked on `build-clang` (failed Assume aborts): coinsstore_tests (39 cases, SanityCheck() after every kind of change) and validation_chainstate_tests, coins_tests, validation_flush_tests (42 cases) pass. Mainnet runs are the maintainer's; the two mboxes (`utxo-partial-order.mbox` with both, `utxo-partial-order-without-keyed.mbox` with the spent cells alone) were sent.
- Three more from the comparison with Will's branch (27 November; three read-only comparisons, store, validation, the rest; the maintainer chose: the commit's work "lets try it", the eviction mark "a decent change in any case", the wake "interesting"; the walk prefetch "meh"; the rest not taken). Branch `utxo-partial-order-commit-trial` on 27357eb7f9: 227e6a121b (a coin a lookup brings in from the database is marked referenced, as a found entry is, so eviction's sweep does not take a coin a window block read before its commit spends it); 091263ccb3 (`OnWindowBlockIndexed()` wakes the window's waiters only for a failed read, after its mutex: the waits are for complete, not validating, committed, and Index leads Running to Indexed); dfbcb61e1e (each commit item's key hash worked out on the block's worker into `StagedConnect::hashes` and handed to `ApplyConfirmed()`, which then neither hashes nor filters; `VisitShards()` sorts over the range of shards present, a group's four, in the thread's buffers). 21 code lines net (48 added, 27 removed). Checked on `build-clang`: 402 unit tests, and feature_block, both feature_connect_window variants, feature_connect_window_double_spend (a take-back), the three feature_coins_lag, rpc_invalidateblock, mempool_reorg and the three p2p_ibd_stalling variants pass. Not measured on the harness, which showed a 12 to 20% shorter commit as no rate change (the hold probe); the 41-patch mbox was sent for a mainnet run at 2000, against 24:13.54. Then on the maintainer's ask, chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, BIP30 off, alternating (`cpu/ctrial_ab.sh`, load average 7 to 9 from two builds just before and the runs, nothing foreign): at `-dbcache=1024` 610.0, 625.2, 637.6 against the trial's 641.3, 634.6, 630.3 blocks/s (+1.8%); at the floor 409.3, 399.5 against 393.2, 392.8 (-2.8%); CPU and RSS level. No change the harness resolves, as the hold probe predicted; the eviction mark has little to act on there, since chain DX barely writes out at the floor since the spent cells. Dropped on the maintainer's word (27 November, "Then revert the changes, doesn't seem worthwhile"): all three, the branch deleted, the series left at 27357eb7f9; the commits above are the record if one is wanted again.
- The compactness pass (27 November; the maintainer: "find more compact representations of existing logic", "the same speed, or better. I also don't want regressions just to save a few lines", then "prune these aggressively", with every removal complete, comments and tests included). Three read-only comparisons with Will's branch, briefed on lines saved for the same behaviour, found the repetition mostly in our own code. Folded into the commits that introduced it (store 17 and 38; storage 3, 10, 11, 14, 19, 24, 25, 28; validation 24, 27, 29, 35; the stored coin 37): one spend helper (`SpendEntryAt()` with an optional `moveto`, `SpendEntry()` gone), one erase (the node overload and `Map::erase(key)` gone), one `Refile()` for the six writes of a coin into an entry, `BatchWrite()`'s spends through `SpendEntryAt()`, `VisitShards()` hashing and prefetching for its callers, `StoredCoin`'s assignment from a `Coin` through `AssignOutput()`, one `PutCoin()` in txdb; master's `FlatFileSeq::Allocate()` for the chunk allocation, one `QueueFileTrim()`, one `SyncFile()` for the sync, latch and report, the block cache's drop as a `Take()`, the forwarding `ProcessBlock()` folded in, a failed block write handled once in `FinishAcceptBlock()`, the `CommitBatch()` forwarder and `ReadBlock()`'s unused out-parameter gone, effect completions optional; in validation the coinbase handled first in `ConnectBlock()` without its unreachable branch, the spent table probed and filled in one walk, the positional mode as one `BlockValidationContext::window_tx` (`BlockApplyView::SetTransaction()`), `WindowOutputs`' shard walks through one `InShards()`, the window flags replaced by their `sync` pointers, the group claim an `atomic_flag`, one `abandon` for the window's failure exits. 232 code lines fewer in the 14 files touched (16,572 to 16,340). Checked on `build-clang`: 402 unit tests, and feature_block, both feature_connect_window variants, feature_connect_window_double_spend, the three feature_coins_lag, feature_pruning, feature_reindex, p2p_compactblocks, the three p2p_ibd_stalling variants, rpc_invalidateblock and mempool_reorg; the folded tip's tree is that tested tree byte for byte. Not measured: the candidates were argued to do no more work (the same hashes, lookups, locks and allocations or fewer; the spent table walks each input's run once instead of twice).
- Window width above the default (20 November): the maintainer raised `MAX_CONNECT_WINDOW` and ran a mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` with `-connectwindow` above 64 at 32 workers and `-dbcache=30000`: no change in runtime. The window width is not a limit there. Not to be raised again.

Tried and dropped:
- The ActiveTipChange-before-signals change (21 November, on the maintainer's "Yes, drop it."): it did not reach the case it was written for. A block connected at the tip without a reorganisation has its `BlockConnected` handed to the validation queue in `PublishConnectTip()` before the reordered code, so the 5.9 ms wait could still happen; it covered reorganisations only, was never measured alone, and changed the order of signals against master. Whether the wait is still there after the later tip commits was not measured; a reordering in `PublishConnectTip()` would be a new change and needs that measurement first.
- The apply by shard on the pool's workers (one helper per 1,024 changes), tried 20 November on the maintainer's go: it first looked slower, a debug-build artefact (4.4 ms alone against 10 ms with helpers, from the lock-order bookkeeping; clean, 2.17 ms alone against 1.64 with 3 or 7 helpers), then about 2.5% faster on heavy whole blocks and equal on mainnet's 2013 blocks, which have too few changes to start a helper. On 21 November, on the maintainer's "You have all the benchmarking you need yourself" and then "Ok, drop it again.", it went: `-reindex-chainstate` at 16 workers was 2 to 4% faster without helpers in all three sets (521.0 against 499.2 blocks/s in the clearest), and a variant asking for at most the pool's idle workers equals the tip, so the cost is not tasks handed to busy workers but idle workers taking another block's shards. Under 5% on the harness predicts nothing for mainnet; the direction was the same in every set. Not to be proposed again in this form: a gain for a lone block that costs in the window.
- Lever 2 in its plain form, the tip block's serial `Prefetch()` before the parallel fetch: the bench round trip fell from 26 to 29 ms to 22 ms, but in the node the heavy full mempool got slower (54 against 52 ms, since with warm inputs the serial pass replaces a fetch that overlapped it). Dropped, and so was a variant in which the core claims an input no worker has taken: the convoy it addressed exists only in the debug build.

I/O work on the harness (22 to 23 November; chain DX, three serving nodes on cores 14 and 15, `-dbcache=1024`, BIP30 off, alternating rounds, foreign load 0.3 to 1.1 cores). No step was distinguished from its parent:
- Block reads on I/O threads: `-reindex-chainstate` at 16 workers 0.992x (579.7 against 575.1 blocks/s), at 12 workers 1.024x (496.1 against 507.8, higher in each of five rounds, under the 3% the harness distinguishes); peak RSS 14 to 26 MiB higher. With every core taken by a worker the I/O threads add nothing and cost nothing, with four cores spare about 2%; sixteen workers beat twelve either way (1.13x to 1.17x). The harness cannot show a disk wait: chain DX's 2.5 GB sit in the page cache, so a read is a copy.
- The block I/O pool with the received block's write: network sync equal (parent, block-read form, tip in blocks/s: 14 cores 247.0, 246.4, 244.5; 8 cores 240.2, 241.3, 237.7; 4 cores 133.4, 133.8, 131.3), the tip about 1% lower each time. The four I/O threads used 3.9 s of 60.7 s client CPU for the whole write of 2397 MiB at 8 cores, which bounds a write of the wire bytes below the harness's resolution: not built. Pruned sync, parent against tip: 179.0 and 185.0 (8 cores), 120.2 and 118.2 (4), 224.2 and 217.5 (14). At 14 cores the tip showed a slow mode (209.1 blocks/s, four slow runs of five) carrying about 10 s more client CPU, 80 MiB more memory and more blocks read back from disk: oversubscription, four I/O threads beside one worker per core where the network thread alone runs 78% of a core (with 12 workers on the same 14 cores the parent made 235.2 and the tip 238.6, both as fast as 14 workers). Open with the maintainer: the default worker count (one less than a worker per core would leave room for the I/O threads). Also found: tasks of blocks read on the pool reach the connect pool in read-completion order, not block order, and the stepping unit tests put their blocks in the block cache (`CacheStepBlocks()`), so a successful read on an I/O thread is covered by functional tests only.
- File work off `cs_main`: pruned sync at 8 cores 185.5 against 189.3 blocks/s, with 12 workers on 14 cores 228.7 against 236.8, plain sync at 8 cores 232.9 against 231.0 (parent against commit): not distinguished, the largest difference 3.5%. The commit stands on the placement rule, not on a gain; the harness writes to the page cache of a fast disk, where an allocation or an unlink under `cs_main` is short.
- Block I/O threads 2 against 4 (pruned sync, 14 workers on 14 cores): 208.3 against 228.6 blocks/s; four was ahead in each round and the ranges overlap. Nothing supports lowering the constant; it stays 4.

Passes and review rounds on this code:
- ThreadSanitizer at 3c224b4d8b (21 November, upstream's CI options, Core's suppressions): no report, 397 unit tests and 295 functional tests passed, the two known failures (`tool_wallet.py`, `feature_init.py`); an earlier pass at c600e4b1f9 (20 November) was clean too. No further pass until the maintainer asks for one.
- Review round 13 (21 November, six fresh readers, `/work/parallel-validation/full-r13-verified.md`): no consensus or crash-safety defect. Clear fixes were made (comments and messages, an `Assert` of no conflicts after a lone block's apply); `Sync()` virtual was tried and taken back, as master's prefetch view deletes `Sync()`, which cannot override. Decided without a code change (the record's section F): the BIP30 takeover path is unreachable because enforcement is monotone in the window; a window worker waiting for started helpers of its pool is an accepted reading of C8; the reorg step's single flush, the mode selections and the late mark outside initial block download are accepted. Four test gaps were closed (the undo-sourced miss, a replay required in `feature_coins_lag.py`, the thread default's bounds, a conflict between two real window blocks in `feature_connect_window.py`) and the rest named as untested with reasons. The rest of job 2 (8.4) was checked by reading: nothing found.
- Review round 14 (22 November, nine fresh readers, read only, every finding re-read by me): no consensus difference and no high finding, nine findings: six fixed on 22 November ("Fix all the findings. Only hold things for me where you really think my input could be valuable."), two left as code with their comments, one closed by ruling. (1) The most severe, crash safety: `CollectRecords()` scanned the open blocks' records one slot mutex at a time, and a takeover in `SpendLocked()` between two scans could hide a confirmed dirty coin from the run, which then settled the database above the coin, and a crash before the next run lost it; it now takes a dead spend item as a candidate too and asks its question with the shard held, which covers `Flush()`. No deterministic test of that interleaving exists: untested, named so. (3) a bit-field mixing `uint32_t : 31` with `bool : 1` under a size `static_assert` (MSVC does not pack the two). (4) The emplace booked the Coin's usage, not the StoredCoin's. (5) The undo-write effect now runs under `util::RunWorkerTask(UNDO_WRITE_TASK)` and raises the fatal error; a write failure used to terminate past the effect's own failure path. (6) `m_downloading_since` restarts when the new request is the first on the wire. (9) `*new_block` is reset when the entry failed during the write. Left as code, documented: (7) the window byte account is one per node, so two chainstates in window steps at once would overwrite each other's figure (whether they can was not established); (8) a create over a dirty confirmed coin records `dirty` but not `fresh`, so an unwind costs one unneeded write and erase, only at the two BIP30 duplicates. Closed by ruling: (2) a pending pong waits on the global hand-off mark, not on the peer's own blocks as its comment said (the comment was corrected), so a peer that serves no blocks stalls behind a connector pass of other peers' blocks. The maintainer: "finding 2 is well known, and is pretty bad currently on master", and a per-peer mark "seems like a hack just to make the functional tests happy". The held pong stays, which is master's behaviour; the real fix is a pong answered at once with an explicit barrier in the test framework, a change of its own outside the series. The threaded store test now runs every third round highest block first, each thread joined before the next, so a block connected alone no longer depends on timing. Left: three unit tests that prove a negative by sleeping (a slow machine cannot make them fail), `blocks_on_message_handler` in unit tests and fuzz (the hand-off runs only in functional tests); `SanityCheck()`'s record scan went with the records (4.5).
- The bot review of the stacked pull requests (23 November). A colleague put the series on a stack of ten pull requests in his fork (willcl-ark/bitcoin #38 to #48, the same 34 commits regrouped, the tree that of the series), and his review bot commented on eight of them; #45 (the connect window) and #47 (the top) got no review, #44 (the store) none it could verify. The comments, the reports and the verdict on each are in `/work/parallel-validation/stack49-reviews` (`verification.md`): 51 findings and one comment by the colleague, each checked against the series by reading, the two major ones and the minor ones by me again. Two were real defects of the series. (1) A block file could be truncated: `FlatFileSeq::Open()` created a missing file with a truncating open, as master does, where master serialised every write and flush under cs_main; in the series a block's file is claimed under cs_main and created by the write off the lock, while a write-out run's batch syncs the same file on the executor, so a sync whose first open failed could, preempted, truncate the file after the write and leave `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` on lost bytes. The open now creates exclusively and opens again if the file appeared meanwhile (master's own obfuscation key file is opened the same way). No deterministic test: the window is between two system calls. (2) The download window, measured from the active tip where that is lower, stalled a fork whose blocks above the tip carry less work: the tip never moves, nothing is in flight, no stall is detected, and a fork that overtakes beyond 1,024 blocks is never fetched. The tip is now the base only where the last common block descends from it; a fork is measured from its own last common block as on master. Untested: regtest's fixed difficulty cannot make such a fork. Fixed as well: a position claim under cs_main waited for the allocation's mutex held over `fallocate` (a second mutex now serialises the allocations, the claim's is never held over a system call; the I/O rule of 4.14); three casts narrowed 64-bit file offsets to 32 bits; a write that failed before snapshot validation let a valid snapshot be judged against an older coins database and discarded (it is now not judged); the compact filter requests disconnected a peer asking for a block stored and not yet connected (they wait for the pending blocks, outside initial block download only); the connect pool could be destroyed on one of its own workers through a block's deleter (it is stopped in `~ChainstateManager`); a reorganisation's reads were dropped when the target only extended (they are kept); a missing parent check in `ConnectBlock()` (an assert). Documentation: the stop-and-restart contract after `flush_error` in the kernel's public header, O10 (numbered when a block becomes linked), the hashing from the bytes read (why it agrees: every accepted encoding round-trips), `ConnectBlock()`'s contract, `RunWorkerTask()`, the reorganisation read-ahead in the byte ledger, a comment block moved back above `UnserializeTransaction()`. Tests added: the BIP30 limit at 1,983,702, the mempool's wait rule, a tip read on another thread while cs_main is held, a pinned file trimmed after its write, a parent invalidated between a block's accept and its finish and a second copy while the first is stored, the witness flag in the transaction fuzz target. Known limit left: a pruned node on a nearly full disk may stop at the coins' disk space check where master went on, since the series unlinks pruned files after it (on a block I/O thread); it recovers on restart, and a credit for the files being pruned would need their sizes after they are zeroed and fails where `-blocksdir` is another filesystem. Skipped: commit splits, the per-peer rule's accepted limit (4.7), findings already fixed in the series, tests that would need production hooks. Gates: one build of `build-clang` (no warning) and the fuzz binary, unit suite 402 passed (script_assets skipped), 26 functional entries passed (the series' own, the pruning, filter, invalidation, reorganisation, compact block, assumeutxo and stalling tests); `feature_pruning.py`, dbcrash and TSan not run. The bot's second round (#44 again, #45, #47; 27 findings, `verification.md`) found no consensus defect. Fixed: `BeginWriteOut()` refuses after a failed write-out as its header said; two tests shifted a 32-bit `size_t` by 40 bits (32-bit CI with warnings as errors); an invalid snapshot found while another thread has a window batch of it staged leaves its views and directory for the restart instead of resetting them under the batch's workers; asserts for the restored coins and for the undo shortcut's precondition (a block connected alone); one copy of a coin fewer under the shard mutex; the documentation of the stats cursor's precondition, a write-out's memory beyond one chunk (the open blocks' records), the effects hook (never blocks on the requester's lock), the GUI's thread setting and the window's memory estimate (long scripts of spent coins). Tests added: the flush with every helper busy, a failed undo file finish remembered. The pruned-node disk check order is documented at the check (accepted, recovers on restart). The parse-path cleanup (`streams.h` out of `primitives/transaction.h`) measured on `bench_bitcoin`'s DeserializeBlockTest, build-bench, eight rotating rounds: the header-light reader 1.0004x of the code before, the segment hashing moved out of line through `HashWriter` 1.0088x (slowest in every round); the hashing stays inline in the header and only the reader changed. Gates at the tip: one build of each tree (no warning), unit suite passed, the 26 functional entries passed. A second run on #45 (nine findings, six of them repeats; `verification.md`) added three: a write-out run's completion compacted the coins database without master's free-space check (the periodic path outside initial block download), so both the completion and the synchronous form now go through `Chainstate::MaybeCompactChainstate()`; the two announcement tests in `tip_snapshot_tests.cpp` depended on timing and now latch an effect (`HeldEffects`), so that the block signal is pending when the transaction is admitted. The kernel callback's re-entrancy ban stays as ruled. Gates at 31a9b7b0d9: `build-clang` and the fuzz build without a warning, unit suite 403 passed (script_assets skipped), the functional subset passed. Coverage: each of the ten pull requests has a completed bot run (#44 and #45 two); every report says its historical record was incomplete, #43 and #46 ended with their experiments incomplete (the provider declined the verifier), and the bot read the heads before the fixes, so the fixes themselves are checked by the series' own reading and tests only.
- The outside review of 25 November (ralph, runs 3065 to 3074 on willcl-ark's split of the series; the findings and the plan in `/work/parallel-validation/ralph-reviews-2026-11-25.txt` and `ralph-findings-plan.md`), fixed in the commits that introduced the code (tree ea2f151ccb): block file space allocated for the new range alone (`posix_fallocate` from the offset, not from 0: glibc's emulation wrote over bytes other writers had); no commit and no disconnect's batch meets a write-out run in flight (asserted in `ApplyConfirmed()` and `BatchWrite()`, settled before a lone block's commit and a disconnect's flush; the in-flight filing branch of `CreateLocked()` gone); getblocktxn's depth decided in the hold that copied the block's facts; a validated window block's byte count assigned under the window's mutex; `RunCommitWork()` noexcept; pinned blocks charged by their measured size; a coins write short of room removes queued pruned files and checks again; a request's round trip (the peer's least ping) taken off a block that started at its request, so far peers are no longer held at one block (4.7); the coins DB cursor reads its best block from its own snapshot; no block connected once a file sync failed; the O6 check allows a neighbouring best block only after a failed write-out (`TipWriteFailed()`); only the current chainstate's tip moves mark the published tip changed; `ConnectBlock()` derives the script flags (the context's field gone) and `ContextualCheckBlock()` reads height and median time past from the context; the transaction decoder back in master's shape (`UnserElements()` gone); the hand-off machine without its unread Done state; `CreateLocked()` without its unreachable conflict result; `FlushBlockFile()` and `FlushUndoFile()` without their dead modes; `MayHave()` asking for keys only; `Uncache()` tracing without a `Coin`; a log before the abort on node-number exhaustion; the page-cache release after the block's task is submitted (the release is gone since 26 November, 5.9); the IPC test strings, the `connect_block` fuzz harness, and comments and messages (tracing, the memory guide, the restored coins' writer, shard grinding, loading-time publication, the prune and durability commit messages). New tests: scripts given apart from their coins at the inline boundary, a collected heap script outliving a spend, statistics refused under an open run marker, node numbers with dirty erases and eviction across regions, the take-back making again a netted output, blocks met while being stored, blocks written out of order across chunks, a short block file, CompactSize boundaries in hashing, deployments at their activation heights, the script cache under concurrent use, getblocktxn on an unreadable block (`feature_io_errors.py`). Not made: tests that need a pause hook in production code (the maintainer's 15 November ruling); the items left to the maintainer. All targets built without a warning, the unit suite (1059 cases) and 25 functional runs passed.
- Fuzzing by the maintainer (20 November, with sanitizers, the qa-assets corpora, replays and longer runs): `coinsstore_window`, `utxo_total_supply`, `process_messages`, `connect_block` and `utxo_snapshot` ran without a crash. `coinsstore_window` does not reach `StoredCoin`'s heap path (scripts over 35 bytes) or `CopyFrom`/`Clear`; only `stored_coin_round_trip` covers them. A fuzz-only change to reach long scripts (a setter for a threshold and a two-worker pool in the target) was proposed and declined by the maintainer: the gap is known and accepted. `coinsstore_window` is no longer in the tree (5.3).

### 5.2b Performance sweeps by reading, 21 and 22 November 2026

Asked for by the maintainer for the sync and `-reindex-chainstate`, with memory speed as the bound and nothing already dismissed proposed again. Three readers, their leading claims checked in the code at 8b647073b9. The shares named are those of 7.9 and 7.3 and are ceilings for what an item addresses, not expected gains. What each of the nine items became:

1. Cell lines, taken. A store cell is 104 bytes and cells lie back to back, so a cell starts at 0, 8, ... 56 bytes into a cache line and four of eight cells span three lines; `PrefetchNode()` asked for the first line and the one 64 bytes on, so the list links (bytes 96 to 103) were on a line nobody asked for. `PrefetchNode()` now also asks for the line of the cell's last byte; the comment that said the link and the key share a cell's first cache line is corrected and `LINE_BYTES` named.
2. The change record, taken in part (second half on the maintainer's word: "this slim change record, a create that overrode nothing is recorded as its outpoint alone"). `ConfirmBlock()` frees a block's record on the connector under `cs_main`; unless the block is marked or has owed spends it reads nothing of it, but the vector's destruction ran a `Coin` destructor over every 104-byte item the workers wrote (very likely the 4.8% of load-latency weight 7.9 gives `ConfirmBlock()`). A create that went over no confirmed coin is now a 36-byte outpoint in a second per-block list, `WindowBlock::plain_creates`, not an `Undo` item; `ConfirmBlock()` swaps both out and frees them outside the slot's mutex; `UnwindBlock()` takes the record back first and the plain creates after it, each last first (the only changes of one outpoint a block can have in both lists are a plain create and a later spend, which keeps the old order). Harness 1.055x (550.5 against 581.0 blocks/s, faster in every round, the ranges apart; CPU 0.97, peak RSS 383 against 366 MiB), just past the 5% below which the harness predicts nothing. Freeing the record off the connector was not asked for and was not built; the record itself went with the read-only window (4.5).
3. Witness allocations, closed for the series: "The witness allocations will be handled by a different pull request, not part of this series." They are the largest identified share (the witness stacks, 14 to 15% of CPU).
4. `PrecomputedTransactionData`, taken. `ConnectBlock()` built one (about 296 bytes) per transaction for every block, zeroed and destroyed element by element, about 1 MiB a full block; its stable addresses serve the check queue, which a window block does not have. The vector is now sized only with a check queue control, otherwise each transaction has its own local.
5. The store's totals, not built. A create-then-spend coin makes eight lock-prefixed additions, and the 64 stripes are summed up to 18 times a block, 13 of them under `cs_main`. Estimated at 1 to 2% of CPU at most (about 0.2 us of 10.7 per transaction for the additions, 0.1 to 0.2 ms of about 17 ms a block for the sums), below the 2.3% spread of the maintainer's two runs of the day. The maintainer thinks they can go at some point and said "Don't bring them up again" (5.6).
6. The zero-filled read buffer, taken. `ReadRawBlock()` zero-fills its buffer before the read overwrites it; `ReadBlock()` now reads into a `make_unique_for_overwrite` buffer through a private `ReadRawBlockInto()` that `ReadRawBlock()` shares and whose callers keep the zeroed vector; the shared part catches `std::ios_base::failure` where the upstream code caught `std::exception`. `-reindex-chainstate` and blocks that left the block cache only.
7. The undo data of a sync, left open on the maintainer's word ("Ok, lets leave it as you recommended."; "Lets leave that for now too."). It is measured on the worker, measured again, serialised into the hasher and serialised into the file on the effects thread, and its vectors are freed there, on a thread that did not allocate them; serialising once on the worker would fix that. Sync only (a reindex writes no undo). Left on the recommendation given: no effect on `-reindex-chainstate`, the sync gain never measured, a second shape beside the solo block's structure, and a change to the undo write path (5.6).
8. Sizes and sigops from the bytes consumed. A transaction's size is walked twice in a reindex and six times in a sync, the legacy sigops twice; the share is `SerializeTransaction<SizeComputer>`, 2 to 3%. Tried as S2 below: nothing measurable.
9. Serial per-block work, small and never measured: `cs_main` taken eight or nine times per received block, three effects items per connected block of which two carry no effect, the `UpdateTip` line formatted under `cs_main`, the BIP34 ancestor walk three to four times a block, `WindowBytes()` summing every slot per staged block. "doesn't seem material enough to spend more time on" (5.6).

Items 1, 4 and 6 were built on 22 November on the maintainer's word ("do 1, 2, and 3 and do your local measurements of them with the normal configuration", by the numbering of the recommendation) and measured once together: 507.4 against 508.2 blocks/s (six alternating rounds, chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers on 16 cores, `-dbcache=1024`), ratio 1.00, process CPU 0.98, inside the spread. The harness sees nothing from the three together; as with every change under 5% there, the maintainer's run decides.

Read and not proposed, because the record already answers them: the view's second find in `SpendCoin()`, the `HaveCoinInCache()` before each `AddCoin()`, the txid part of the store's hash computed once per transaction (three trials flat on the maintainer's machine, 7.9 and the lookups trial); the second `find` per spend in `ApplyBatch()`; deferring list membership for coins created and spent in the window (the young table under another name). A cheaper keyed hash for the store is ruled out: the salted SipHash stays (the maintainer, 24 November; 4.2).

Reading pass over the worker side of the store (22 November). The question was what else a worker writes per change that nobody reads on the common path, and what is destroyed on another thread.
- W1, spend items that need no destructor. A spend was a 104-byte `Undo` holding a `Coin` that `ConfirmBlock()` never reads, yet the vector's destruction ran `~Coin` per item and pulled a line per spend out of the worker's cache. Built as a trial, without a harness run (W2 had read 1.00 there): `StoredCoin` is split into `StoredCoinData` (the bytes and their readers, trivially copyable and destructible) and `StoredCoin` (the owner of the heap block of a script over 35 bytes); a record item holds a `StoredCoinData` and is 92 bytes without a destructor; a long-script coin's owner lives in `WindowBlock::long_coins`, which the item points into, and the unwind copies the coin back where it moved it. Test `long_script_coins_through_a_block_record`. The maintainer's run: 10:26.94 against 10:34.57 for the series with W2 (7.1; user CPU 0.984x), then the fastest on record, and "Yes, put it on."; the block records it acted on went with the read-only window (4.5).
- W2, no `Change` vector. `ApplyChanges()` moved every dirty view entry into a 104-byte `Change` that `ApplyBatch()` read once and the end destroyed item by item (about 1.4 MiB a full block). It now applies from the view's entries: `CoinsStore::Change` holds a pointer to the outpoint and one to the coin (24 bytes) and the view is emptied after the apply instead of before; the store's unit tests and the fuzz target write changes through `test/util/coinsstore.h` (`OwnedChange`, a free `ApplyBatch()`), so that production has no entry point for tests alone. Harness 1.00 (589.9 against 590.6 blocks/s). It was built on "W2 seems like a decent change to me. Lets do that first.", dropped on "Yeah, drop it." after his question whether it simplified the code (it did not: 51 insertions against 49 deletions in four files and a 36-line test helper, a new lifetime precondition on `Change`, and the harness was flat), and put back on "Ok, give me W2 as well." after his run of 10:34.57. It is on the series.
- W3 (the record written once: each item goes to a local vector and is moved to the record at the end of the shard visit, 104 bytes per spend and 36 per create a second time, in first-level cache; writing in place means holding the slot mutex over the shard visit), W4 (the index vectors, `shard_of`, `hashes`, `order`, `starts`, `next` and `busy`: 24 bytes per change as read, 16 once W2 removed `applied`, and six allocations per apply, the same again per `FetchFor()`) and W5 (`FetchFor()`'s result vector, 96 bytes per input; removing it means inserting into the view with the shard held, or a second pass; next to the closed lookups trial): small, not recommended.
- Destroyed off the allocating thread, complete as read: the record and the plain creates (on the connector; a freeing thread is ruled out); map cells erased by another worker (they go to the shard's own list, under its mutex); the heap block of a script over 35 bytes (rare); for marked blocks the entries `ConfirmBlock()` erases on the connector (BIP30-checked heights only). Nothing new there.
- The profile read after the three top commits (no run): the one share without a known cause is the workers' short sleeps, 6.4% of a worker's time in about 1,550 sleeps a second (7.11); the switch stacks were read with a 2% cut and name one cause. Candidate first step, not started: the same reports read without the cut.

Second reading sweep (22 November, tip cbef835ea5; three readers, leading claims checked in the code; no share below is measured for that tip):
- S1, closed ("S1 doesn't count", reindex only; not to be proposed again). `-reindex-chainstate` builds the undo data of every block and drops it: `UpdateCoins` reserves a vector per transaction and moves a coin per input, and `SettleUndo` clears it when `write_undo` is false.
- S2 and S3, tried as a trial on the maintainer's word ("do them as experimental changes just to see how much they give us. I will discard them again"), two commits on tip 274baabfc8. S2: the two serialised sizes of a transaction are known at parse time (`TxSegments`) and thrown away, and `CheckTransaction` and the block size test could then be sums. S3: witness items are zero-filled before they are read (`v.resize` then `is.read`). Result: 10:33.99 against 10:26.94, user CPU 1.004x (7.1): nothing measurable. Dropped with the trial, as announced; the estimate of 1 to 2% of wall for S2 was wrong. Not to be proposed again. S7, measured only together with them, shows no gain either.
- S4, withdrawn: "I'm very apprehensive at turning CheckBlock into a stateful function, or changing its order of operation." `CheckBlock` walks a block's transactions five times (merkle, size, coinbase scan, `CheckTransaction`, sigops); merging the loops changes which reject reason wins, and carrying the sigops or the size out makes the function stateful. Nothing built. Item 7 was offered next and left.
- S5, the window width: `MAX_CONNECT_WINDOW` is 64, so 32 workers have 32 blocks behind 32 running; late sleep for lack of blocks was 1.9% (7.4): small. The maintainer's run with a wider window (5.2a) found nothing.
- S6, the store map. It grows only at one coin per bucket while only the first cell of a chain is prefetched. By the Poisson chain lengths a create (which walks the whole chain to find nothing) reads past the first cell in 9% of cases at half a coin per bucket, 17% at three quarters and 26% at one; a spend or lookup of a coin of random age in 21%, 30% and 37%, less for the young coins most spends take. Calculated, not counted; the time share is unknown. Growing at half a coin per bucket would cost 4 to 8 bytes more per coin (against 147): closed, "If S6 costs more memory per coin, that is out of the question." Never propose a map change that costs bytes per coin; the second prefetch pass, which costs none, is untried.
- S7, `Detach()` writes the dirty-span bucket, which the links prefetch does not ask for (a span is 1024 heights and a bucket 16 bytes, so the arrays are about 15 KiB a shard and 4 MiB in all: the line is in a cache, never in main memory). On the series then (`PrefetchDetach()`, 274baabfc8, no longer in the tree), on the maintainer's word ("A one-line tiny change still seems fine to me"); no gain measured.
- S8, large per-block frees reach glibc's trim path (`_int_free_maybe_trim` 2.38% on 17 November); a no-code run with `GLIBC_TUNABLES` is the first step. Set aside by ruling (5.6).
- S9, small: validation caches consulted by window blocks; `create_directories` on a read-only block file open; `own_txdata` built with scripts off; `Map::erase` computing an iterator nobody reads; the shard mutex on the map header's line; two `FlushStateToDisk` entries per published block.

The apply-only experiment (22 November, on the maintainer's word: "create a separate commit on top that skips actual validation as executed during a reindex chainstate. I want to see how a pure \"reindex\" operation would perform"; then "No hidden option. Just skip the checks." and "This is a dirty experiment, the tests are obviously going to fail, lets them. Just make sure it doesn't corrupt anything"): one commit on the series tip, not part of the series. A block whose index entry is `BLOCK_VALID_SCRIPTS` (every block of a `-reindex-chainstate`) is applied without `CheckBlock()`, the BIP30 scan, the input amount and maturity checks, BIP68, the signature operations, the scripts and the coinbase amount; the read and parse, the deferral, the undo build and the coin write-out stay. No option; a block not connected before is checked as usual. Index safety was checked once on regtest with a throwaway script: after two reindexes (window on and off) the tips and every header of every branch were as before, the coin set hash was the same, and a restart without reindex passed `verifychain` level 4. The maintainer's run took about 8 minutes (7.1).

A sweep was made by the maintainer on 2026-11-21, on a tree that had a target for the store under window blocks (address and undefined sanitizers; the corpus of 16006 files replays clean, edge coverage flat at 2199; also `connect_block` and other targets, no finding; full-r13-verified.md section N).

It matches the thread pool deadlock fixed on 26 November (a task freed under the pool's lock while holding a block whose deleter submits to the same pool, 4.3); not reproduced, so not proven.
