Closed on 2026-11-22, C16's pointer: `chainstate_write_tests`. With master's original file the case passed 40 of 40 alone and the suite 160 of 160 under 32 at once; by reading, outside the window a flush settles its run inside `FlushStateToDisk()` and `NotifyTipAdvanced()` hands the tip notification on before cs_main goes, so the order is master's. The file is master's again, and the commit message says one existing test changes, not two. The rulings closed with it are in 5.6.

The decisions of 15 November (fixes, closures and rulings of review rounds 8 to 11) are in the review record (4.11); the costs accepted among them are in 5.7, the test gaps in 5.5.

### 5.4 Parked

One trial is parked: the front stall (15 lines; it fails an existing test, 7.5).

- Unkeyed per-block outpoint tables, a known limit left for review (27 November, the maintainer: "Doesn't seem important enough. Lets document it and if it comes up during actual review, we can still easily address it there."). The four open-addressed tables of outpoints, the spent table of `ConnectBlock()`, the own outputs of `PrepareCommit()`, the creates of `CommitGroup()` and `WindowOutputs` (the outputs of the window's blocks, each shard's probe start precomputed), take an outpoint's slot from eight bytes of its txid and the output index, with no key. A miner who grinds txids to agree in the slot's bits puts every input of a block in one probe run: about n^2/2 probes in the lookup and again in the insert, about 3x10^8 each for a block at the weight limit, on one worker, of the order of a second. Bounded: the block needs valid proof of work on a chain with enough work, and nothing is decided wrongly, since every table confirms a match against the full outpoint (a `CommitGroup()` tag collision only leaves two changes un-netted). Bitcoin Core keys every table of attacker-chosen data (`SaltedOutpointHasher`), so a reviewer may ask. The fix is built: 0de7b09208 on `utxo-partial-order-keyed-spent-trial`, a keyed SipHash-1-3 tag of the txid (one random key for the process, `TxidTag()`), about 3.5% slower on the harness at `-dbcache=1024` (section 7, the ports from Will's branch). The shard pick (4.8, item 2) is grindable as well: it buys contention on one shard and a larger share of a forced flush's scratch memory, which stages a whole shard's changes (`ShardIndex()`), and decides nothing wrongly.

- Pruned download stalls on the prune run, for the low-end phase (7.1, the pruned syncs to 970,000 of 25 November; moved out of 5.3 on the maintainer's word, 26 November: "Yes, do it"): the block files grow to the target, then one run writes the coins out nearly to the tip while the sync waits, about 30% of the time with a 20 GB cache. Not acceptable as remedies, on the maintainer's word: letting the files exceed the target, or pruning before it with more headroom. The candidate: background write-out runs started by the block bytes above the written head as well as by memory, so that the written head stays close behind the tip, and at the target pruning below the written head without forcing a run (one is forced only where nothing below the head frees enough). The files stay within the target; the cost is more writes and compaction in the coins database, as fewer coins cancel between runs. Not built or measured; where the stall comes from (the connector's wait for the run, or the full download window) to be confirmed from a log first. Why it does not hold the series: no pruned sync of master was made (on the maintainer's word), and master in initial block download writes every dirty coin up to the tip at each prune event, under cs_main, where the series writes only the changes at or below the prune height; the stall is the connector's wait for the run (7.7), and letting a prune run overlap publication is the low-end phase's design question, on the write-out logic kept for that phase.

From the comparison round of 7.7, on paper only:

- P8, outputs first: a block's creations are inserted before its spends and a miss is final once every lower open block has published its creations (a watermark). The window enters a block's outputs before the block is validated (4.5); a miss made final by a watermark was not built.
- P9, sorted immutable runs by age with a hash-prefix directory in place of the node table (Hornet's shape): rewrites 4.2; paper only.
- P10, a salted aggregate of creations minus spends (SwiftSync's check) as a test oracle: cheap, optional, not built.

From the survey of other implementations (four readers: the other Bitcoin nodes, the Bitcoin Cash and BSV lineage, other chains with low-level techniques and their upstream state, rbitcoin; sources in 10). None of the candidates has a gain measured by its authors; the readers' claims were checked here only where they touch this code. On paper only:

- S1, a second request for the lowest missing block. Gocoin (up to three copies of the lowest blocks), rbitcoin (up to four peers, chosen by queued requests over measured rate, only once the queue behind the hole is a quarter full, far requests held while the hole is open, a silent request moved after 30 s), the BSV node and BU (a re-request without a disconnect) all do it; libbitcoin and Floresta drop the relatively slowest peer instead. It is the "second request" alternative of the stalled-front row in 5.2 and, unlike the front-stall trial (7.5), disconnects no one earlier, so `p2p_ibd_stalling.py` would keep its assertion. rbitcoin's own defect is the caveat: a getdata cannot be cancelled, so the copies a peer still owes must be counted, or the node floods itself (77 to 85% of peers' upload re-sent, by its changelog).
- S2, a request budget in bytes (rbitcoin): bytes queued plus a reserve per outstanding request from the largest of the last 32 block sizes; a requested block is never dropped for budget and a reader never parked. One rule for all heights. It touches the uncounted receive tasks of 5.1; the counted budget for parsed blocks was dropped (8.1), this one counts requests.
- S3, Schnorr batch verification with one batch per worker and a single-verify fallback. The window already runs a block's scripts inline on its worker (4.2), so a batch needs no shared queue and may span blocks. Gated on secp256k1 1134 (open); Core 29491 is a draft, and its one sync-level measurement, of a batch behind a mutex, was slower than master. Its reach is taproot signatures above the assumevalid block; that stretch was 9.7% of the connect in 7.1 and its taproot share is not known.
- S6, a block's prevouts read in key order (the BSV node sorts them; a code comment is its only evidence). Not checked against the fetch tasks here.
- Looked at and not taken: outputs first (BU and BCHN run it as two serial loops, Flowee keeps the insertion serial because it contends and drops the order check before CTOR, Teranode's variant sits in a disabled path with no rollback; no measured gain anywhere, which leaves P8 where it is); dependency groups inside a block (BSV; the window supplies that parallelism); a per-block arena for parsed blocks (libbitcoin; `CTransactionRef` outlives the block); script template fast paths (rbitcoin's findings log has eight consensus defects in them); a store other than LevelDB (BCHN's LMDB trial: 3 h 06 against 3 h 11, not significant); libbitcoin's speculative signature capture and GPU batch (a second mode; off by default on the CPU in its own code); raised thread priority; an io_uring read path; fuse filters (they save misses, which the sync's lookups rarely are).
- rbitcoin's findings on several blocks validated as one unit, as a checklist for this design: an input bound to a creator in a higher block (its 087), the residue of a rejected speculative application (114), and the point where a missing input becomes invalid (089). Here these are the window's outputs, which show a block only what unpublished blocks below it make (`WindowOutputs`), the commit that is taken back from the block at fault up (`TakeBackCommit()`, I7), and the lone connect that decides a missing input; the unit cases `double_spend_in_the_window_is_taken_back` and `invalid_window_block_is_judged_alone` stand for them.

On punishment with a receive pool, the maintainer's condition: the checks still make the peer disconnect at once on failure. The verdict travels by block hash and peer id (`BlockChecked`, `mapBlockSource`, `MaybePunishNodeForBlock`); `Misbehaving` sets a flag and the disconnect is the first thing `SendMessages` does.
- Structural points from an outside review (25 November), shelved on the maintainer's word: the process-global node depot (static depot and region table shared by every store; memory of a destroyed chainstate never returned; a kernel-review concern; settled on 28 November: each store owns its cell pools, 4.3); the single effects executor carrying both per-block effects and write-out runs (its order is load-bearing for durability; not a limit in any sync so far); the store fusing the in-memory map with LevelDB batching details; the kernel callback contract (internal thread, no calls back) to settle before a release.

### 5.5 Never measured, never exercised, no test

Statements of this kind stand where their subject is; the ones with no other home:

- Never exercised: the kernel library build on the harness host and anything above 16 cores or 16 threads on the harness. On the tree of the tip: ThreadSanitizer and a network sync on the maintainer's machine (7.1).
- No test exists for two interleavings (the old-file sync barrier, prune under a paused write): each needs a pause hook in production code; the maintainer keeps them untested (15 November, "Keep the test gaps"). The low-severity review items (C4, C5, V5, D3 and the known design costs) stay as they are on the same day.
- The store, no test: `ApplyConfirmed()` from several threads against runs and eviction, compared with a model; a run in flight while a confirmed change lands on an entry the run collected; a coin made below the apply height filed by a take-back (`CreateLocked()`); `Change::script` and the drop of unspendable creates. `Event::BatchOverwrite` is raised only from the cache-batch path.
- The view, no test at its own level: `ChangesCommitted()`, the overlay (`SetOverlay()`, `SetTransaction()`) and a conflict reported by the commit; the window's cases cover them through the node.
- The window, no test: a block that completes ahead of a lower one in a unit test (the stepper reaches blocks that complete in the order they were staged); which blocks share a commit; a take-back whose read of the undo data fails; `ConnectBlock()`'s `implicit_creates` path on its own. The take-back has a unit case (`double_spend_in_the_window_is_taken_back`) and no functional one.
- Still untested, each for want of a pause hook in production code: `feature_async_undo.py`'s read step (a read of undo data while its write is still queued needs the executor held); a getdata put off while its block is being stored, which `p2p_failed_block_not_served.py` does not reach (the lock order there is unverified by any test).
- Never exercised by any test, in the file work of 4.14: a disk that fills between the claim of a position and its write; a read-ahead that fails; a target that changes between a reorganisation's two holds; the read use of `InvalidateBlock()`. No measurement on a slow disk exists for any of it.
- Never measured: how uneven shards are on mainnet; how often a commit is taken back on mainnet (the `Connect window:` line counts the conflicts); more workers than cores; the script stretch above assumevalid in the window.

### 5.6 Closed by the maintainer, not to be raised again

- The block write is not queued behind the connect (23 November; asked by the maintainer and withdrawn, "No, don't do that"). O2 and O11 rest on a block being written before it is recorded as stored. Not to be proposed again. Tip and RPC block writes and reads are not optimised either ("little point in optimizing those"); they stay on their callers (4.14).
- Rulings of 2026-11-22, after the test gaps commit (tip fb1255d0f0):
  - glibc tuning (S8 of 5.2b, and the tcache and page-cache runs named in 5.2): "I don't want to do more glibc optimisation. Lets leave that for the future once this series actually lands." Not to be raised before the series lands.
  - The store's totals (item 5 of 5.2b): the maintainer thinks they can go at some point and need not be kept indefinitely, but they stay for now. "Don't bring them up again."
  - Item 9 of 5.2b and S9 stay as they are: "doesn't seem material enough to spend more time on."
  - Item 7 of 5.2b (the undo data of a sync serialised once on the worker) left as it is, on the recommendation given: no effect on -reindex-chainstate, sync gain never measured, a second shape beside the solo block's structure, and a change to the undo write path. "Ok, lets leave it as you recommended." Not to be raised again unless the maintainer does.
  - The maintainer ran the full functional suite and `feature_dbcrash.py` on the tip fb1255d0f0. Not to be asked for again unless a change is material (production code that changes what is written, replayed or connected; not tests, comments or messages).
- Rulings of 2026-11-21 and 22 on the last review round's leftovers: `interface_usdt_utxocache.py` on a build with USDT tracing, never run against the store, is set aside ("Don't worry about usdt"), not to be raised again. C2, a reader seeing neither coin while a block that overwrote a coin was open, reachable only by the two BIP30 duplicates: "just document both of them. Don't seem worthwhile to fix" (it was documented at `Reading::BLOCK_COIN` in `coins_entry_machine.h`, which the read-only window removed); not to be raised as a fix again. X4, the bound on receive tasks is a count with no account of bytes, at most `RECEIVE_THREADS` tasks of one `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` message and its parsed block each (the comment at `HandOffBlock()`), closed on the same word; not to be raised as a fix again.
- The coins database's LevelDB cache is not resized while running (CCoinsViewDB::ResizeCache removed with the connect window): with a snapshot both databases keep about their opening share, at most MAX_COINS_DB_CACHE (8 MiB) more than intended, until restart. Accepted (15 November, the maintainer: "It simply doesn't matter whether there is an extra chainstate with like 8MiBs more of memory. Completely inconsequential."). Never raise it again, in reviews or elsewhere.
- From the outside reviewer's trim list of 26 November (5.9): the flush's four workers stay ("Keep the flush workers, those seem trivial."), the block cache and `-blockcache` stay ("That stays."), the receive pool stays ("Lets keep the pool then for now"), and the published tip snapshot stays ("Keep the published tip snapshot. It is a nice feature in my opinion."; "We're going to find a different way.").
The slow profiles of the benchcoin run without the receive pool are an outlier, and no re-run is asked for (7.5). No perf on the development box. No sync from public signet peers. Resident memory of the highest cache size plus about 2 GiB is fine (7.6). No further conditions on when the window opens (C2). LevelDB is not tuned. The low-end write-out logic stays. A short phrase with two possible referents is asked back before it is recorded as a decision (8.2).

### 5.7 Regressions, known and expected

Made for pass 3 at 82a1b90f76 (14 November). The heavy-block sync and reindex rates show none (4.9, 7.7).

Measured, against master (figures in 4.9):

- Empty blocks at two cores: 0.78x to 0.96x of master's rate; ahead of master from four cores. The branch spends about 1.4x master's CPU on an empty block's own path and spreads it over threads that two cores cannot all run; the code that holds the extra cost is not identified (4.9).
- Resident memory: higher at harness scale at two and at sixteen cores; the window's blocks and the block cache are outside `-dbcache`.
- CPU on a sync from one peer: idle connect workers cost CPU, the same rate for 0.75x the CPU with two workers and a window of four. Cause not read.

Expected from the code, never measured:

- Outside initial block download `PublishConnectTip()` waits for the effects executor and applies its completions (review round 2, 4.11), so the undo write is on the connect path at the tip, as master writes undo on that path too. Never measured alone; the tip as a whole was measured against master on 26 November (5.2a): the series at or ahead of master in every case.
- The prune gate since review round 2 (4.10, 4.11): the figures of the prune rule were measured with a run for each block file; the gate now makes no run below the prune target. Equal or better expected, not rerun.

Accepted, each with its lever if it is taken up (decided 15 November):

- The window's apply views keep a pool each (read 28 November, not measured): a view empties its map after each block and makes its pool again with the map sized for as many entries (`BlockApplyView::ChangesCommitted()`), so each of the `-connectwindow` views (64) holds a fresh pool chunk (256 KiB) and a bucket array while idle; after the download only the tip's view is used, so about 16 MiB or more stays with views that no block uses until restart. The store's own base map (CCoinsViewCache's) is unused and holds one chunk. Not acted on: the views do use their maps while connecting (a block's changes), so a smaller chunk would cost them.
- A store's pools keep their peak (the maintainer: "maybe we just need to accept this for now"): freed cells stay with their shards for reuse, and the regions go back only with the store, so resident memory stays at the initial block download peak, at most about `-maxmempool` above the budget once the mempool takes back its allowance. The lever: when the store shrinks below that peak, evict clean entries by region (the emptiest 32 MiB regions first, occupancy read from the shards' lists) and release a region once no live cell is left; no cell moves. Compaction and a full flush-and-drop at the end of initial block download were weighed and set aside. Not measurable with -reindex-chainstate runs, which never leave initial block download.
- The pending pong, documented and kept (no test-only machinery to preserve master's send-and-ping meaning). A ping's answer waits in `Peer::m_pending_pong` until the peer's own block tasks are done and every hand-off to the connector made before it is done (`ChainstateManager::HandOffsDone()`), and no further message of that peer is taken meanwhile (`net_processing.cpp`); master's pong waits only behind that peer's own earlier blocks. Far inside the 20-minute ping timeout; the throughput lost in initial block download is not measured. Ruled on 22 November (review round 14, 5.2a): the held pong stays, as on master; the real fix is a pong answered at once with a barrier in the test framework, outside the series.
- C5's replay cost (C5 amended, option A): a replay after a crash reads only kept blocks because pruning is held at the written head's fork with the chain, with a write-out run first where that holds a file back; the pruned node's lag cap is gone for good. Cost: a crash on a pruned node with a large cache may replay thousands of blocks at startup, as master after a long flush interval; gain: about half the coin writes on a pruned sync (4.10).
- A deep reorganisation's memory (D3): a step that disconnected blocks checks the coins cache only in `FinishConnectStep()`, so its connects can take the tier over the critical budget by about the depth times one block's changes; master checks per block, but also holds a reorganisation's new branch in `connected_blocks` outside `-dbcache` until the block that passes the old tip's work (its step returns there, `ActivateBestChainStep()`), so about the same depth (corrected 2026-11-18 against e211dd0186; this said master held the whole new branch). The lever (option B, which the maintainer liked): in a step that disconnected blocks, end the step after a connected block that leaves the cache critical, and let the next step carry on along the branch.
- The kernel's validation callbacks may run on the effects executor's thread and must not call back into the chainstate manager (V8, option B: the kernel interface is experimental, a documented break is fine); the sentence is in `bitcoinkernel.h` (with the window commit), no code change.

### 5.8 A store without entry lists (designed and tried; on the series since 26 November)

Asked: "just do the design for now and see what you think of it", after the per-coin floors and the slab simulation (7.1's notes of 26 November). The store as it stands is in 4.2; this section keeps the design reading, the trial and its figures.

What the lists did. Every entry was on one list of its shard: a dirty entry on the list of its span of 1,024 change heights (`dirty_spans`, with each span's byte sum), a clean one on the clean list (`clean_young`, `clean_old`, second chance by `referenced`). `Detach()` and `Attach()` move an entry at every change; `CollectChunk()` walks the dirty lists up to a run's bound, `BoundForBytes()` finds the bound from the span sums and, inside the span, from the entries' heights; `EvictClean()` walks the clean list from its old end; `Flush()` walks every dirty list. The links are 8 bytes of the 96-byte cell, and the list field 2 bits of the height word.

The design. The cell keeps its chain link, outpoint, coin and height-and-flag word: 4 + 36 + 44 + 4 = 88 bytes, the floor for a fixed cell with a chain (689 bits of content). The map, its prefetches, the node allocator, the kept lists and the depot are unchanged, and nodes still never move, so a run in flight and the kept lists keep their pointers.
- Dirty accounting: per shard a byte sum for each span of 64 heights over the shard's dirty range (at most about 15,000 spans, 31 MiB over all shards at full cache, sized to the range as `dirty_spans` is now), moved where an entry's dirty bytes or filed height change. `BoundForBytes()` reads the sums alone and rounds the bound down to a span's edge; no entry is read.
- Runs: `CollectChunk()` walks the shard's buckets in order with a cursor kept in the run, taking dirty entries at or below the bound (and the owed erase under a create above it, as now), prefetching the next chain heads as `PrefetchAll()` does; a chunk is sorted before it is written, as now, so map order costs nothing. A shard does not grow its bucket array while a run is in flight on it (lookups may insert clean copies meanwhile; the load factor runs over for that time), so the cursor stays valid. `COLLECTED` and the settle are unchanged.
- Eviction: a clock per shard, a bucket cursor that sweeps the map, skips dirty entries, clears `referenced` on a clean entry that has it and erases one that has not: the second chance without a list. `Flush()` walks every bucket.
- Gone: `Detach()`, `Attach()`, the `List` field, the list heads and the list half of `SanityCheck()`, and the rule that an entry leaves its list before its height changes (`Enter()`'s assertion). The entry machine, its events and its height rule stay; so do the lag runs, eviction and the owed erase, which the low-end rule keeps (2), in another form.

What it buys. 103 to about 95 bytes a coin on mainnet (88-byte cell, about 5.8 bytes of bucket array, about 1.5 of long scripts), 18.2 to 16.8 GiB at the peak, the node about 18.6 GiB against 20.0. Each change today detaches and attaches its entry, writing the neighbours' links: two to three more cache lines written per change, which goes, and the commit (`RunCommitWork`, 5 to 6.5% of cycles on mainnet) with it in part; not measured.

What it costs. A run reads every entry of the shards it walks, dirty or clean, where the lists read the dirty ones up to the bound: at `-dbcache=2000` about 20 million entries read sequentially with prefetch for a run writing 12.3 million, against a pointer chase over the 12.3 million; likely no slower, not measured. Eviction reads dirty entries to skip them. A chunk's place in a shard is a bucket index, not a height, so a run's chunks no longer go oldest first inside a shard; nothing depends on that within a run (the bound is fixed and the chunk is sorted). The precision of a run's target falls to 64 heights.

Not taken, with the reason:
- Open addressing in place of the chain: 4-byte slots in a table between 40 and 80% full cost about 6.9 bytes a coin (6.1 growing by 1.5x) against 9.8 for chain link and bucket array, so about 3 bytes more; but a probe past a slot of another key reads that slot's cell unless the slot carries a fingerprint, and a fingerprint in a 4-byte slot needs cells numbered per shard (24 bits), so per-shard regions in the depot. Worth it only after the lists are gone and measured.
- A clean bit in the slot (an outside reviewer's suggestion): it belongs to open addressing.
- Variable records in slabs: 82 to 86 bytes a coin with 13 to 17% waste at 1.5 to 2 copies (simulated), 4 to 8 bytes below a fixed cell with an index, for records that move.

Beside the cache. At the peak 1.83 GiB, by reading in part: the window's parsed blocks (up to 64 late blocks, about 0.3 GiB), the bins (up to about 0.17 GiB; cut on 26 November, back in a smaller form since 28 November, 4.3), the block index in memory (about 975,000 entries, about 0.15 GiB), kept lists, the pending-txid table and small LevelDB caches (tens of MiB); the block cache is not filled by a reindex. About 1.1 GiB is not accounted for by reading, most likely allocator arenas and fragmentation of the 30-odd threads; only a measurement (`malloc_info`, or the byte ledger's accounts at the peak) would say. By the end of the run the gap is 4.9 GiB, the depot keeping the regions of freed cells (accepted, 4.3).

Built (26 November, on the maintainer's word "lets just try this out"): trial 9b00f56e82 on 2d478529df, branch `utxo-partial-order-listfree-trial`, 97 insertions and 130 deletions in the store. Kept exact: `BoundForBytes()` (the existing tests pin exact heights), with the height inside the span from a walk of the maps of the shards that have entries there; a run's walk starts over at each chunk (a chunk is settled before its shard is walked again) instead of keeping a cursor, so no freeze of the bucket array is needed; eviction passes over a shard with no clean entry. Kept as well: `Detach()` and `Attach()`, now only the accounting of the dirty count and the span sums, and an entry's list tag (dirty, clean or none), which `SanityCheck()` checks against the dirty flag. No test changed; 403 unit tests pass. Chain DX (16 workers, BIP30 off, three alternating rounds, against 2d478529df): at `-dbcache=30` 55.0 to 57.7 against 43.6 to 46.0 blocks/s (1.25x), CPU 114 to 123 against 157 to 165 s, 95 runs writing 11.2 million changes against 146 writing 13.8 million; at 450 and 1024 level; the coins cache at the tip 22.7 against 24.6 MiB for the same 246,383 coins (7.7% less); UTXO hash as expected at the default cache and at 30. Whether the gain at 30 is the density or the lists' work was not separated. Taken onto the series the same day on the maintainer's word ("fold this change in", into the store's commit): the store's commit has no entry lists, and the later store commits make the cell 96, 92 and 88 bytes (7.7); their messages give these sizes, and their rate figures were measured on cells with links. The maintainer's runs of the trial are in 7.1 (9:49.32, 27:38.62).

Reading. The list-free store is the last large step a fixed cell allows: about 8% fewer bytes and fewer concepts, not enough alone for a 20 GB machine (18.6 against a 17.5 GiB budget), which needs about 1.1 GiB more from beside the cache or from the open-addressed index.

### 5.9 An outside reviewer's trim list (Neon, 26 November): the decisions

The reviewer judged that a quarter to a third of the diff does not pay for its review cost and sorted it in three groups: delete outright, cut unless re-measured on today's tree, and separate pull requests. The maintainer: "we are going to focus on item 1". Against the record, the first group crossed rulings in places: the removal of the flush's four workers was measured and closed (M1, 17 November); the catch-up mode and the lock-task layer had been patched on 26 November on the maintainer's word; the state machines answer his stated preference for explicit machines (C1), and file work off `cs_main` is the "I/O to the edges" goal (4.14). Under the rule that a new technique overrides earlier rulings when the maintainer asks for it, those were his to reopen. His decision on the checked list: "Keep the flush workers, those seem trivial. Measure the receive pool with your own ibd connection harness. Ditto the table merge with additionally your chain DX sync. Remove the 4 items you list there." The three of the four that went took 182 lines out; in `build-clang` that tree built without a warning and passed the unit suite (403) and fifteen functional runs of the window, the store, reindex and the hand-offs. The rest of the first group was then read on "go over the other things that are slated for removal" (report only) and acted on where the maintainer said "execute". Below, one entry per item with its outcome. Harness figures are chain DX synced from three local master peers (`syncX.sh`) unless said.

Group 1, delete outright:

- **The machine headers and the O1 to O15 catalog** (five of the six headers, `util/machine.h`, `blockstore_machine.h`, `durability_machine.h`, `connect_machine.h` and `lifecycle.h`, with `coins_entry_machine.h` and `message_tip_rule.h` to stay; claimed about 1,600 to 2,000 lines). Kept: no defect found, and the maintainer's preference for explicit machines (C1) decides. Counted: 466 lines and 687 of tests at the first count; after the durability state went, `util/machine.h` 77 lines, `connect_machine.h` 65, `lifecycle.h` 126, `blockstore_machine.h` 91 and `durability_machine.h` 58 (the flush order), 417 in all, their tests about 400; the catalog about 90 lines of comment in validation.h, cited by number about 35 times. Wrong in the list: `BLOCK_STORE_TABLE` is a constexpr array and allocates nothing.
- **The durability state** (claimed: `DURABILITY_TABLE` no longer used). Removed from the history (the maintainer: "remove the durability_table then. Are you sure those tests are still needed? Remove them otherwise. Then check the other three items"). The table was already gone, but `DurabilityState`, which it had run on, and the block manager's `DataDurability()` and `UndoDurability()` were left, read by tests only. The rule is documented on `m_unsynced_block_files` and `m_unsynced_undo_files`, and the tests read the sets through `TestBlockManager` (`BlockFileUnsynced()`, `UndoFileUnsynced()`). The cases that are each the only check of a set kept right stay (a write off the lock marks its file again, undo data in a left file, the other cursor's file, the left block file): the commit's assertion catches a batch that owes a sync, never a file missing from the sets. Gone: `durability_follows_the_files`, every step of which a narrower case covers (its prune check saw only the status flag, the file being synced before the prune), and the set checks in the failed-sync cases and the left-file write-out case (the failure memory is what keeps the index there; the left file staying unsynced is the block manager tests' case). 35 lines added, 148 removed. The flush order table stays.
- **`RunWipeWorker()` and the flush's four workers** (claimed: about 12% of the shutdown write). Kept: it is the workers' body, the workers were measured significant (M1, 8.4), and the maintainer: "Keep the flush workers, those seem trivial."
- **`CatchingUp()` in `SyncMode`** (claimed: about 120 lines). Removed from the history (the maintainer: "execute"). A node more than 144 blocks behind its best header after the latch synced in the download mode until it was 24 behind. It came with the receive hand-off commit without a ruling; it was a further rule for when the window opens (against "no new window exceptions") and contradicted C2, whose kept deviation says such a node catches up serially. Gone with it: its constants, `m_catching_up`, the pass's copy of the latch (the download mode is the latch at the pass's start again), two unit cases and `p2p_catch_up_handoff.py`.
- **`ReleaseBlockFilesBelow()`**, block files let go of the page cache 2 GiB behind the tip (`POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED`). Removed, one of the four items. Its own record: huge page faults falling back to small pages late in a sync, and a mainnet reindex of an earlier form at 13:28.92 against 13:47.17 to 14:18.66 without it, inside the spread of two runs of one tree (7.1).
- **The `BLOCK_FILE_WORK_UNDER_CS_MAIN` scopes** (claimed: 19 of them, one path missed). Kept on reading: 13 scopes in production then, 12 since the take-back lost its undo read (28 November) (12 by the macro, one by `std::optional<node::BlockFileWorkUnderMain>` for a reorganisation's block to connect that is not in memory), the rest in tests; every file open goes through the checked `OpenBlockFile()`/`OpenUndoFile()`, and every scope sits where `cs_main` is held. The missed path was real: the window's take-back (`TakeBackCommit()` under the publish hold's `LOCK(cs_main)`) reads a block's undo data when the undo was on disk before the block was staged (`write_undo` false, as in `-reindex-chainstate`), with no scope, so a `DEBUG_LOCKORDER` build stops there. It is reachable only if a commit meets a conflict among blocks that were connected before, which a consistent chainstate does not produce, and no test reaches it (the take-back tests' blocks were never connected). Fixed in the commit that checks the rule (the maintainer: "execute"): a scope in the lambda, with its line in the rule's list in validation.h (4.14). Since 28 November the take-back reads no undo data at all: no consistent chainstate reaches it (a conflict makes the block at fault invalid, and the blocks taken back are it and its descendants, none ever connected, so none has undo on disk), and on the maintainer's word the read, its scope, its line in the list and the fatal error for a failed read are gone, with no assertion in their place (8.1). Also read: the scope in `WriteBlockUndo()` has no production caller, and a kernel library user that reads a block or its spent outputs from inside a validation callback (immediate signals, under `cs_main`) meets the check as well.
- **`ReorgReads`** (claimed: overlaps the open PR #36000). The disconnect half kept, the connect half cut. As read: up to 16 disconnected blocks with their undo and 16 blocks to connect, read on the block I/O threads before the hold, about 150 lines, two unit cases, never measured. #36000 (prefetch while connecting, l0rinc) reads ahead only the new branch's first blocks, waits for them under `cs_main` and has nothing for the disconnect side; its conflict with the series is the rewrite of `ConnectTip()`/`ActivateBestChainStep()`, not `ReorgReads`. Without the read-ahead every reorganisation reads under the hold, as on master, through the fallback scopes. The connect half, the part #36000 would duplicate, went with the block bins (below, "Lets try them out then"), and with it the one production caller of `BlockCache::Has()`, which went later with the other functions only tests called (8.1, 28 November).1).
- **The lock-task layer, by a small pool of its own for receive tasks.** Done. A first trial (3f8951a508 on c43ca88e93, branch `utxo-partial-order-recvpool-trial`, not for the series; client on 12 cores, `-dbcache=1024`, three alternating rounds): the series 235.7, 231.8, 233.4 blocks/s; four threads of its own 238.8, 229.1, 233.9 (level); two threads 121.7, 123.6, 124.9 (half). A fixed small pool kept the gain at four threads with little margin; the layer was left in, so its lines were not counted. The size, on the maintainer's word ("a pool that allows to keep up with a 2.5Gbps line would be a decent size"): 312.5 MB/s at about 63 MB/s a thread (17.9 ms of parse and checks per 1.13 MB heavy block on the harness host) is five threads. Trial 0896c93347 (on f5f1eb5dc6, which is c0cdbdb928 with the first trial; branch `utxo-partial-order-recvpool5`): both parts of a receive task on the pool, the part that records the block under cs_main included (the first trial had left that part on the connect pool, so the layer still guarded it), the layer left in for the measurement; three alternating rounds, load average 3.7 to 7.5 after the runs: at `-dbcache=1024` the tip 248.4, 247.4, 243.3 blocks/s, the trial 250.5, 249.7, 247.9; at 450 246.0, 251.5, 250.4 against 248.4, 252.5, 251.1; ahead in all six pairs by 0.4 to 4.6 blocks/s (means 1.2% and 0.6%), inside the tip's own spread; client CPU 1.04x (63.3 s against 60.8 s). With nothing that takes cs_main on the connect pool, the layer had no case left. Made the series' form on the maintainer's word ("Ok, go."): the hand-off commit hands blocks to that pool (4.7), and `LockTask`, `LockTaskLimit()`, the `LOCK_TASK` scope, `RegisterLockTaskThreads()` and its unit case are gone from the history, the sync commit's part included. `FreeWorker.tla` models the layer and no longer describes the series (9.2).
- **The window's multi-job scaffolding** (claimed: `StartCommitJob` asserts `sync.jobs.empty()`), to one optional `CommitJob`. Done, one of the four items: one commit job at most (`WindowSync::job`; no queue of jobs, no `ready`, `in_job` or `draining`, which nothing read).
- **`CoinsStore`'s `CCoinsViewCache` inheritance** (claimed: 11 overrides, an unused `PoolResource`, the `thread_local` coin of `AccessCoin()`). Kept. 13 of the cache's methods are overridden (not 11) and 5 of the view's; the base's map and its pool resource are built and unused (one 256 KiB chunk per store, `PoolResource` allocating at construction); `AccessCoin()` returns the thread's copy, valid until the thread's next call, which no caller in the tree holds across a second call. Dropping the inheritance would change the type `CoinsTip()` returns: about 23 production sites (the mempool's checks and limit, `VerifyDB`, the snapshot load, the RPC and REST coin reads, the mempool view's base) and 37 test sites, most of them master's, take the tip as a `CCoinsViewCache`, and of the 14 methods the series makes virtual in coins.h, 5 serve `BlockApplyView` as well.
- **`BlockApplyView::InputCoin()`'s pointer identity**, by a span of coins. Kept, the item of the list the maintainer's "4 items" left: passing the coins explicitly would take overloads of `Consensus::CheckTxInputs()`, `GetTransactionSigOpCost()`, `CheckInputScripts()` and the BIP68 heights in `src/consensus` and validation, and an outpoint not found by its address is answered by the view's own lookup, so the trick is a shortcut, not a trap: a miss costs a lookup, never a wrong answer. The reviewer's second report puts the overloads at 26 lines; nobody has counted.
- **`PublishedTip()`'s `m_tip_unpublished` branch.** Kept: a reader during a hold that moved the tip takes `cs_main` and reads the chain. The kernel's `blockTip` notification fires inside that hold (`NotifyTipAdvanced()`), before the hold's publisher publishes at its end; a client woken by it (`waitfornewblock`) and then asking `getbestblockhash` would read the old tip without the branch. Publishing in `NotifyTipAdvanced()` before the notification would be the alternative, a change of design, not a removal.
- **Three of the four open-addressed tables of validation.cpp, with `WindowCoinSource::FindCoins()` and `BlockApplyView::Prefetch()`** (claimed: about 170 dead lines). The uncalled functions removed, one of the four items: `BlockApplyView::Prefetch()`, `WindowCoinSource::FindCoins()` and `CoinsStore::Fetch()`. The three outpoint tables as one helper were tried and not taken (trial 9d3c45af08 on c43ca88e93, branch `utxo-partial-order-tables-trial`, 70 lines added, 54 removed; chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, three alternating rounds): level at `-dbcache=30` and 450 (0.985x, inside the spread), 0.96x at 1024 (681.6, 680.0, 687.3 against 721.9, 709.3, 706.4, lower in every round), the chain DX sync level (233 to 237 against 234 to 241); UTXO hash as expected. Likely cause: the spent-outpoint table of `ConnectBlock()` carries a tag beside each pointer, 16-byte slots in place of 8. Longer and slower at 1024. The fourth table is the window's sharded outputs table, not in question.

Group 2, cut unless re-measured:

- **The block bins** (claimed: 1.023x on the mutating window). Cut from the history (the maintainer: "Lets first look at the block bins", then "Lets try them out then"): a block built on the connect pool takes the deleter every other block has, its free a pool task, and the three emptying calls are gone (4.3). Probe 378daf957e on 3dce4e6d4b, branch `utxo-partial-order-nobins`, client on cores 0-11, three alternating rounds, load average 4.8 to 7.3 after the runs. At `-dbcache=1024` the tip 244.2, 245.3, 232.2 blocks/s, without bins 239.9, 240.7, 233.2; at 450 244.4, 242.5, 239.9 against 236.4, 243.7, 238.9. Rates inside the spread (means 1.1% lower at both); client CPU higher in every pair (61.4 s against 64.2 s on average, 1.05x). The harness sync is capped by the network thread (7.7), so a CPU saving does not reach its rate.
- **The block cache and `-blockcache`** (claimed: at most 1.04x on the harness). They stay (the maintainer: "That stays."); no `-blockcache=0` measurement. The claim takes the network harness, whose block files sit in the page cache (7.7); the figure on the network path is 1.18 to 1.4x (row 6 of 4.1).
- **The byte ledger** (claimed: no combined cap). Removed from the history together with `SyncMode`, on the maintainer's word ("prune those three items", the tests that existed for a dropped feature going with it), each taken out of the commits that brought it. Each holder keeps its own count; `util::ByteAccount` stays, in `util/byte_account.h`, with its unit cases; the undo backlog's limit is the constant it was. `SyncMode`: an operation's copy of the latch is a `bool in_ibd`, the rule is documented on `IsInitialBlockDownload()`, and `sync_mode_tests` went, its one case of substance moved to `validation_chainstate_tests`. The two together: 236 lines added, 378 removed.
- **The published tip snapshot**, the third of "those three items". Taken out as well and measured afterwards (chain DX sync, the tree without it, e9cb08006f, against the receive-pool trial: 2.1% lower at `-dbcache=1024`, level at 450, inside the spread), then restored: it stays (the maintainer: "Keep the published tip snapshot. It is a nice feature in my opinion."; "We're going to find a different way.").
- **The effects executor's own thread**, should the lagging write-out leave the first stack. Not taken up; its removal was measured on 15 November and lost in both forms (row 3 of 4.1, Trial 4 of 7.7).

Group 3, separate pull requests (the per-peer request rule; transaction hashing from stream bytes; `StoredCoin` and the 88-byte cell with the store's cell pools; the lagging database with the two-head replay, as a third wave):

- **The receive pool, then all net processing changes, out of the series** (asked: "Remove the receive pool again, I think it belongs into a different series", then "try and get rid of any net_processing changes"). Two probes, nothing changed on the series. Master's `net_processing.cpp` and `.h` build against the series with two lines elsewhere (the shutdown call to `FinishBlockTasks()` in init.cpp, the unit setup's `blocks_on_message_handler`; six fuzz targets set it too): probe df5751143b on c43ca88e93, branch `utxo-partial-order-norecv`. Nothing passes `activate_async` then: blocks are accepted and activated on the message handler, and the connector thread, the hand-off machine, the message tip rule, the catch-up mode and the lock tasks are left without a caller. Client on 12 cores, `-dbcache=1024`, three alternating rounds: the series 240.4, 232.3, 230.9 blocks/s; master's net processing 35.1, 35.3, 35.0 (6.7x slower, CPU 94 against 61 s); plain master 19.0 in one run, paying the BIP30 check. The reindex is unchanged (700.6 against 704.8). Activated as it arrives, a block meets a window of one: the connector's queue is what fills the window in a sync. Removing the receive pool alone (blocks parsed and stored on the handler, activated by the connector; branch `utxo-partial-order-recvless`, on c43ca88e93): 45.6 blocks/s in the one round that finished; the two others stopped connecting (one at 3,491, nothing for four and a half minutes until the timeout, no stall or disconnect logged). The stops were a worker of the connect pool freeing a finished task under the pool's lock: the task held a block's last reference, whose deleter submits to the same pool; the fix is on the series (4.3). Fixed, the probe synced at 46.2 to 47.8 blocks/s in eight rounds against 239.0 for the series, its message handler on CPU 34.3 of 38.3 s. Per heavy block (1.13 MB) on the handler: parse with the transaction hashing 14.4 ms, the block's checks 3.5, the write 2.3, the hand-off 0.5, of 20.8 ms; an asynchronous write alone would gain at most 1.13x. Either way the series' sync gain rests on the network side. The pool stays (the maintainer, 26 November: "Lets keep the pool then for now"); the probe branch keeps the removal.
- The other items of the group were not taken up.

A second report from the same reviewer (26 November), read against the record; nothing changed. Already done by then: the catch-up mode and the durability state were out. Stale or wrong in it: the series was then 138 files and 21,084 insertions; 13 production scopes, not 12; the block cache's harness figure on the network path is 1.18 to 1.4x, not "at most 1.04x"; the rare hang of `p2p_ibd_stalling_handoff.py` (5.3) matches the thread pool deadlock fixed that day, not the shared pool as such, though, not reproduced, it is not proven. Standing rulings it crosses: the node depot (shelved 25 November, 5.4), a store fuzz target (never to be proposed), the machine headers (C1).

## 6. How to measure

### 6.1 Method

The maintainer: "I just don't believe that this is the ceiling already." And: "you are allowed to write your own instrumentation and benchmarks, as long as they don't take long and don't slow you down. Are you really sure you need all these probes?" Kept from this: a reference on the same box (master on the harness chain), counts of work per transaction and not only timers, and trying the change itself on a throwaway branch; kept when it beats the noise of alternating runs, dropped otherwise.

Throughput in the window stretch is threads times the share on CPU divided by the CPU a transaction costs; a limit on one factor is not a ceiling.

### 6.2 A run on the maintainer's machine

Mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` to height 975073, `-dbcache=30000` (no lag run happens during the connect at that size: every coin, 166 million changes, is still unwritten at the stop height), 32 connect workers, `-par=16`, `-debug=coindb`, 32 vcores. Every run command carries `-disablewallet`: a loaded wallet past its birthday does per-transaction work on `BlockConnected` during a reindex and filled the validation-interface queue (hundreds of seconds of publisher wait in two runs). Bench per-block figures divide by the blocks connected (`num_blocks_total`). `-debug=bench` slows the publisher; sampled runs say so.

### 6.3 The harness

Scripts are in `/work/utxo-partial-order-harness/` (README). Chain DX is kept there too (`chains/chainDX`: the builder's datadir and `tip.txt`, the one durable copy; the serving and reindex datadirs of a run are copies of it); chain D is a byte prefix of it and is not kept, and the other chains regenerate from their scripts. Datadirs in use and binary snapshots live in a session's temporary directory and go with it; their numbers are in these notes. Marks and the standing caveat are in the header; in particular the harness chain's blocks are all allocation and its store is small, so its ratio for anything about a block's memory is an upper bound, not a prediction.

Settings that every harness run needs:

- The baseline is branch `utxo-partial-order-baseline` (326374edc0): master e211dd0186, the series' base then, plus one probe commit that adds the series' `-test=bip30_off` to regtest, never for upstream. Built by `cpu/build_baseline.sh` with `build-bench`'s settings into `cpu/bin-baseline`. Every comparison against master uses it with the same options as the series; given no `-test` option it is plain master and serves the syncs. Straight master is no baseline on a regtest chain: it pays the BIP30 check on every block (7.7).

- A clean conf: a test-framework datadir carries `par=2` and `prevoutfetchthreads=1`.
- Regtest's consistency checks off: regtest alone defaults `-checkblockindex` and `-checkmempool` to every operation (`fDefaultConsistencyChecks`), so every measured node and every serving node gets `-checkblockindex=0 -checkmempool=0` (`run.sh`, `syncX.sh`).
- The BIP30 check off, through `-test=bip30_off` (regtest only, on the series since the fifth review, 4.11). Master has no such option, so master's runs pay the check, on one thread; the earlier `BENCH_NO_BIP30` probe switch is read by no current binary. Regtest, testnet4 and signet run the check on every block; mainnet only up to height 227,931 and from 1,983,702, the two repeat blocks aside (the BIP34 block is checked too: its parent has no ancestor at that height). `ConnectBlock` calls `HaveCoin` for every output a block creates, each a LevelDB `Get` that finds nothing under the database's one mutex, and from eight workers on the workers queue on it: `DBImpl::Get` is 7% of worker CPU at 4 workers and 42% at 16; a reindex runs 122 / 151 / 142 / 140 blocks/s at 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 workers with the check and 169 / 302 / 345 / 300 to 333 without (83 of 95 ms of worker time per block on chain C).
- `-dbcache=1024` in the network simulation; at 450 the window's byte cap made a false plateau (4.5).
- `-maxtipage=1` for a sync, or the client leaves initial block download once the tip is recent and no later block goes to the pool (`cpu/syncX.sh` passes it; its first runs on chain DX did not, left initial block download at chain D's end, connected the new heavy blocks outside the window at 22 blocks/s, and are void).
- A quiet host. The machine also runs other work (the maintainer's guix build on 18 November); a rate counts only with the foreign load during the run recorded and near its floor (`cpu/rounds.py`): about one core with nothing else on the machine, the driver's own polling. Idle sampled just before a run predicts nothing. The first run after a pause reads the block files cold and does not count (7.7).
- Pruning needs a sync: `-reindex-chainstate` refuses `-prune`. The minimum target is 550 MiB on every chain; `-fastprune` shrinks block files to 64 KiB and lowers regtest's prune-after height to 100 but leaves the target, and with 64 KiB files pruning is checked at nearly every block, a cadence no mainnet node has and one that would penalise master's prune flush far beyond mainnet. Chain D (428 MB of block and undo data) never prunes; chain DX does.
- The binding low-end corner is `-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5`, because the idle mempool's allowance is lent to the coins cache; `-dbcache=50` alone never bound it. There the tip ran 18 blocks/s against 55 unbound, core at 38 ms CPU a block against 11: the low-end path's own cost, never looked at (5.2).
- Matched builds and settings: harness snapshots are clang builds (`build-bench`); GCC builds run about 7% slower on this harness and are not compared with them (a harness check once found an apparent regression to be exactly that). Compare runs only at matched `-dbcache`: on chain D's `-reindex-chainstate` 1024 against 2000 MiB moves the rate about 4%. A full reindex of chain D is bound by the block loader, not the connect (7.7).
- `taskset` does not change the detected core count; chain confs pin `par` and the workers (the `tune/` harness of 4.9).
- Resident memory: the harness records the peaks of anonymous and file-backed resident memory beside VmHWM (`tune/memsample.py` in `cpu/run.sh`), and the sync recorder (`net/recorder.py`) a column of each, since VmRSS counts LevelDB's mapped tables, which the kernel drops under pressure (an outside reviewer's point, Neon, 26 November); on chain DX the file-backed part is 11 to 59 MiB of about 400.

Chains:

| Chain | What it is | What it cannot show |
|---|---|---|
| C | 500 blocks of 3000 two-input transactions, outputs spent 40 blocks later | no block spends an output made fewer than 40 blocks below it, so no input is found in the window's outputs at a window narrower than 40 |
| D | `cpu/build_chain_d.py`: 300 heavy blocks of 3000 two-in two-out transactions, inputs 60% old, 30% one to twenty blocks old, 10% from the same block (assumed shares); defers about 12.6% of transactions at 16 workers | its coins come from fan-out transactions, so its shards are uneven; not kept: DX begins with its blocks, so a chain D reindex is a chain DX reindex with `-stopatheight=2781` |
| DX | `cpu/build_chain_dx.py`: chain D continued on a copy of its datadir, the coin pool rescanned from the UTXO set in 40 age groups; 1,500 more heavy blocks of D's shape (heights 2,782 to 4,281, built in 1,645 s), then 2,100 empty; 6,381 blocks, 2.51 GB of block and undo data in 16 file pairs, about 1.39 MB a heavy block | the same assumed shares as D; its top blocks are recent, so a sync needs `-maxtipage=1`; at `-prune=550` it prunes about a dozen times in its heavy section (inferred) |
| E | small blocks below the BIP34 height (4.6) | a block is so small that the serial threads compete with the workers (7.4) |
| M, N | the network simulation's chains (7.5) | |
| empty | `empty/build_chain_empty.py`: 40,000 coinbase-only blocks with timestamps in the past; `empty/run.py` follows the log and prints the per-thread split, `ab.sh` alternates master and the tip | only the fixed cost of a block; not a design input (C9) |

Drivers: `cpu/rounds.py` alternates binaries on chain D's `-reindex-chainstate` (through `runx.sh` and `run.sh`) from the rate line and prints the foreign load during each run; `cpu/syncX.sh` syncs any chain from one local peer running the baseline binary as plain master (`f14/bin-base`, in the job's temporary directory, is the series at 18dd95aeb5, not master), the client's options given per run, and reports prune events and the blocks directory's size (`cpu/syncD.sh` needs a series server binary that is gone). `cpu/ab.py` needs the sampler scripts, which were not kept, and `ab2.py`, `ab3.py`, `ab5.py`, `f14/*.sh` and `reqfix/syncn.sh` read log lines the consolidation removed (Worker phases, Connect pipeline, Block receive, Block cache:). Noise: only ratios of alternating runs in one session count, and below about 3% nothing is distinguished. The per-thread split comes from `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/schedstat`. "Window wait 0.00 s" under `-debug=bench` is the logging slowing the publisher. No perf on the development box (`perf_event_open` is refused under the seccomp filter).

## 7. Measurements

### 7.1 Runs on the maintainer's machine

Each run is the command of 6.2, named by its wall time. "Connect" is the connect time where the log gives it, otherwise the time from the start to "Shutdown in progress".

| Run | Date | State of the series | Connect | Flush | What it showed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 33:03 | 10 Nov | 24 workers | 24:36 | 8:27 | the first split of connect and write |
| 36:22 | 10 Nov | 32 workers, a wallet loaded | | | not comparable (6.2) |
| 31:17 | 10 Nov | 32 workers | 1363.5 s | 513.5 s | the sweep the largest worker phase (36 to 42% of worker wall); the write 73% stalls |
| 29:12 | 10 Nov | undo off the connector, two wipe workers | 1381 s | 369 s, 273 s in a stall at every 12th batch | the connector idle, the workers the bound |
| 24:05 | 10 Nov | the key-ordered flush | unchanged | 62.7 s, no stall | total 1.21x, all of it the flush |
| 21:59 | 10 Nov | the sweep removed | 1254.6 s | 62.2 s | total 1318.9 s; 1.10x on the connect against an estimate of 1.3x |
| 21:46 | 10 Nov | 1024 shards, eight flush workers, 15 hash threads, per-phase instrumentation (all removed again) | 1264.6 s | 39.4 s, no stall | total 1305.8 s, 2447% CPU; worker time accounted to 99.6%, the short sleeps located |
| 23:55 | 11 Nov | job 1's defaults: 256 shards, two flush workers, four hash threads | 1292.8 s | 140.4 s, 65.4 s in 28 stalls | total 1435.1 s, 2191%; the stalls back, a shard exceeds a batch |
| 23:36 | 11 Nov | block bins, own hash | 1263.7 s | 151.1 s, 78.0 s in 32 stalls | total 1416.6 s, 2194%; 1.023x on the connect |

| 17:39.13 | 12 Nov | two flush commits, thread default, shard's table, depot (tip then 4b183c1dd1) | | not read out | 25563.66 s user, 857.94 s system, 2494% CPU; late workers asleep 17.5% to 1.9% (7.4); the step is not the depot's alone |
| 18:00.87 | 13 Nov | window below BIP34, spent mark; sampled | | | 26337.80 s user, 1205.20 s system; start to 240,000 2.5x, 840,000 to 860,000 slower (4.6) |
| 16:36.45 | 13 Nov | marker freed at confirmation; sampled | log span 995.5 s | 42.84 s, four workers | 25531.76 s user, 1090.02 s system; 1.085x on 18:00.87, 1.063x on 17:39.13 |
| 13:47.17 | 14 Nov | the series after pass 3; whether with the huge page commit (the 13:52.88 run of the second table) is not recorded; not sampled, `-networkactive=0` | | not read out | 20416.33 s user, 1308.30 s system, 2626% CPU; 1.205x on 16:36.45 with user CPU 0.80x and system 1.20x; the share of pass 3 and of the hint is not separated |
| 14:00.95 | 14 Nov | as 13:47.17, sampled with `-debug=bench` | log span 803.6 s | 35.36 s, four workers | 20577.11 s user, 1311.57 s system, 2602% CPU; 1.017x slower than the unsampled run; the reading below |

| 13:59.75 | 14 Nov | 47 commits (txid vector, input coins by position); not sampled, `-networkactive=0` | | not read out | 20121.27 s user, 1354.55 s system, 2557% CPU; against 13:47.17 wall 1.015x slower, user CPU 0.986x, system 1.035x, cores busy 25.6 against 26.3. The harness ratios of 7.3 (worker CPU 0.91x) did not carry over: at most 1.4% of CPU, no wall time. No two identical runs exist, so the run-to-run spread is not known |
| 14:10.94 | 14 Nov | 47 commits, `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.tcache_count=65535`; not sampled | 812.6 s to the stop height | 35.82 s, four workers, 104.87 s summed in the database's write | 20803.06 s user, 1288.74 s system, 2596% CPU; against 13:59.75 wall 1.013x slower and user CPU 1.034x: the larger per-thread cache gains nothing, not to be tried again. Stretches in seconds, this run / 14:00.95: to 240,000 26.7 / 24.4; to 840,000 505.5 / 491.7; to 860,000 30.1 / 29.9; to the tip 250.2 / 257.5; shutdown 37.6 / 36.6. Finer: 400,000 at 69.0, 550,000 at 184.9, 700,000 at 338.3, 800,000 at 455.4, 900,000 at 621.3, 950,000 at 689.2, the assumevalid block at 711.7; the 8,930 blocks above it 100.9 s. So a run is 711.7 s below assumevalid, 100.9 s of scripts and about 38 s of shutdown: below 10 minutes needs the part below assumevalid at about 460 s, 1.55x. Closing line: 401,880,995 transactions deferred of 1,476,335,135 (27.2%), 1 conflict, at 32 workers and a window of 64; the harness chain D defers 12.6%. The page cache run was not made (the maintainer: enough data). Rate by 10,000 blocks (`lograte.py`, 14 Nov): 2.46 to 2.55 million transactions a second from 550,000 to 570,000, 1.6 to 2.1 from 610,000 to 780,000 (870 to 1050 blocks/s), 1.26 from 810,000 to 820,000 (426 blocks/s), 2.7 to 2.8 from 840,000 to 860,000. Between 500,000 and 860,000 nothing but tip lines is logged and no gap between two tips exceeds 0.43 s (seven above 0.25 s, 2.2 s together, all between 784,000 and 853,000): the shortfall is steady, not pauses. The maintainer saw CPU use near 60% around 600,000 to 700,000; no sample exists there (the sampled run has 550,000 at 87% and 800,000 at 76% on CPU), so what the workers wait for in that stretch is not known; a `-debug=bench` log would give each phase's wall against CPU by stretch |

The same 166,055,947 changes are flushed each time. Not on that machine: a friend's mainnet sync (7.5).

What the runs of 10 and 11 November established and later runs did not overturn:

- The publisher's own serial time was about 650 s over a run, 0.7 to 0.9 ms a block: a floor that more workers do not move.
- Master's shutdown write was 83% inside the database's write call; the 29:12 run's stall at exactly every 12th batch confirmed the mechanism of 4.8, and the stalls of 23:55 to 22:39 were a shard of about 43 MiB exceeding a batch (4.8). With four or more flush workers the flush is at the database's serial write.
- Moving the undo off the connector bought no wall time: the connector fell to 3 to 5% of a core and waits on the window about 90% of the late chain. The workers are the bound.
- The sweep's removal gave a third of the sweep's time; the rest came back in batch and cont, since the sweep was mostly work that has to be done somewhere. The maintainer the same day: "Nono, definitely keep the low-end hardware logic! We will move to that shortly."
- The 15,073 blocks above the assumevalid block (966143) ran at 124 blocks/s, 9.7% of the connect, never sampled.
- Freeing a published block was 14% of worker time, and the short sleeps (about 1470 a block) sat mostly in the free, cont and batch phases, inside deallocation of what another worker allocated; what followed is in 4.3. When the two CPU commits took 824 sleeps a block away the blocked time barely fell: idle time follows the number and kind of the sleeps, not their length (7.4). The bins pay about 3.5% of worker time; the own hash costs about 1.4% on a machine with cores idle for a hash pool, and paid where every core is busy.
- 256 shards with the record freed in place against 1024: the store-touching phases wait 4.3 ms a block more; an inference, worth at most 1.05x late.
- After the 22:39 run the maintainer: "I think this is looking pretty good."

The 16:36.45 run. Stretches in seconds, this run / 18:00.87 / the sampled depot run of 12 November (log span 1102.0 s): to 240,000 25.0 / 26.2 / 65.0; to 840,000 590.5 / 621.5 / 639.1; to 860,000 36.7 / 74.1 / 38.8; to the tip 299.1 / 321.4 / 320.0; shutdown 44.2 (flush of 166.1 million changes with 96.38 s summed in the database's write). 6.7 million entries at 240,000, no lag run, peak 28162 MiB (in the 18:00.87 run the kept markers were 24.2 million entries more from 240,000 on, about 3.4 GiB, the cache at its ceiling at 839,999 and a lag run of 33 s in the stretch to 860,000). At 950,000: Window wait 592.62 s; worker phases wall/CPU in ms a block read 5.61/4.73, fetch 1.55/1.25, core 7.86/7.06, batch 3.49/3.22, cont 4.58/3.58 (sum 23.09/19.84); Confirm 130.04 s, Publish finish 6.29, postprocess 17.55. Workers (32 on a machine 3.8% idle) on CPU 56% at 300,000, 89% at 550,000, 82% at 800,000, 92% at 950,000, plus 4.5 to 5% waiting for a core. The publisher is on CPU 10 to 20% and waits for a core 10 to 31%; its timers are wall and include that wait.

The 14:00.95 run (sampled, the series after pass 3). Stretches in seconds against 16:36.45: to 240,000 24.4 / 25.0; to 840,000 491.7 / 590.5; to 860,000 29.9 / 36.7; to the tip 257.5 / 299.1; shutdown 36.6 / 44.2 (flush 35.36 s, 102.17 s summed in the database's write).

Six-second thread samples, the 32 connect workers each: at 300,000 56.2% on CPU and 1.8% waiting for a core; at 550,000 87.1% and 4.7%; at 800,000 76.0% and 3.2%; at 950,000 89.0% and 6.8%. The machine at 550,000 and 950,000 is full: 28.2 and 28.7 cores busy, 33 threads runnable on 32, 4 to 8% idle. The publisher thread runs 16 to 26% and waits for a core 23 to 30% late. Worker CPU per block, cumulative at 950,000: 16.74 ms (read 3.27, fetch 1.18, core 5.99, batch 3.07, cont 3.23), wall 20.00 ms; between 800,000 and 950,000 the phases are off the CPU 16% of their wall, of which the samples put 5 to 7 points in waiting for a core, the rest spread over read, core, cont and fetch at 2 to 4 points each. 800,000 to 840,000 is the one stretch with slack (465 and 596 blocks/s against 670 before and after): idle 17%, I/O wait 5.1%, system 7.2%, the store growing 116.5 MiB a second; the cause is not separated. Minor faults there are 31,937 a second, about that growth in 4 KiB pages (29,800).

Over the whole run the picture is the opposite of what that one sample suggests: all threads together had 4.70 million minor faults at 950,000 (17.9 GiB if each were 4 KiB) for 27.8 GiB resident, where the three earlier sampled runs had 8.9 to 10.0 million (34 to 38 GiB) for 29 to 32 GiB. By stretch against the 16:36.45 run, in GiB of 4 KiB faults: to 300,000 1.1 / 3.3, to 550,000 +3.0 / +8.1, to 800,000 +5.1 / +10.5, to 950,000 +8.7 / +12.5. So roughly 45% of this run's memory arrived in larger units than 4 KiB, most of it early, less as free memory fell (10.8, 5.1, 1.1, 0.8 GiB free at the four samples): the huge page hint (the 13:52.88 run) was very probably in the binary and granted in part, with fallback to small pages late. A first reading of the 800,000 sample alone as "no huge pages" was wrong. The 1.205x over 16:36.45 is therefore shared between pass 3 and the hint, not separated; whether the fallback or compaction costs time at 800,000 is not known (the machine's `thp_fault_alloc`, `thp_fault_fallback` and `compact_stall` counters would say).

Reading: from 400,000 on (92% of the wall before shutdown) the workers hold the machine; what is left on 32 hardware threads is CPU per block, which is work (deserialising 24% of worker CPU, core 36%, cont 18%, batch 16%, fetch 6%); no floor for it was ever measured on mainnet. The publisher's own time to 950,000 is 226 s wall, by sampled CPU nearer 80 to 160 s: the serial part a larger machine would meet first, 3.5x away by the wall figure. The flush is 4.4% of the run.

The shutdown line "Connect window: … (N wide)" read the width after the connect pool had stopped, so it always said 1; it reads it before since 609e733f58 (the window commit).

Inputs by position, without the view's map (24 November, on the maintainer's go after an outside suggestion; commit 35 of 9.3, 4.5; its first form 4365349ea1 on b223f274b8, measured here). A window block's inputs are resolved before it is checked into a list by position, from the window's outputs (`FindInputs()`, also the outputs of the block's earlier transactions), the tier (`CoinsStore::FetchInto()`) or the database; `ConnectBlock()` checks each transaction against its part (coin-taking `CheckTxInputs()` and `GetTransactionSigOpCost()`, the spent outputs prepared before the scripts), takes the undo data from it, and keeps the outpoints the block spent in a table of its own. The table is the block's rule against spending one outpoint twice: the suggestion as made left it to `PrepareCommit()` and the commit, and a scratch run with the table switched off shows why that is wrong: a window block whose two transactions spend one output an earlier transaction of the block made was connected (heights 114 to 116), since such spends never become commit items. With the table, that block and one spending an old coin twice fail in the window (no conflict) and again alone. Unit suites (chainstate, store, coins, block validation, transaction) and twelve functional runs pass (`feature_block.py`, `feature_taproot.py`, `feature_csv_activation.py`, `p2p_invalid_block.py` among them). Harness, chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, BIP30 off, two passes of two alternating rounds, load average 5 to 11: series / first form 608.5, 599.2, 633.6, 611.7 / 736.6, 735.8, 720.8, 711.7 blocks/s at `-dbcache=2000` (1.18x), 547.5, 548.0, 515.7, 523.3 / 640.9, 629.0, 604.4, 604.6 at 450 (1.16x); client CPU 42 to 44 s against 35 to 36 s a run (0.83x); peak RSS within 25 MiB; UTXO hash d4ba681a7662 as expected. The input coins by position of 14 November (probes per input cut, the map kept) gave 0.91x worker CPU on the harness and nothing on mainnet; the mainnet run below shows this one does.

Inputs by position behind the view's interface (24 November, on the maintainer's go after 9:45.32; the form the series keeps, commit 35 of 9.3, measured as 7ac390a422 on b223f274b8). Nothing under `src/consensus` changes: `ConnectBlock()` hands the window block's view each transaction and its coins by position (`BlockValidationContext::inputs_for`), and the view answers `AccessCoin()` and `HaveCoin()` for an input's own `prevout`, found by its address, from there; every other lookup goes as before. The spent table, the undo data from the list and the resolution are as in the first form; 191 lines in four files against 217 in six. Checks as for the first form, all passing, the scratch double-spend case (own output, old coin) included. Harness, two passes against the series, load average 7 to 12: 618.8, 617.4, 625.4, 622.2 / 703.3, 710.4, 699.0, 660.1 blocks/s at `-dbcache=2000` (1.12x), 507.5, 523.7, 532.4, 534.1 / 617.3, 620.3, 614.2, 605.6 at 450 (1.17x); against the first form in one pass 728.4, 710.2 / 719.8, 682.5 at 2000 and 601.9, 599.2 / 599.1, 606.0 at 450, with the same CPU: no difference beyond the spread. UTXO hash as expected.

The connector's publication hold, measured (24 November, throwaway branch `utxo-partial-order-hold-probe`, probe commit 628d49b820 on 7ac390a422, inputs by position on b223f274b8; outside suggestions on the commit's burst, the order of its groups and commits off cs_main). Harness, chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, BIP30 off, two rounds each. The hold in which the connector commits and publishes takes 58 to 73% of the wall time; the commit inside it 1.0 to 1.5 s of a 3.2 to 3.9 s run, the publication the rest (about 0.9 to 1.0 s). The connect pool is idle 29 to 41% of its worker time at 16 workers (42 to 68% at 30 on 16 cores), about three quarters of it begun during a hold. A commit holds 8.5 blocks on average at `-dbcache=2000` and 5 at 450, so `MAX_COMMIT_BLOCKS` is rarely reached: 32 / 16 / 8 gave 727, 696 / 705, 713 / 714, 686 blocks/s at 16 workers and 2000, 706, 684 / 677, 693 / 669, 659 at 30, and the same at 450 (no difference; 8 not better). Every worker asked to help a commit (`COMMIT_WORKERS_PER_HELPER` 1, at most 64, against 4 and 16): the commit 12 to 20% shorter (1.30-1.39 to 1.15-1.16 s at 2000, 1.15-1.17 to 0.92 s at 450), the rate 728, 710 / 709, 700 at 2000 and 618, 626 / 620, 653 at 450, the idle share unchanged: not distinguished. The order of the 64 groups was not tried (each group a 64th of a commit's items within a few percent). Reading: since the faster workers the hold is the harness's serial part, and its publication share, during which no block is staged (staging takes cs_main too), more than its commit; what publication spends per block (about 0.15 ms over 6,384 blocks) is not split yet. Mainnet's share of the hold was not measured.

Mainnet syncs to 970,000, pruned (harness host, 25 November 2026, the tip's production code in its `build-bench` binary, `bin-tip` at 62b2b93400): the series alone, on the maintainer's word ("only do a sync on the series. Don't sync master, it will take too long"), each node from a fresh copy of the presynced datadir, `-stopatheight=970000 -v2transport=0 -onlynet=ipv4 -prune=50000 -disablewallet -listen=0`, 16 cores, 9 to 10 public peers. Neither run reached 970,000; both datadirs are deleted.

- `-dbcache=4000` was stopped at 852,448 (about 62 minutes), when the maintainer had the cache raised: from the mid-chain on the UTXO set is about three times the cache, and the connection, not the peers, set the pace (70 to 110 blocks/s near 850,000, 35 of the 160 request places in use, 8 to 9.5 of 16 cores). `-dbcache=20000` was killed by the kernel's out-of-memory killer at 839,064 (56 minutes, exit 137): its resident set grew with the cache to 21.4 GB, beside about 6 GB of other jobs on the host's 30 GB. Up to there the larger cache was ahead at every reading, by little.
- Peers were not the limit: 10 peers delivered 320 to 340 MB/s in 10-second samples near 650,000 with every request place in use, while the average over the run was 165 MB/s (361 GB in 2,185 s, 4 GB run). Below 390,000 the rate is per-block bound (about 650 blocks/s, some 90 MB/s).
- The prune target does little in initial download, by master's rule: `FindFilesToPrune()` adds 1 MB for each block between the tip and the best header to its buffer, which here is hundreds of GB, so every prune takes every file it may. The series lets the block files grow to the target before it writes the coins out to the height pruning may reach (`FlushStateToDisk()`, the prune run), and then prunes nearly to the tip: the block files saw-tooth between about 2 and 53 GB.
- The prune cycle stalls the sync, measured on the 20 GB run by polling `getblockchaininfo` every 5 s from about 697,000 (`-debug=prune` turned on there): four cycles, 235, 252 and 330 s apart; in each, about 150 s at 220 to 265 blocks/s while the files grow from 2 to 51 GB, then 60 to 90 s at 0 to 40 blocks/s while the run and the removal of the pruned files are made. 60 of the 204 five-second samples advanced fewer than 100 blocks: the sync stood about 30% of the time, which is why the 20 GB cache gained so little here. The remedy is parked for the low-end phase (5.4, "Pruned download stalls on the prune run").

A write-out run for memory written beside the commits (trial, 25 November, on the maintainer's go: "try it out and test it for a tiny dbcache run"; one commit d454de361b on 590e4d6cd2, its branch deleted; dropped the same day on the maintainer's word, not to be proposed again for this series). Today a run started because the cache is at its limit streams to the database without cs_main, but the connector waits for it before the next commit: `ApplyConfirmed()` asserts that no run is in flight, since the run collects shard by shard while it writes and a change it has not collected yet would be lost to it. The trial collects such a run whole under the hold that starts it (`CoinsStore::PrecollectWriteOut()`: every change at or below the bound, by shard, with the nodes they came from), so commits may go on beside it; a change to a collected entry clears COLLECTED and stays dirty, as the entry machine already provides. The executor writes the collected changes in batches of shards and settles each batch. The connector waits for such a run only where the cache reaches its limit while it is in flight; runs for pruning, periodic writes, disconnects and the lone block's commit are waited for as before. Chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, `-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5`, 16 workers, BIP30 off, four alternating rounds, foreign load under one core: the tip 46.3 blocks/s (45.6 to 46.6), the trial 49.5 (48.4 to 51.1), 1.07x, higher in every round, resident memory alike (419 to 458 MiB). The trial ends on the expected UTXO hash after 180 runs for memory, and `verifychain 4` over all blocks passes. Not examined: the crash case the connector's wait is documented for (a run outliving the connection of the block that started it); the collected copies are outside the cache's budget (up to half of it while a run is in flight); no unit or functional test. A harness gain of this size predicts nothing for a sync by itself (see the memory profile notes); a 1 to 4 GB sync with the cache below the UTXO set is the measure that would decide it.

The store as a ring of the last blocks over the database (trial, 26 November, on the maintainer's go, after an outside reviewer's (Neon's) two-layer proposal; one commit 956209b6c9 on 2d478529df, branch `utxo-partial-order-ring-trial`). The store keeps only the changes of the last 288 to 432 blocks: once the written head is 144 blocks below tip - 288 a write-out run writes up to there and every clean entry is evicted when it lands, so coins read from the database are not kept; the periodic write of everything is off. The runs keep the series' discipline (the connector waits for each). Chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, BIP30 off, three alternating rounds: at steps of 16 blocks 0.53x the tip at `-dbcache=1024` and 0.55x at 450; with written coins kept in memory (the same writes, no database reads) 0.55x and 0.57x, so the reads cost little here (the page cache holds chain DX's database; 60% of its spends are of old coins); at steps of 144 blocks 0.81x (546 to 572 against 679 to 691 blocks/s), the heavy section 0.5 s longer of which 0.42 s is the wait for runs. Anonymous memory about 140 MiB above the tip (chain DX's whole set is about 30 MiB). Two unit test files fail on the periodic write the trial turns off. Not run on mainnet. Read against the record: 144-block steps make about 6,000 runs over mainnet, the shape of the removed fixed lag ceiling (4.10, benchcoin 390 GB written for 102 GB received); a database single read costs about 2 microseconds of CPU from the page cache (64 to 75 s for 35 million, 7.1). Retirement by bytes with a 288-block floor and runs beside the commits (d454de361b's `PrecollectWriteOut()`, ruled out for the series) is the store at a small cache without its clean copies.

Fewer changes per write-out run (26 November, asked after the `-dbcache=2000` run of 7.1: 60 runs, 735 million changes, the effects thread asleep half to two thirds of the run time and LevelDB's compaction thread 0.53 to 0.64 of a core with disk waits, from the per-thread tables at 800,000 and 900,000, the two threads told apart by creation order). Read, not built: an erase the database does not need comes only from an entry that is not fresh, and every create but a coinbase output's is fresh (`PrepareCommit()`, transaction 0 OVERWRITE, the others FRESH; `CreateLocked()`), as in master's `AddCoins()`; a fresh coin spent before a run is erased without a trace (I4). So the needless erases are coinbase outputs spent before they were written, and coins put back by a disconnect or a take-back: under 1% of the changes on mainnet at most. Not tried. The run's head marker rides with the highest shards (the stream goes from the highest shard down, `WriteRun()` puts the marker in its first batch), so no run's first batch spans the key range; only a final write's last batch does.

Per-coin floors on paper (26 November, asked: "just do the theoretical calculations"). Anchors: the store 103.0 bytes a coin on mainnet (18,608.5 MiB for 189,352,033 coins at 872,566, the peak, in the `-dbcache=30000` run of 7.1), 1.83 GiB of anonymous memory beside the cache there, and the shutdown write's 11,043 MiB of batches for 166,055,947 coins: 69.7 bytes a coin serialised, of it 3 of batch framing and 34 of key, so the compressed coin (height code, amount, script) is 32.7 bytes on average. At the peak: the store of that day 103.0 B (18.2 GiB, anonymous 20.0); a fixed cell without per-entry bookkeeping (no list links, no height and flag word: 84 bytes) 91 B (16.0 / 17.9); variable records in the database's encoding with a chain link 78.7 B (13.9 / 15.7) before fragmentation; with open addressing 73.9 B (13.0 / 14.9); with transaction ids shared, 66.6 B at 1.5 outputs a transaction id and 60.4 B at 2.0 (11.7 / 13.6 and 10.7 / 12.5); the data alone without an index 66.7 B. With 2.5 GiB left to the system, a 16 GB machine allows 66 B a coin, 20 GB 89 B, 24 GB 111 B. Unknown: outputs per transaction id in the set (from a `dumptxoutset` file, which groups coins by transaction id), and the waste of variable records as coins are spent (none in a fixed cell with free lists).
