- Four commits on the trial from that collection, on the maintainer's instruction of 24 November (trial tip 9754463b5a, nineteen trial commits). "A window block's size is estimated, not measured": the parsed block is counted once by its worker from its serialized size and its counts of transactions, inputs and outputs (`ParsedBlockBytes()`), no walk over scripts and witnesses; a block of unknown serialized size is still measured; the view and undo data as before. "A filter in front of each table of the outputs overlay": sixteen bits a tag or more, three bits of one word, asked before every probe of a table (`MayName()`). "The connect pool's free workers apply the shard groups, no commit pool": the connector asks one worker in four of the connect pool, sixteen at most, one for every 256 changes, with the task at the front of the pool's queue; `m_commit_pool` is gone; the store is still at the tip whenever cs_main is free. "The BIP30 check of a window block is made for the block at once": `CCoinsViewCache::HaveAnyOutput()`, which a cache answers output by output; a window block's view asks the overlay once per transaction (`NamedIds()`) and the store once per shard (`CoinsStore::MayHaveFor()`), and asks an output as before only where a window block names its id or the store may have it. The database read per output stays (by key, `HaveCoin()`); skipping it while nothing is written out was not built, it is a separate decision. No existing test changed: the unit suite has its two expected failures, the fifteen functional tests pass. Harness (chain DX, 16 workers, foreign load 2 to 10), before / after: BIP30 off 571.9 and 563.9 / 571.0 and 588.9 blocks/s at `-dbcache=2000` (CPU 47.2 and 48.3 / 45.1 and 45.7 s), 456.9 and 448.8 / 476.5 and 489.7 at 450 (CPU 50.5 and 51.5 / 48.7 and 49.1 s); BIP30 on 132.9 / 156.4 blocks/s (CPU 188.6 / 158.0 s), so the check is still most of such a run and what is left of it is the database read; UTXO hash d4ba681a7662 after an off and after an on run. The harness does not predict mainnet for this trial: it shows only that nothing broke and that CPU fell by about 5%. Whether the 11:25.22 comes down is the maintainer's run to say.
- The maintainer's mainnet run of the nineteenth commit (24 November, 7.1): 10:58.80, 26 s faster than the fifteenth commit, 15 s slower than the eleventh and 26 s slower than the series at 30 workers, with 502 s more user CPU than the eleventh commit and 647 s more than the series. The four commits pay on mainnet, but every run of the trial with all blocks on the overlay and the commit in the publishing hold is slower than the eleventh commit's form (workers commit before publication), and uses more CPU. Where the extra CPU goes has not been measured on this tip.
- Single-table outputs overlay (24 November, branch utxo-partial-order-single-table-trial, 81a25cdbdf on 9754463b5a, not on the trial): one table for the window in 1024 mutex-guarded shards, items carry the height, one probe per read, reads grouped by shard, published blocks' items taken out one by one by the workers. Harness, chain DX, against 9754463b5a, UTXO hash equal with BIP30 on and off: dbcache 2000 569 against 612 blocks/s (CPU 47.8 s against 43.8 s), dbcache 450 510 against 544, BIP30 on 162.1 against 163.5; peak RSS 730 to 846 MiB against 403 (the erase, one thread at a time, falls behind the entries). A first form with 64 shards and the erase before a block's entry ran 540 against 607. Reading: an item now costs an entry and an erase in a shared table, where a block's own table was built in cache and dropped whole; that outweighs the filter checks saved. No gain expected on mainnet; not timed there. The eleventh commit's commit form remains the fallback.
- Creation-only outputs overlay with a commit that can be taken back (24 November, b800502205 on the trial): the form of Will's branch. The table above carried every input as a spend item because a commit of confirmed changes could not be taken back and stopped the process on a conflict, so validation had to catch every cross-block double spend. Now the table holds one entry per transaction of an unpublished block and no spends; a block validated with a coin a lower window block spent meets the conflict at its commit, CoinsStore::ApplyConfirmed() reports the changes it did not make, and TakeBackCommit() takes the first conflicting block and the job's blocks above it out of the store with the inverse changes (creates spent, spends put back from undo data, in memory or read from disk), under the same hold; that block is then connected alone. Nothing is kept for this on the valid path. Harness, chain DX, against 9754463b5a, UTXO hash equal with BIP30 on and off: dbcache 2000 603 and 601 against 594 and 600 blocks/s, dbcache 450 517 and 520 against 511 and 523, BIP30 on 160.8 against 154.2; CPU and peak RSS equal (406 against 415 MiB). Level, within the margin; the harness predicts nothing for mainnet here. Not exercised by any test: the take-back (no unit or functional test of the subset makes a cross-block double spend inside one commit job). A failed read of undo data during a take-back still stops the process. The maintainer's mainnet run (7.1): 10:36.16, the fastest of the trial, 3.6 s behind the series.
- A `Reference()` that skips dirty entries (24 November, the last item of the outside branch, on the maintainer's word; one line on b800502205, not committed, diff at /work/patches/utxo-partial-order-reference-skips-dirty-trial.diff): the mark is set on clean entries only, since eviction passes over the clean lists only. Harness, chain DX, BIP30 off, foreign load 5 to 8, against b800502205, UTXO hash equal: 593 and 584 against 611 and 600 blocks/s at `-dbcache=2000`, 513 and 500 against 499 and 520 at 450; CPU and RSS equal. No gain, 3% lower at 2000 inside the margin: the mark was already written once per entry, into a line the spend writes anyway. Dropped.
- Reading of willcl-ark's utxo-partial-order-v2 at f38c755a3a (24 November): store files unchanged since -v2-c2; five new commits are hardening (shutdown, file pipeline, assumeutxo isolation, network hand-off, ExecuteConnectStep) and a fast path past the empty signature and script caches, which the maintainer ruled out as a cheat over master.
- Perf collection of the trial at 6afae10107 by the maintainer (24 November, `/work/reports.tar.gz`, script `perf-collect-2026-11-24.sh`, the 11:25 command line; A at 302 to 342 s, B at 581 to 618 s, B wholly past assumevalid), read beside the collection of 22 November (7.11; that one is the series at an older tip with 32 workers and no block-read pool, so shares compare loosely). Window A, per 10 s: 263.6 s task clock (289.5 then), 265,567 context switches (645,268), 919 G instructions (932 G), branch misses 3.31 G (1.97 G), dTLB load misses 701 M (523 M). Threads in A: the 30 workers 23.45 cores on CPU (78% each; 89% then), the four block readers 1.38, the four commit threads 1.10, the connector 0.39 with 0.23 waiting for a processor after a wake-up. Nobody is saturated: a reader spends 1.07 of 3 s in futex waits (no work) beside 1.45 s in `read`, a worker about 0.46 of 3 s, the connector 1.33 of 3 s over 10,039 futex calls. The workers' switches are preemptions inside `FindCoins()` and validation, not lock waits; the mutex symbols of the series' profile (5.6%) are gone, malloc and free are 16% against 22%. Cycles in A by symbol: `WindowOutputs::Ask` 12.7%, `FindCoins` 3.8%, `Index` 1.2%, `FindCoin` 0.4% (the overlay, 18%: an input no window block names is asked in the table of every unpublished block below its own); `RunCommitWork` 5.1% with `ApplyConfirmed` 3.4% and `CreateConfirmedLocked` 2.1% (the commit, level with the series' `CreateLocked`, `ApplyChanges` and `ApplyBatch` at 11%; three fifths of `RunCommitWork` runs on the workers at their help points); `node::BlockCache::Bytes` 4.0% (5.0% in the call graph: `RecursiveDynamicUsage` of the block, once in `IndexedBlockBytes()` and once for `s.bytes` in `RunContinuation()`; the series estimates from the serialized size; in since 2c2c894e35, so before the eleventh commit); `RunContinuation` itself 3.3%, its inlined parts not resolved. Window B: the workers at 100%, secp256k1 85% of cycles, the overlay 1.3%, 3,418 G instructions per 10 s: nothing of the trial shows there. Not answered by the collection: which of the three commits after the eleventh added the 1,420 s of user CPU (there is no profile of the eleventh commit; the overlay and `Bytes` were in it), and what the idle workers wait for (the lowest block of the window or the commit in the publishing hold; the log's `Trial overlay` and `Connect window` lines would say). Candidates from it, not built: one probe per input in the overlay instead of one per open table (a filter over a table's tags, or one table for the window); the block's size estimated as in the series.
- The BIP30 check on the trial, read on 24 November on the maintainer's word (nothing changed). Since 117a6c49a7 the blocks where BIP30 is checked are on the overlay, and `ConnectBlock()` asks `HaveCoin()` per output: an insert and erase in the view's table, `WindowOutputs::FindCoin()` over the table of every unpublished block below (two writes to each table's shared reader count and a probe, about 30 tables at a window of 60, always the whole way since the probe is a miss), `CoinsStore::LookupFor()` with two shard locks, and a database read under LevelDB's mutex, of a database that is still empty. The inputs have the batched `FindCoins()`; the outputs do not. Fix candidates, not built: the overlay asked once per transaction for the whole block in one pass; the store checked in a batch per shard without the view's table; the database read skipped while no write-out has landed. The bound, from the maintainer's log of a run of 6afae10107 (24 November): height 227,931 at 28.54 s after the first log line (13:27:14.75 to 13:27:43.29; 14,935,237 transactions, cache 475.7 MiB, 4,463,707 coins), start-up included. So at most 28.5 s of about 685 s, 4%, is below BIP34, and a fix gains some part of that; the series' time to that height is on no record, so the trial's extra cost in the stretch is not known. It does not explain the 41 s the trial lost between its eleventh and fifteenth commits by itself.
- Flat tables (the outside branch's table form, on the maintainer's instruction of 24 November; built on 6afae10107, measured and not committed; the diff is `/work/patches/utxo-partial-order-flat-tables-trial.diff`). A shard's table is one piece of memory, a control byte per slot and then the nodes themselves, groups of sixteen searched with SSE2, tombstones, the salted SipHash kept. Our lists were kept: their links are slot numbers, rewritten when a table is made again, and a run in flight settles its entries by key. Memory is counted by capacity and a table is made smaller in `EvictClean()` at a third full. The node allocator and the depot are unused with it. No existing test needed a change: the unit suite (the two expected failures) and the fifteen functional tests pass. Harness, BIP30 off, foreign load 3 to 8, series / trial tip / flat tables: 574 and 569 / 586 and 559 / 565 and 551 at `-dbcache=2000`, 407 and 412 / 482 and 463 / 482 and 481 at 450, 47.7 and 48.3 / 45.6 and 45.6 / 42.7 and 41.5 at 30 (CPU 162 s to 204-211 s there); peak RSS 40 to 70 MiB higher; UTXO hash d4ba681a7662 with BIP30 off and on. So nothing at full cache or 450 and 8% slower at 30, as expected from fewer coins per byte. It crosses "no more memory per coin" and the entry below that closes redesign 2. The harness store (400 MiB) sits far nearer the processor's caches than mainnet's, so the full-cache figure says little about mainnet; only a run by the maintainer of the diff could. A variant without the lists (their table scans for the write-out and a clock eviction) was not built.
- What consolidating on the trial's design would save (24 November, asked; an inventory by reading at 673064eebc, function by function, by one reader; three of its claims checked against the code). Deletable from the series' production code: 1,750 to 2,100 of about 12,600 added lines (store 1,050 to 1,200 in `coinsstore.cpp`, 300 to 350 in `coinsstore.h`, 150 to 170 in `coins_entry_machine.h`, 230 to 280 in validation), plus 120 to 150 lines of the trial's own two-path glue. To add: 250 to 450 lines (a spent set of the window or a check pass before a batch applies, so that a double spend across window blocks is a verdict and not an abort; BIP30 through the overlay; the owed erase for a confirmed create over a spent entry the database still holds; the tip path through `ApplyConfirmed()`; the step exits with committed, unpublished blocks). Net: 1,400 to 2,000 lines below series plus trial, so about the series' size without the trial or up to 6% under it. Tests: 1,800 to 2,200 of about 9,200 lines go, about 550 to rewrite, 300 to 500 to add. Concepts that go: a block's identity in the store (slots, open, applied, confirmed), per-block records and unwind, visibility by height, takeover, the conflict count, the pending-txid table, deferred transactions, `CollectRecords()`, 17 or so of the entry machine's 27 rules. Concepts that come: the outputs overlay with its reader counts, the ordered batch commit in shard groups with netting (not reversible once a group applied), the gate between commits and write-out runs (load-bearing for correctness: with it a run needs no records), committed but unpublished blocks. Findings of the reading: every block on regtest, signet and testnet4 and every mainnet block below 227,931 enforces BIP30 and so takes the old path (`staged.confirmed_commit` false), so no unit or functional test runs the trial's path without `-test=bip30_off`; `CreateConfirmedLocked()` does not keep the owed erase (a wrong database state for a recreated outpoint under a lag run is possible by reading, not shown); the deferral in `ConnectBlock()` is already dead in the trial.
- Step 1 of the consolidation, the trial made consensus-complete (24 November, trial tip 117a6c49a7, one commit). Every window block but the genesis block takes the overlay and the confirmed commit; `-test=bip30_off` is no longer needed to reach the path. The overlay is exact: a block's table holds its transactions and its inputs (16-byte slots, capacity the next power of two above 1.5 times transactions plus inputs, against 12 bytes times twice the transactions before; `IndexedBlockBytes()` does not count the growth), a matching tag is checked against the block, the first table below the viewer that names an outpoint decides it (spent, made, or no such output), and the scan runs down to the published height, not the committed one, so an outpoint no table names has one value in the store whatever is committed. The BIP30 check of `ConnectBlock()` so reads the window, a double spend across window blocks is a missing input of the higher block, and a failed block is retried alone for the verdict. `CreateConfirmedLocked()` keeps the owed erase (`BlockCreateOnConfirmed`). The failure it closes, by reading: `ConfirmedWrite` over a dirty spent entry refiles it at the create's height; if that create is disconnected before a run carried it, after a lag run moved the written head past the old erase height, and the node then crashes, the replay never touches the outpoint and the database keeps the coin. A step that ends with committed, unpublished blocks takes them back in `DrainWindow()`: the store is set to the highest of them and they are disconnected with their undo data through one cache, highest first; the abort there is gone and a stop request no longer publishes the committed blocks first. Checks: unit suites pass but for three cases of `validation_chainstate_tests` whose expectations belong to the slot path (`failing_deferral_ends_a_window_step` expects a deferred transaction; `write_out_under_open_blocks_costs_a_retry_and_no_verdict` expects a conflict and a retry that no longer happen; `failed_periodic_write_out_ends_a_window_step` expects the periodic write at the step's first block, where it now waits until the committed blocks above are published and fails two blocks later); fifteen functional tests pass, `feature_connect_window.py` in both variants, `feature_block.py`, `feature_pruning.py`, `feature_assumeutxo.py`, `rpc_invalidateblock.py` and `mempool_reorg.py` among them. Harness, chain DX, 16 workers, hash d4ba681a7662 with BIP30 off and on, foreign load 2 to 11: BIP30 off, blocks/s against 673064eebc: 590 and 566 against 630 and 627 at `-dbcache=2000` (CPU 47.8 s against 43.8 s), 487 and 507 against 513 and 515 at 450, 53.0 and 56.5 against 56.8 and 55.9 at 30; RSS within 20 MiB either way. BIP30 on at 2000: series 135.6, 673064eebc 129.8, step 1 132.9 to 137.7, the check's database misses setting the rate for all three. So the exact table costs 6 to 10% on the harness at full cache and less at the low end; not yet tried: a cheaper table for the inputs. Not tested: the take-back under a reorganisation target and after a failed publication (only the stop path runs it in a test), dbcrash, the full functional suite, mainnet. Known and left: between a commit and its publication the store is ahead of the tip for readers outside the window (RPC, mempool acceptance).
- Reading of the outside branch `utxo-partial-order-v2-c2` (willcl-ark, tip 5f3a31415c on 8dce01e4a2, three commits, +3510/-6038; gist 9797efb9; read 24 November, nothing built or run). Same idea as the trial, one structural difference: a window block's changes reach the store only in `CommitWindowBatch()`, on the connector under cs_main in the lock hold that also moves the tip (up to 64 blocks, shards in parallel on a commit pool of at most four threads, block order kept inside a shard). The store so never runs ahead of the tip: no take-back, no gate on write-outs, no reader that sees unpublished state. The overlay there is keyed by txid, holds creates and spends inside one block only, and is not exact: a double spend across window blocks and a BIP30 repeat are found at the commit from the table's state, the commit rolls back to the longest prefix without a conflict, and the conflicting block is connected alone for the verdict; a write-out between a block's reads and its commit is covered by an epoch each block records, with database reads under the shard lock. Their figures (8 cores/16 threads, `-dbcache=32000`, 975,073 blocks): 1,423 s connect against 1,541 s for the base, after 1,656 s before the commit was fused and given its own pool. Carried into the trial on the maintainer's word: the commit at publication (next entry). Not to carry: the flat 88-byte-slot tables (they drop the node depot, charge `-dbcache` by capacity, collect a run by scanning whole tables, and leave window database reads uncached: against the per-coin memory ruling and unmeasured below 32 GB); the keyed xor/multiply hash in place of SipHash with shards chosen from unsalted txid bytes; `WriteCoins` chunking without a marker. Already on the series or the trial: 256 shards, `ReallocateCache()` after extract, the referenced bit written once, prefetch at lookup, commit items that point into the block (their arena contention came from a `StoredCoin` per item). Small and open: a reference that skips dirty entries. By reading, their tree has no way back when `FinishConnectBlock()` or the flush fails after an accepted commit, and none of what the last commit added has a unit test (tests: about +320/-2300, no functional test touched).
- Commit at publication on the trial (24 November, trial tip a488bec285, one commit on step 1). The connector, in the hold that publishes the front block, forms one job of that block and the complete, valid blocks above it (32 at most, none above the target), applies it with the workers that come by through their help point, and publishes every block of the job before the hold ends; the store is at the tip whenever cs_main is free. The overlay stays exact (creates and inputs), so the commit cannot conflict and there is no rollback, unlike the outside branch; netting across the batch is kept. A job is one block where a write is due by the clock, for pruning or at a critical cache (the write is made at that block), under a stop request or where a publication limit is reached, and eight blocks at most while the cache is large; a run in flight is settled before a commit. Removed: the take-back in `DrainWindow()`, commits started by workers, the gate's job count and run wish; `ahead` counts only the blocks of the running hold. A committed block that cannot be published aborts the process (nothing of it is written). A stop request ends the step once the hold's committed blocks are published. Tried and dropped: helper tasks submitted to the connect pool at each commit (no gain on the harness, and `window_step_block_by_block` counts queued tasks). Checks: unit suites pass but for the two `validation_chainstate_tests` cases whose expectations are the slot path's (`failing_deferral_ends_a_window_step`, `write_out_under_open_blocks_costs_a_retry_and_no_verdict`); `failed_periodic_write_out_ends_a_window_step` passes again; the fifteen functional tests of step 1 pass. Harness, chain DX, 16 workers, hash d4ba681a7662 with BIP30 off and on, foreign load 2 to 12, blocks/s against step 1 with BIP30 off: 534 and 559 against 586 and 587 at `-dbcache=2000` (single-block-on-large form: 570 and 556), 456 and 473 against 495 and 491 at 450, 41 against 54 at 30; BIP30 on: 127 and 139 at 2000, 136 and 137 at 450, the run at 30 no longer inside the minute. At 30 the counters read: 1,012 commits of 6.3 blocks, 11.1 s of a 45 s run in commits on the connector, 6 lines of a cache over its total space and 390 MiB RSS; step 1 had 2,530 commits of 2.5 blocks made beside the publication, 1,695 such lines and 517 MiB, so its rate there rested on running over the budget; the series does 48 at 30 with 460 MiB, the master baseline did not finish in the minute. So the form costs about 5 to 7% at full cache and 450 against step 1 on the harness (the commit's time is now inside the hold) and is below the series at 30. Against the series itself, same harness and day, BIP30 off, load 1 to 6: 557 and 570 against 597 and 600 at `-dbcache=2000`, 488 and 471 against 454 and 425 at 450, 41 against 48 at 30; so 5 to 7% below the series at full cache, 7 to 11% above it at 450, 15% below at 30. Conflict detection at the commit (the outside branch's form, on the user's instruction of 2026-11-24; built as c7e8161c86 and dropped the same day on the user's word, since it gave no gain): the overlay holds creates only, a block's worker does not learn what a lower block in flight spends, `ApplyConfirmed()` notes what each change found and `RevertConfirmed()` puts it back; a commit job with a conflict is taken back whole and its blocks are committed one by one, the block that conflicts alone fails in the window and is connected alone. No epoch as in the outside branch: a miss reads the database. Checked: unit suite (the two cases that fail by expectation, no third), the fifteen functional tests, and a throwaway variant of feature_connect_window.py that holds the fork's lowest block back so both spends of a coin are in the window (three commits taken back, end state right). No measurable gain: harness, BIP30 off, foreign load rising 2 to 9 over the grid, series / trial before / with it: 560 and 553 / 568 and 543 / 561 and 555 at `-dbcache=2000`, 433 and 434 / 473 and 470 / 452 and 457 at 450, 47.5 and 48.5 / 40.5 and 40.2 / 42.1 and 40.5 at 30; time in commits up from 1.36-1.42 s to 1.46-1.48 s at 2000 and from 1.58 s to 1.75-1.83 s at 450 (the revert notes are written in the hold); UTXO hash d4ba681a7662 with BIP30 off and on. What it buys is the removed input half of the overlay, not speed. Commit pool (the outside branch's sizing, a quarter of the connect threads, one to four; trial tip 6afae10107 on a488bec285): its threads take shard groups of a running commit beside the publishing thread, one asked per 256 changes of the job. Harness, BIP30 off, foreign load 4 to 9, series / trial before / with the pool: 573 and 594 / 556 and 554 / 586 and 596 at `-dbcache=2000`, 420 and 431 / 459 and 464 / 498 and 494 at 450, 48.8 and 47.5 / 42.2 and 41.5 / 42.7 and 44.6 at 30; group phase of the commits 1.35-1.46 s to 1.11 s at 2000, 1.56-1.63 s to 1.16 s at 450, 10.6-10.8 s to 7.5-8.1 s at 30; UTXO hash d4ba681a7662 with BIP30 off and on; unit suite (the two expected failures) and the fifteen functional tests pass. So level with the series at full cache, 16% above it at 450, 8 to 10% below at 30. The diagnostic end time of a job's group phase was read before it was written now and then; an atomic since this commit. Not tested: the abort path, dbcrash, the full functional suite, mainnet. Left: the input half of the overlay tables (removing it needs conflict detection and a rollback at the commit).
- The trial carried onto the series tip (24 November, on the maintainer's word "carry them over here"): the twenty trial commits rebased onto cecbce8958, tip b4d715b782, 54 commits on 19128e8b77, so the trial has the fixes of 23 November (IO-4, IO-5, IO-10, NET-3, NET-4, VAL-3, CS-5, CS-6, CS-8 part 1, CS-10, the kernel's settled undo writes, `dumptxoutset` without undo data). Two conflicts, both additions on the same lines of `validation.cpp`. Harness, chain DX, BIP30 off, 16 workers, foreign load 6 to 10: 587.5 and 605.3 blocks/s at `-dbcache=2000` against 624.4 and 603.0 before, 528.4 and 530.9 at 450 against 535.4 and 523.5, UTXO hash as expected: level. CS-5 read again for the trial: `Height::Oldest` differs from the earlier rule only where a dirty, unwritten entry that is not fresh is changed again, which a forward commit does only for an outpoint made twice; the connector settles a run in flight before a commit, so no run collects inside a commit or a take-back. One fix from that reading, in the take-back's commit: a coin a take-back puts back was filed under the height below the first block taken out; made above the settled height it is not in the database, and a run bounded between its creation and that height would have settled the database without it (lost by a crash before the next run), the case `TagFor()` covers for a disconnect; `CreateConfirmedLocked()` now files it under its creation height. Unit suite: the two cases that expect the old path fail (`failing_deferral_ends_a_window_step`, `write_out_under_open_blocks_costs_a_retry_and_no_verdict`), nothing else; fifteen functional tests pass. The take-back was exercised by no test then (a unit case since, 4.5). Plan for the consolidation and the removal of the block identities from the store: `/work/parallel-validation/overlay-consolidation-plan.md`; carried out, the series' store knows no block before it is valid (4.2).


### 7.14 Will's utxo-v4 against the series on a hardware matrix (an outside run, 28 November)

Source: https://utxo-partial.fish.foo/ (results.tsv, summary.md and commentary.md copied to /work/parallel-validation/fishfoo-2026-11-28/; the page's readings are its author's). `-reindex-chainstate` from 0 to 950,000 on an i7-11700K (8 cores, 16 threads, 64 GiB), blocks on an NVMe 970 EVO Plus, chainstate on an NVMe 990 PRO, `-par=16`, `-debug=bench`; memory capped by a systemd cgroup (`MemorySwapMax=0`): none at `-dbcache=32000`, 8 GiB at 4000, 4 GiB at 450 (running at the time). Builds: Will's `utxo-v4-series` at d65786dcbb (20 commits on 0958604118), and benchcoin PR 201 at 0cd346e7b860, whose tree is the series at 3fa620877f (the block bins back, before the store's cell pools, the prune and the batch of 28 November) plus a design-notes commit.

| Profile | Branch | Connect (s) | Wall (min) | to 300k / 700k / 900k (s) | Peak anon / cgroup (GiB) | Chainstate read / write (GB) | Flushes (s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 32000, no cap | utxo-v4 | 912.9 | 16.65 | 21.5 / 436.3 / 809.7 | 20.23 / 22.87 | 2.35 / 21.79 | 1 (68.85) |
| 32000, no cap | the series | 892.3 | 15.66 | 34.4 / 440.1 / 796.9 | 19.12 / 23.39 | 0.58 / 14.02 | 1 (37.50) |
| 4000, 8 GiB | utxo-v4 | 3720.5 | 62.40 | 21.8 / 618.5 / 3002.5 | 6.17 / 8.00 | 14,607 / 186.1 | 16 (317.34) |
| 4000, 8 GiB | the series | 4181.4, killed (OOM, rc 137) in the final forced flush | 69.87 | 34.5 / 482.5 / 2958.0 | 7.76 / 8.00 | 15,699 / 160.8 | 14 (341.08) |

Reading:
- At 32000 the series is 1.023x faster on the connect and a minute faster on the wall: it trails to 300k (34.4 against 21.5 s), is level to 700k, and gains from 700k on (452.2 against 476.6 s), where the store's density counts; its shutdown flush writes 14.0 GB in 37.5 s against 21.8 GB in 68.9 s. Part of the early gap is the run's `-debug=bench`: the series writes the per-block bench lines for every block (329 MB of log by 287,000 against 79 MB); the rest is the known cost of small blocks (5.7).
- The page cache: the series leaves the block files' pages in the page cache (21.3 GiB of file cache by 338,000), utxo-v4 drops a block file's pages when it closes it (`POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED`) and stays at about 2 GiB. The series released them until 26 November (the block-file page-cache release, cut on the outside reviewer's trim list, 5.9). Whether this is the system time the maintainer's runs have kept above those of 25 and 26 November (7.1, 170 to 230 s) is not measured.
- At 4000 under 8 GiB: the series leads to 800,000 (707.3 against 963.5 s) with more of the set in memory, then its anonymous memory reaches 7.76 GiB (3.76 over `-dbcache`, utxo-v4 6.17), the page cache left to LevelDB falls to about 510 MiB, utxo-v4 is 1.70x faster from 900,000 to 950,000, and the series is killed in its final forced flush. The memory beside the cache at 4000 on late mainnet blocks has not been measured against master (the yardstick of 7.6 is at the floor, four threads, on the harness), nor what the final forced flush adds at its peak. The series' chainstate size at 4000 (13,380 MiB) is that of an interrupted flush.
- Will's branch since the 27 November comparison (f2fbb6de86): every commit changed (49 files, 5,912 insertions and 960 deletions between the two), among them a salted window output table, a concurrent block reader with a pread cache and the page-cache drop, and 80-byte slabs in its sharded view; 6,881 production code lines over master, against 6,013 then and the series' 6,274 at 3b19733898 (6,672 at the benchmarked 3fa620877f). Fetched to `refs/remotes/will/utxo-v4-series` and its four parts.
## 8. Dropped, withdrawn, and the simplification review

### 8.1 Tried or weighed and dropped

The reason stands where the subject is; this list exists so that none is proposed again unknowingly.

- Block bins (dropped 26 November, 5.9): a block built on the connect pool freed by the thread that built it, a bin per thread emptied before its next block. Under the mutating window 1.08 to 1.13x on the harness's reindex and 1.023x on mainnet (7.3); on the read-only window, at 3dce4e6d4b, the chain DX sync inside the spread without them (1.1% lower at `-dbcache` 1024 and 450) at 1.05x the client CPU. Up to a window of freed blocks, about 170 MiB, outside every account.
- The connect half of a reorganisation's read-ahead (dropped 26 November, 5.9): up to 16 blocks of the new branch read before the hold. In a shallow reorganisation they are in memory; PR #36000 reads the same blocks ahead; never measured.
- The pool per shard and the kept-node bound of 8192; the duplicate `HaveInputs` (7.3; built all the same on 16 November as F18 of performance-suggestions.md, on the maintainer's go, the 0.54% is the size to expect).
- Notifying the connector only for the lowest block; one bin shared by all threads; a freeing thread, a preallocated region, another allocator (4.3).
- Master's pool allocator for the tier (11% more coins for the same bytes), not built: master's pool returns memory only when destroyed, the rebuild would have to be driven by the budget, and the chunk size chosen so the shards do not overshoot a small budget.
- Overlapping lag runs with publication (4.4).
- 1024 shards as the remedy for the flush stalls (4.8).
- A rule that opened the window below the BIP34 height, and the round for a run below 20 minutes with its instrumentation (7.1); the height clause itself went later, for a different reason (4.6).
- A read-ahead hint for block bytes (13:49.90 on one mainnet run, no gain, 7.1); the continuation's batched fetch and input coins by position (14 November, 7.3; none showed a gain on the mainnet runs); skipping the wtxid for a block read from disk (7.4).
- The young table (option 2 of the maintainer's grouped-commit notes; debug-only `-coinsyoungtable=<n>` entries a shard): a small table probed in line inside each shard's map for the coins blocks create, a create that is neither BIP30-checked nor an overwrite made there without a probe of the main table, every lookup probing it first, a full table moved on to the buckets. At 1024 entries the 256 shards held about 262 thousand entries in about 8 MiB of slots, at 4096 four times that. 0.930x and 0.945x on the maintainer's full-memory reindex run (7.10), dropped on 2026-11-18.
- One counted byte budget for parsed blocks; removing the effects thread, in the hold or on the connector; a block cache default of an eighth of the coins cache (7.7).
- The hash pool and `DeserializeBlock()` (4.7).
- A write-out run at every prune check (4.10).
- A mainnet snapshot for the network work (4.7); asking one peer for more than sixteen blocks; a sync from signet (7.5).

- Another form of the duplicate input check in `CheckTransaction()` (2026-11-25, `build-bench`, block 413567): master's `std::set` 849 µs a `CheckBlockTest`, comparing pairs up to eight inputs and sorting above 554 µs, sorting alone 596 µs; on the 24,000-input worst case 8.2 against 2.5 ms. About 0.3 ms of a block's 36 ms of worker CPU, invisible in a sync, for a change to the CVE-2018-17144 check; master's check stays.

- Writing a write-out run for memory beside the commits (the run collected when it starts, the connector not waiting for it): tried on 25 November, 1.07x on chain DX at a 30 MB cache with the UTXO hash kept (7.1); dropped on the maintainer's word, not to be proposed again for this series.
- A thread that made the store's next node region ready ahead of its use, once the nodes came to 1 GiB: on the series until 25 November, then dropped from it on the maintainer's word. On the read-only window the maintainer's runs without it and with it were 9:57.45 and 9:55.60 (7.1: wall 1.003x, system 1.019x), inside the spread; it had paid 1.024x when window workers inserted into the store themselves.
- Three store ask-aheads for the commit's apply: a transaction's outputs in neighbouring buckets (groups of sixteen by index), a create asking ahead only for the key of the node it may meet, a spend asking ahead for the list neighbours it writes. On the series until 25 November, then dropped from it on the maintainer's word: without them 9:56.80 against 9:55.60 (7.1), wall 1.002x, user CPU 1.021x; they saved CPU off the critical path, and the hold probe had found a commit 12 to 20% shorter worth nothing in rate.
- A free list of map cells per shard in place of the lists per thread (25 November): every insert and erase of the store holds the shard's mutex, so the list needs no lock and no thread-local state, and the depot would only carve fresh cells (50 lines fewer). Chain DX, 16 workers, three alternating rounds: 0.974x at `-dbcache=1024` and 0.979x at 450 (slower than its paired run in all six rounds), level at 30; peak RSS 15 to 16 MiB higher at 1024 and 450 (half-full lists in 256 shards). Not sent to mainnet. Unlike the dropped pool per shard (7.3, T1) it shared the regions; it still stranded free cells in their shard.
- Window-wide key-sorted database lookups (an outside suggestion, 25 November; read, not built): a window's cold misses sorted by key before the coins database. Its premise of thousands of 2 MiB tables thrashing a 64-file table cache predates the base: tables are 32 MiB (`DBWRAPPER_MAX_FILE_SIZE`, upstream #30039) and 64-bit Unix keeps LevelDB's 1000 open files, so a mainnet chainstate's few hundred tables all stay open and their indexes and filters in memory. What is left is the read-ahead around each mapped page (about 48 KB a lookup in the memory-bound sync, 7.1): a window's tens of thousands of misses fall about one per 260 KB of a 13 GB database, so even sorted about one neighbour in five shares a read-ahead window, some 15 to 20% of the bytes read where the database is read at all, against holding a block's reads until a window of blocks is parsed. Not worth it for the series; on a disk that seeks, the ordering might count for more. S6 of 5.4 (one block's prevouts in key order) gains nothing at all: one block's misses are about 0.2 a table.
- Removed on 26 November after an outside reviewer's trim list, each with its record in 5.9: the block-file page-cache release; the window commit's multi-job scaffolding; the uncalled prefetch functions (`BlockApplyView::Prefetch()`, `WindowCoinSource::FindCoins()`, `CoinsStore::Fetch()`); the durability state (the flush order table stays); the catch-up mode; the lock-task layer, replaced by receive tasks on a pool of their own; the byte ledger (`util::ByteAccount` stays); `SyncMode`. Tried and not taken: the three outpoint tables of validation.cpp as one helper (0.96x at `-dbcache=1024`), and taking the receive pool or all net processing changes out of the series (5.9).
- The spend hints (P7; built 18 November, ported onto the series tip 19 November): `ProcessBlockMessage` noted a requested block's inputs in a direct-mapped table of 2^20 atomic words (8 MiB), and a write-out run made for memory left a fresh confirmed coin within `HOLD_DEPTH` (144) blocks of the run's head if a note named it. The maintainer's assessment of 18 November: fairly useful for IBD. Without them, on chain M at `-dbcache=30`, 3.9 of 8.8 million written coins were erased again later. Harness syncs of chain DX (three master peers, client on 8 cores, three alternating rounds against the tip, `cpu/hints_ab.sh`, log `cpu/hints-ab-2026-11-19.log`): at `-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5` 45.4 against 41.2 blocks/s (medians; 44.0 / 46.0 / 45.4 against 41.2 / 41.1 / 41.7), client CPU 177 to 181 against 195 to 198 s, coin changes written in runs 13.3 against 16.85 million (-21%), 1,517 against 1,937 MiB, erases 6.57 against 8.34 million, peak RSS 434 to 441 against 476 to 495 MiB; at `-dbcache=450` 231.4 against 236.6, no write-out run in either. Local mainnet syncs to 250,000 the same day (`ibd/local_hints.sh`, log `ibd/local-hints-2026-11-19.log`; a plain master serving node, the client on 8 cores from a fresh datadir with `-dbcache=100 -maxmempool=5 -minimumchainwork=0 -assumevalid=<block 250,000>`, three alternating rounds): wall 2:35.50 / 2:27.71 / 2:27.75 for the tip against 2:29.14 / 2:30.79 / 2:30.68 for the hints, 854.7 blocks/s over heights 150,000 to 250,000 for both (one hints run 847.5), 45 write-out runs and 14.02 million coin changes written in every run; the hints held nothing, each run's bound being 3,800 to 9,600 blocks below the head. Dropped on the maintainer's word (19 November, "lets revert this change again"): they help only caches holding under about 144 blocks of changes (an estimate from the mechanism, not measured), and a deeper limit would cost crash replay length, more reorgs forcing a full write and more memory pressure for an unmeasured gain.
- Window matching (built 19 November on the maintainer's go after an outside suggestion to match spends inside the window before validating): a higher window block took an input a lower block had yet to apply from that block's announced transaction and held back only the matched spend. Harness, `-reindex-chainstate` of chain DX, `-dbcache=1024`, five alternating rounds: deferred transactions 671,000 to 27,000, 713,000 matched spends, no conflict; the first form 312.3 against 320.8 blocks/s at 8 cores and 481.6 against 505.5 at 14, after the position table and the merged apply 309.2 against 319.6 (8 cores, -3.3%) and 489.3 against 500.2 (14 cores, -2.2%, ranges overlapping), CPU per run within about half a second; finishing all deferred transactions had cost only 1.9 s of about 48 s (a throwaway probe). On the maintainer's machine 12:08.21 against 11:57.62 (7.1): 1.5% slower, 2.8% more CPU. A debug option `-windowmatch` was begun and stopped on the maintainer's word.
- A large block validated in chunks, as neighbouring entries of the window (a sketch of 20 November, asked by the maintainer after the steady-state breakdown of 5.2a; not built, considered and not taken, 5.2a). Estimated from the measured shares, not measured: at steady state the serial core of a heavy block, 8.4 ms, over about 8 chunks to roughly 2 to 3 ms, about 5 ms of about 50 ms from receipt to tip; a whole block with cold inputs and scripts to run perhaps 20 to 30 ms of about 115 ms; in initial block download no gain and a small loss possible, since the window already keeps the workers busy with whole blocks.

Parked or held and not on the series are in 5.4.

- **The block bins, brought back small (trial, 28 November).** The 10:11.49 run (7.1) kept the system time that came with the cuts of 26 November (about 1,670 s against 850 to 940 s before), whose cuts included the block bins (48 code lines and about 8 at the call sites). On the maintainer's word ("do a trial run for adding them back. See if you could do it with even less code"): trial 6ad26fecd1 on 08043e77b2, branch `utxo-partial-order-bins-trial`, nine code lines in `NewBlock()`: a thread-local bin (a mutex and a vector, shared), a deleter that hands the block back to its builder's bin, the builder freeing what came back at its next `NewBlock()`, the bin freeing the rest when its thread ends and no deleter holds it. Left out from the old form: emptying every thread's bin at the end of an activation outside initial block download, and the workers' emptying at the help point, so blocks that came back to a thread that builds no more (a receive thread once the download is over) stay until it builds again or ends, about a window's worth at most. The harness (chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, three alternating rounds each, no foreign load): at `-dbcache=1024` system time 4.8, 5.0, 5.3 s against 3.0, 2.6, 3.0 s, CPU 42.6 to 43.7 s against 39.1 to 41.0 s, 621, 574, 583 against 613, 638, 631 blocks/s; at the floor system time 7.1, 6.5, 6.7 s against 4.3, 4.2, 2.5 s, CPU 47.8 to 50.3 s against 41.4 to 45.5 s, 342, 359, 375 against 366, 357, 387 blocks/s; minor faults level, so the system time saved is not page faults. Folded into the window commit on 28 November (the maintainer: "Can you roll the bins trial into the series? We basically already measured its worth and 9 lines is nothing."), tip 3fa620877f; the trial branch is gone. Mainnet at that tip, the command of 10:11.49: 9:50.15, system time 1078 s against 1680 s, wall 1.036x faster (7.1).

- **Store-owned cell pools (28 November, taken into the series).** The maintainer: "It would be nice to get rid of it as a global." Branch utxo-partial-order-store-pools-trial, one commit 3d65740891 on the tip ("coins: a store owns the memory of its cells"): each store owns two pools (cells, spent cells) of 32 MiB HintedRegion()s carved into 16 KiB chunks and numbered by the store; a shard's map takes a chunk at a time and gives out cells itself under the shard's mutex, freed cells on a list of its own, linked by their first word, the next one asked for ahead of its taking. Every number a map needs is at hand (an insertion's from the pool or list, a chain walk's from its links), so no address becomes a number: no regions aligned to their size, no number slot, no MapNodeRegion() or Windows branch, no depot, kept lists or static region table. -148 production code lines (-128 coinsstore.h, -20 coinsstore.cpp); with comments and its tests 163 insertions and 371 deletions, 208 lines net (the test of the regions' alignment went with the mapping it tested). The series then stands at 38,718 production code lines, 6,524 over master (6,672 before). The mutex and carving are a non-template PoolRegions under a thin CellPool<T, KIND>: clang's lock analysis does not see a negative requirement of a member of a class template nested in a class (reproduced in a small file; the depot escaped it only by being reached through Shared()). Harness (cpu/pools_ab.sh, chain DX, BIP30 off, 16 workers, build-bench, TLC stopped, 3 alternating rounds), tip / trial blocks/s: 621.6 / 618.6 at 1024 (0.995x), 556.3 / 553.7 at 450 (0.995x), 373.7 / 376.7 at the floor (1.008x); the trial ahead in 2 of 3 pairs at each, pairs at 1024 spread up to 9%; peak RSS level at 1024, +8 MiB at 450, +11 at the floor (chunk tails of 512 slabs, freed cells kept by their shard); system time level; UTXO hash d4ba681a7662. Unit suite 403/403 and 7 window functional runs pass. Unlike the per-shard free list of 25 November above (slower in 6 of 6), no direction. Crosses that entry's ruling on the maintainer's order. Mainnet 9:45.53 against 9:40.25 (7.1), level; on the series as its 45th commit, tip cfe30be5ee; the trial branch is gone.

- **A prune of code production does not use (28 November, folded into the series).** On the maintainer's word ("If the lines are unused in production, completely delete them, don't leave a comment, or trace ... If they are used by tests, remove the tests!"), after three read-only compactness passes (validation; the store; net processing, primitives and I/O) and the outside reviewer's list. Deleted with their tests: `NodeRegionBytes()` and the pools' `RegionBytes()` (with `freed_nodes_serve_another_thread`), `WriteOutRun::chunks`, `WrittenHeight()` (tests read `WrittenHead()` or the run's settled height), `BlockCache::Has()` (two cache tests read `Take()` and `Count()`), `StoredCoin`'s converting, copy and move members (copy deleted; the round-trip tests assign a `Coin`), a `TraceUncache` overload, `ConnectBlockChecksBIP30()`, `StagedConnect::time_connected`, PreparedBlock's CheckBlock-failure flag and its parameter, dead forward declarations, the recorder's unused stream members. Compacted, the same behaviour: the transaction segment recorder for its one caller and `TxidCommit()` gone; `CommitGate` as `Chainstate::m_commits_ahead`; the entry's list tag as a `detached` bit; one table builder for the entry machine; `MarkBlockFailed()` for five transitions; `FinishImport()` for the two import tails; `Lookup()` into `GetCoin()`; `NODE_BYTES`; the dirty span sums as plain integers; `PrefetchAll()` absorbing its two helpers; `ShardIndex()` public; about twenty inlines and merges. Held for the maintainer: `WriteBlockUndo()` and `WriteBatchSync()` (master's bench and fuzz use them), `Lifecycle::State()`, inlining `DropUnusableCandidateChain()` and `MarkBlockScriptsValid()`, the receive hand-off in one `cs_main` hold (changes a hold, needs the Pools model). Production code 249 lines shorter (38,718 to 38,469; 6,275 over master); with tests 335 insertions and 654 deletions. Build without warnings, unit suite 403/403, 13 functional runs (window, block cache, lag, async undo, hand-off, stalling, compact blocks, reindex, pruning, assumeutxo) pass on the tree; folded into the commits that brought the code (no per-commit build, on the maintainer's word), tip 0055baa148.

- **A batch from the outside reviewer's second list (28 November, folded into the series; the maintainer: "execute on 1 and 2").** The receive pool stops once initial block download is over and none of its tasks is left (`PeerManagerImpl::StopReceivePoolAfterDownload()`, from `SendMessages()`), so its threads end and the bins of the blocks they built go with their last blocks (4.3): up to about 0.3 GiB of the download's last blocks no longer stays outside `-dbcache` for the node's life. The key hash's salt is the store's own and follows its `deterministic` (`KeyHash()`, `Map::Use()`). Dropped after reading: a small first chunk for the cache base's pool, since the apply views use their maps for each block's changes; only the store's own unused base map wastes a chunk (256 KiB), not worth a change to master's `CCoinsViewCache` (the idle views' pools after the download are noted in 5.7). Tested: `take_back_puts_back_a_coin_written_over_from_the_database` (a fixture with BIP30 off and BIP34 out of reach; a block above the double spender repeats an early coinbase whose coin a forced flush left in the database alone; taking it back restores the coin from the database); with the restore skipped it fails and the two other take-back tests do not. Not testable and removed: the take-back's read of undo data from disk (4.14's reading, 5.9). The thread pool's comment on releasing a task outside its lock gives a reason that holds. Build without warnings, unit suite and 12 functional tests (hand-off, stalling, window with and without BIP30, double spend, block cache, lag, async undo, compact blocks, reindex, assumeutxo, pruning) on the tree; tip 3b19733898.

### 8.2 Withdrawn readings

Claimed once and taken back; not to be claimed again.

- "The publisher at 60% of a core" and the bound of 1.6x derived from it.
- A window worker spends three to four times the CPU per transaction of the serial path: the serial figure was the connector's wall time while pool workers prefetch its inputs (`StagedConnect::FetchGuard`), not a thread's CPU (7.3 has the measurement).
- 1.3x for the sweep's removal (1.10x measured, 7.1).
- The idle time read by the length of the sleeps; it follows their number and kind (7.1, 7.4). Page faults from the allocator's growth as the cause of the extra blocking (a micro-test gives 1 to 2 points, not 7 to 12); the cause was the allocator's lock (7.4).
- The benchcoin readings before the artifact, "likeliest the peer" among them, and the write-out at the prune ceiling as the bound (7.5).
- Backwards scaling past eight workers: the BIP30 check on regtest (6.3). The plateau past ten workers at `-dbcache=450`: the window's byte cap (4.5).
- The weighting of the receive side against the store by lines, corrected by the ruling in section 1.
- "That simple fix" for the flush meant four flush threads; my reading of it as the stall fix alone was wrong. The maintainer: "You should ask back if you are not sure what I mean."

### 8.3 The two defects as found

Both closed (4.5, 4.6); kept for the mechanism.

- The BIP30 gap. A window block's BIP30 check did not see a coin that was never written and that a higher open block had spent, because the spend erased the entry at apply and an applier's miss does not scan the open blocks' records. It can only matter where BIP30 is enforced inside the window and a duplicate transaction can be built: a mainnet fork without the BIP34 block above height 227,931, regtest with `-testactivationheight=bip34@N` for N above 1, and mainnet from height 1,983,702; not on the real mainnet chain today.
- Exceptions on the pool. `RunContinuation` caught `std::logic_error` and `std::runtime_error` and had no guard like `RunConnectTask`'s. `ThreadPool::Submit` wraps a task in a packaged task, so any other exception was stored in a future nobody reads, `finished` was never set and the connecting thread waited for ever; the same wrapping meant an allocation failure on a pool worker did not terminate the node.

### 8.4 Job 2, the simplification review of 2026-11-11

Two fresh read-only reviewers; every finding checked against the code. The full record (instructions, reports, both rounds) is in `/work/utxo-partial-order-job2/`; here the outcome of each item. **Closed on 21 November 2026: nothing of job 2 is open.** The remaining points were checked by reading at tip 3c224b4d8b (`/work/parallel-validation/full-r13-verified.md`, section L) and the maintainer had them marked resolved.

- Taken on the maintainer's word ("fold in the ones that seem without a downside"): three dead window-block branches, `m_window_mutex`, the executor's tickets, an unreachable flush wait, `Shard::coins_usage`.
- The effects executor has no user of its own: the lag run, the undo write, and three completions that queue only to keep their order behind those two. It can go only if the lag run moves inline on the connecting thread (holds by trace, needs a small-cache baseline) and the undo is written inside the publishing hold, which is `cs_main` with the mempool mutex: about 1.35 ms for a heavy block in one harness sample against a block period of 1.67 ms on reindex, shown only by a full `-reindex` and a sync. With only one of the two it stays. The maintainer doubted the removal ("isnt the undo write kind of a big thing?"), and the runs bear that out: 0.82x on a network sync with both in the hold, 0.88x with both on the connector between blocks (7.7). It stays.
- M1, one final write path: `Flush()` as `Sync()` then `EvictClean(0)` then a rehash; the wipe, its helper thread, `m_marker_mutex` and one copy of the owed-erase rule would go. Its cost only a run with a 30 GB tier tells (5.2). **Resolved:** closed by the maintainer on 17 November (the parallel flush stays): the wipe, its helper threads and `m_marker_mutex` (in `CCoinsViewDB`) are still in the tree.
- The block-index batch generation guard (`m_index_commit_mutex`, two counters) is not proven dead with two chainstates (assumeutxo), where the other chainstate's synchronous `WriteBlockIndexDB()` may commit a newer batch while the first's is queued. A possible latent defect either way, not verified by a run. **Resolved, 21 November:** neither dead nor a defect. The mutex and counters are now an assertion of the commit order. The effects executor belongs to the one `BlockManager` both chainstates share; a run's batch is prepared and queued under `cs_main` and committed in queue order, and every synchronous index write of either chainstate waits for that queue (`SettleWriteOut()`) before it prepares its own. It stays.
- Smaller removals proposed and undecided: `PrepareWriteOut` as test-only, `m_tier_height` folded into `m_confirmed_height`, `m_defer_write_out_wait` as an argument, an enum for a window block's progress bools; the block-index states (5.1); `-connectwindow` as a knob (the maintainer's call); acceptance in three holds of `cs_main`, the try-lock pass of `VisitShards` and front submission in the thread pool (one run each); T3 (5.2). **Resolved, 21 November:** `PrepareWriteOut` no longer exists; the progress bools became the `WindowBlockState` machine; the block-index states were dropped; `-connectwindow` is debug-only; `m_tier_height` and `m_defer_write_out_wait` are cosmetic and stay as they are; the three mechanisms a run would decide (three holds, the try-lock pass, front submission) stay as built, no run made and none planned; T3, the retry lock (`CoinsStore::ShardLock`, 20 microseconds), was removed on 22 November: the shard mutex is plain (5.2). The thread pool exception defect seen during job 2 is closed: every task whose future is dropped is `noexcept` or only frees memory.
- Kept as essential: the window's retry rule (4.5), the script-execution-cache lock, deferral and continuations, the connector thread, the striped totals; and everything the low-end rule protects (dirty spans, clean lists and `EvictClean`, the lag runs, the owed erase, `CollectRecords`, `-test=write_out_every_block`). Deferral and continuations, the clean lists and `CollectRecords` have since left the series with the read-only window and the list-free store (4.5, 5.8).

## 9. Validation, export and the series

### 9.1 Export

The export is always `/work/patches/utxo-partial-order.mbox`, the whole series from its master base (`git format-patch --stdout <base>..<tip>`; the base is dd96035e03 today), checked for attribution and private strings, then sent. Commits carry no AI trailers. Nothing is pushed. The export applies onto its master base with `git am --3way` and gives the branch's tree.

### 9.2 What was validated

The night of 27 to 28 November, tip 08043e77b2 (44 commits) and its tree: every commit built and passed the unit suite in a worktree and build directory of its own (three passes: the first found three intermediate breaks of the re-cuts, an include, a member renamed in the fold and a stream concept used a commit early, each fixed in its commit; the second a test asserting the next commit's behaviour, moved to it); the full functional suite on a binary snapshot, 503 passed and 23 skipped; `feature_dbcrash.py`; ThreadSanitizer, 403 unit tests and 24 functional runs of the window, the receive pool, the flush and the new tests, no report. TLA+: every config of LagRuns (extended with the owed erase, 4.11), WindowCommit, WindowCommitSeq, WindowOutputs and Durability gave its recorded result, the code's configurations no error; FreeWorker not run, its `LockTaskLimit()` gone; on 28 November `ReceivePool.tla` took its place (below). Performance against fecda61f0f, both from build-bench's settings: chain DX `-reindex-chainstate` at 16 workers, 597 and 595 blocks/s against 608 and 632 at `-dbcache=1024`, 387 and 357 against 354 and 377 at the floor; the block deserialisation bench level (2.008 against 2.023 ms, five runs each, inside the spread); the tip replay level in every scenario (medians of the full, 80% and 50% mempool and the whole block 45.9, 88.1 to 94.6, 168.8, 126.6 ms against 46.9, 86.9 to 98.8, 166.5, 125.9 ms; the 80% case six rounds each).

At the first built state (2026-11-10, before most of the series): full unit and functional suites, `feature_dbcrash.py` and `feature_pruning.py` against snapshot binaries.

The rebase onto master 6f1365e34f: no conflict over 60 master commits. The leveldb subtree moved (#36779); nothing was measured against it.

The ThreadSanitizer pass at delivery (build-tsan, upstream's CI options, 2026-11-13). No data race and no lock-order report. Two findings, both fixed in their commits:

1. A forced flush stopped the sanitizer itself: a flush worker keeps every shard a batch took changes from locked until the batch is written, and with shards of few changes one worker held its whole range of the 256, past the deadlock detector's bound on mutexes held by one thread; six unit suites hung inside the sanitizer. Fix in the young-tier commit: `MAX_BATCH_SHARDS{32}`, a worker ends its batch before it takes a 33rd shard.
2. `p2p_ibd_block_handoff.py` failed on any build since the request sizing commit (a new peer is asked for one block until its first arrived, the test waited for twelve requests). Fix: a fresh test peer delivers one block first (`deliver_first_block`).

Covered: the whole unit set under the sanitizer with full options (script_assets skipped for want of its data file), fifteen functional tests under the sanitizer including the series' own six and `p2p_ibd_stalling`. Not covered: `feature_pruning.py` to its end (its full-chain `verifychain` exceeds the 60 s RPC limit under the sanitizer, natively 1.9 s; the limit was not raised; the parts before ran without a report), `feature_dbcrash` (by rule), fuzz targets, bench, the kernel library, anything above 16 cores.

Pass 3 at its tip 82a1b90f76 (2026-11-14), after the fixes of 4.11:

- Native (`build-clang`, all targets): no warning; the unit suite passes; the full functional suite against snapshot binaries, 517 entries: 493 passed, 23 skipped for what this build lacks (wallet migration and compatibility with old releases, IPC, USDT, ZMQ, GUI, the kernel tool), one failed. The failure is `feature_discover.py`, whose node could not bind 127.0.0.1:34092 at start; alone it passes. The runner's port base of 23000 puts the onion ports at 33000 and up, inside the kernel's range for outgoing connections, so another test's connection can hold the port; that reading was not confirmed, the failed node's log was deleted with the run's directory.
- ThreadSanitizer, the one pass (`build-tsan`, upstream's options from `test/with_sanitizer_env.py.in`): no warning in the build; all 366 unit entries pass (script_assets skipped for want of its data file) with no report; eighteen functional tests, `p2p_ibd_stalling.py` on both transports, pass with no report in any node's output: the series' own (`feature_block_cache`, `feature_async_undo`, `feature_connect_window`, `feature_coins_lag`, `feature_coins_lag_prune`, `p2p_ibd_block_handoff`), and `p2p_compactblocks`, `feature_reindex`, `feature_assumeutxo`, `feature_block`, `feature_coinstatsindex`, `feature_init`, `mempool_reorg`, `p2p_segwit`, `rpc_invalidateblock`, `feature_fastprune`. `wallet_reorgsrestore.py` was skipped: the sanitizer build has no wallet.
- Not covered: `feature_dbcrash` and the extended functional tests (by rule), `feature_pruning.py` under the sanitizer, fuzz targets, the kernel library, the benches after those fixes (the measurements of 7.7 are on the tree before them; they add a wait in `PublishConnectTip()` outside initial block download only, and a predicate in the prune gate), anything above 16 cores.

Model checking (`/work/utxo-partial-order-tla/`, TLC 2.19; README there). `LagRuns.tla` (23 November): the lag's runs, the two-head marker, restored coins and start-up's replay against reorganisations and crashes on the confirmed path; each of its four safeguards (`KeepWritten()`, `TagFor()`, `m_fell_below_written`, the settled height below the oldest change left) switched off alone breaks crash safety, the code passes. `WindowCommit.tla` (24 November, tip 609e733f58): one commit of a window as one step (the gate and the settle before a commit keep runs out of the hold), against every content of two window blocks over two coins (no change, a spend, a transaction's new output, a coinbase's output that may overwrite), every state an entry may be in before (absent, database only, clean copy, fresh, owed erase, coin over an owed erase, written over) and every commit length; after the commit and its take-back, a run at any bound, bounded or final, lands and the node crashes, and the replay of the marker must give the head's state. Checked: blocks that stay are valid alone, the store holds the last of them, the entry states (I4), the take-back meets no conflict, crash safety. Results at two blocks (5,834 states each) and, for the code, at three (71,810 states, 19 minutes): the code before b223f274b8 fails `StoreTruth` in one case (below); the code passes; switched off alone, the remaking of a netted coin, `Height::Oldest`, the put-back's filing at its creation height (`CreateLocked()`) and the gate each break an invariant, the netting changes no outcome, and the put-back's `possible_overwrite` is not load-bearing (a coin the take-back puts back without it is fresh only where it was fresh). Every conflict in the model is a block that fails alone. The case: a coinbase's output that overwrites a coin the store does not hold (only the database does; the historic duplicate coinbases) records no overwritten coin, because `ApplyConfirmed()` looks for one in the store's map only; if the commit then meets a conflict at or below that block, `TakeBackCommit()` inverts the create as an erase and the original coin is lost, to be erased from the database by the next run. Reaching it needs a duplicate coinbase and an invalid block in the same commit, so not on the valid chain. Fixed at b223f274b8: the store reports a create that may overwrite and found no entry, and the take-back puts back the database's coin for it, which nothing writes in the hold. Found by reading for the model and removed at b223f274b8: `CommitGate::run_active`, written and never read. `WindowOutputs.tla` (24 November): blocks entered into the sharded table by their workers (`Index()`, which waits for its ring place, sweeping released blocks meanwhile), read by the workers above (`Probe()` under a shard's mutex, the block followed through its place), published and released by the connector and taken out shard by shard before the place is cleared (`EraseOne()`); invariant: a reader that follows an item finds its block in the place; property: with fair workers and connector every block is published. The code's proportions pass (ring 4, window 2, two workers, six blocks: 166,735 states; window 3, three workers: safety at seven blocks, 73.2 million states, liveness at five, 3.3 million). Clearing the place before the items are out of every shard breaks the invariant (trace of 27 states); a ring as wide as the window passes; a ring narrower than the window deadlocks (block 2 entered first holds the one place, block 1 waits for it, block 2 waits for block 1). The code's ring is 256 against a window of at most 64, and since cdc90d5b22 a `static_assert` keeps it so. The models of 25 November, against 293b2f5a6f, each with switches that turn a safeguard back: `ReceivePool.tla` (28 November, in place of `FreeWorker.tla`, whose lock-task layer the receive pool replaced): the message handler's admission, receive tasks in two parts taking `cs_main` with the write on block I/O between them, window reads and unlinks on block I/O, connect tasks and the tip's prefetch on the connect pool, and the connector's four kinds of step; no deadlock and both invariants hold at two and three receive threads (28,913 and 175,747 states), receive tasks on the connect pool or a block's rest on block I/O deadlock with the connector in `DrainWindow()`, a task admitted beyond the threads breaks the admission bound)`, a reorganisation's held window and the tip's prefetch, the last also with receive tasks of the download mode outstanding): no deadlock at two and three workers; with the limit equal to the workers it deadlocks, and both counting mistakes made and fixed while the check was written break the `LockTask` assertion. `Durability.tla` (block and undo bytes against the block index and the coins' head, a crash at any instant, a failing sync with its latch, pruning): holds at four and five blocks and with a failing sync (up to 47.6 million states); the undo file marked at the write rather than the claim, unlinking before the index stops referring to a file, index writes after a failed sync, and a write's completion not marking its file again each lose durability; `FlushUndoFile()` keeping a file marked for a queued write is not load-bearing (above). Its first run reported a sync between a block's claim and its write that covered nothing; the code marks the file again at the write's completion (`UnpinBlockFile()`), the model did not, so the model was corrected. `WindowCommitSeq.tla` (two window commits in a row, the second validated against the store as the first left it): holds at two and three blocks (148,555 states), and each switch WindowCommit catches is caught after two commits as well; a retry between the windows does not occur in its vocabulary, which has no BIP30. Every earlier configuration of `LagRuns`, `WindowCommit` and `WindowOutputs` gives its recorded verdict and state count on 293b2f5a6f. Covered by reading instead: commit against a write-out run (every write into the store settles a run first in the same hold, and `ApplyConfirmed()` and `BatchWrite()` assert it), the shutdown order (`FinishBlockTasks()` waits while the connector, pool, block I/O and scheduler run: `ReceivePool`'s end state), and mode switches (a block message in the tip mode waits for the receive tasks and the connector; outside initial block download a window step sends each block's signal in the hold, behind its undo write). The hang of one `p2p_ibd_stalling_handoff.py` run (9.2) was hunted on the build with lock-order checks with a watchdog that captures every thread's syscall and futex address on a silent node: 1,200 runs at eight and twelve at once, no hang, still open.

The series at the tip, by what was run on it (what went on 26 November is in 5.9, the commits in 9.3):

- The maintainer's runs. On 24 November, on the tree of 525940e906 (the read-only window, after the store lost its block identities), the full functional suite failed in two tests. `p2p_segwit.py` expects the text of a block message that ends early, and the text names the reader the series reads a transaction with (`TxSegmentRecorder::read(): end of data`); the test names it now. `wallet_assumeutxo.py` prunes, after background validation, the file the validated chain's write position was left in; the position moves on instead of keeping the file (4.14 step 8, `LeaveCursorFile()`). With the two fixes (7cca06380f) the full functional suite passes, `feature_dbcrash.py` passed and a mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` took 10:35.95. On 25 November, on be59276522: the kernel library built and `test_kernel` passes, the full functional suite passes, a mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` took 9:44.91, and `feature_dbcrash.py --usecli` passed (7 minutes, 40 iterations, restarts [15, 6, 2], 34 crashes on restart, the UTXO hash the same on every node). The maintainer's per-commit builds of 590e4d6cd2 and 293b2f5a6f passed, so each commit built on its own then, and with 293b2f5a6f the kernel library built and `test_kernel` passed. On 26 November: a mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` of 10:07.16. All in 7.1.
- ThreadSanitizer on fe512d7a43 (25 November, `build-tsan`, libstdc++ uninstrumented, the CI's options and suppressions): the unit suite passes (403 tests) once one unit test was corrected in the store's commit (`flush_fills_batches_across_shards` called `SanityCheck()` while holding its counting base's lock, which the flush workers take under a shard's mutex: a lock-order inversion in the test only, and the run's one report; the workers take the shard mutex with a plain `lock()`, which DEBUG_LOCKORDER does not see); the functional suite against a snapshot of the binaries, with no ThreadSanitizer report in any node: 280 passed, 26 failed, 25 of them from a port base of 30000 inside the host's ephemeral range (binds of 127.0.0.1 failing), all passing when run again at 11000; two left, taken as passing on the maintainer's word: `tool_wallet.py`, whose 512-byte file size limit kills the sanitizer runtime itself (SIGBUS, exit 66; exit 0 without the limit), and `feature_init.py`, whose node twice did not act on the termination signal (no shutdown logged; the signal reached the process and was held by the runtime, which an uninstrumented standard library allows).
- In `build-clang`, each tree of 25 and 26 November up to the one that took out the first three items of 5.9 built every target without a warning and passed the unit suite (403 tests; 1059 cases on the tree of the outside review of 25 November, 5.2a) and between seven and twenty-five functional runs (the window's tests in both forms, `feature_connect_window_double_spend.py`, the store and lag tests, `feature_pruning.py`, `feature_reindex.py`, `feature_assumeutxo.py`, `wallet_assumeutxo.py`, `feature_io_errors.py`, `feature_index_prune.py`, `feature_remove_pruned_files_on_startup.py`, `p2p_segwit.py`, `rpc_blockchain.py` and the hand-off tests among them). The tree after the store lost its block identities passed the unit suite, 18 functional tests (the series' own among them) and a harness `-reindex-chainstate` of chain DX that ended on the expected UTXO hash. The later trees of 26 November (the durability state, the catch-up mode, the block bins, the receive tasks on threads of their own, the byte ledger and `SyncMode` out) have no build record here; the maintainer's 10:07.16 ran on the tip.
- Single changes: the commit that resolves a window block's inputs by position passed the unit suites (chainstate, store, coins, block validation, transaction), the twelve functional runs of 7.1 and `feature_connect_window_double_spend.py`, which fails with the block's spent table switched off. The take-back's restoring of a coin only the database held (4.6) has no test.
- Tests added on 25 November, each in the commit whose code it tests: a restored coin written before the run's marker survives a cut at any write (`restored_coin_written_before_the_run_survives_a_cut`; without the write before the marker every cut after the run's first write loses the coins, checked by removing it), flush helpers that come up after the store is gone, a block view's reset waiting for its prefetch, a block file the write position left synced by the write-out and the other cursor's file synced as well, snapshot completion after a failed flush (`STATS_FAILED`, the snapshot kept; checked by removing the guard, which invalidates it), and an outside reader during a reorganisation of five blocks; the framework option `--connectworkers`, with which five P2P and IBD tests run with two connect pool threads (the five variants pass, as do `p2p_ibd_stalling.py` and `p2p_invalid_block.py`). Not added: a reorganisation's target changed during its read-ahead and the use of the disconnect read-ahead made observable, which need hooks in production code (5.5).
- Not run on the tip: ThreadSanitizer, a compile of the kernel library on the harness host, fuzz targets, the full functional suite, `feature_dbcrash.py`, and a network sync on the maintainer's machine.

The specifications of the model checking above describe a block's record in the store and a run's scan of the records; the store has neither. Nothing models `ApplyConfirmed()` against a write-out run.


The pools model (`Pools.tla`, 28 November, the code at 08043e77b2; table in the models' README): tip_small, tip_sw and tip_rpc without error (37.6, 2.5 and 426.6 million distinct states); tip_hist without error to depth 87 (292 million states), not exhausted, stopped on the maintainer's word; each of the seven switches fails as intended (five deadlocks, one invariant, the receive pool's two). Not modelled: the receive pool stopped after the download.
### 9.3 The commits, in order

Branch `utxo-partial-order`, tip 3b19733898, 45 commits on master dd96035e03, which keeps witness stacks in their serialized form (bitcoin/bitcoin#36895). The series leaves `src/script` and `src/policy` alone and changes `src/consensus` only by refactors with the same rules (`tx_verify`: the sequence-lock overloads). The order follows the maintainer's rule ("The refactors go first!") and his word that the I/O work belongs before the connector and store work: the pure core, the machines and the executor come first (1 to 10), then the file work (11 to 14), so that the rule, the allocation by the write and the block I/O threads exist when the store, the window and the receive pool are added. Two small fixes to master's code the store would otherwise carry come next (15, 16). The store comes in four steps a reviewer can take one at a time: the store itself (17), the view that commits a valid block (18), the interface for the outputs of blocks not committed yet (20, behind the block cache, 19), and the window that implements it (24), which carries the take-back of a commit and its tests; three preparatory commits stand before the window (21 to 23). The hand-off to the receive pool (25) is followed by the request rule that paces a peer by its deliveries (26). The reorganisation read-ahead (27) stands before the check (28), which needs it for one of its named exceptions; the pruning waits (29) follow the window, where their call sites are. Then come the store check at shutdown (30), the hashing of a transaction from the bytes read (31), the store's inline scripts and 32-bit links (32, 33), the previous block freed off a new block's path (34), inputs by position (35), and the last two steps of the store's cell (36, 37). The commits before the check read and write under cs_main in places their successors move, and none was run with it. The rate figures in the messages of 32, 33, 36 and 37 were measured on cells that still had list links (5.8). The tip was built and tested as 9.2 records; every commit built on its own in the maintainer's per-commit builds of 590e4d6cd2 and 293b2f5a6f, and the commits as they are since then were not built one by one.

     1 03ded9a264 blockstorage: ReadBlock() reads into a buffer that is not zeroed first

     3 41798352f3 validation: a transition table, and the block index and block storage machines
     4 8639df7483 blockstorage: sync every written file before the index records it

    12 0f5719f723 util: a thread pool task can be queued before everything queued so far
    13 ff3222511c validation: atomic block check flags, and a contextual block check from the context only
    14 fd40a1d393 validation: a block is checked and written with cs_main released

## 10. Sources

The sources something was taken from, and those compared and not taken. The first research round's own list, with links, is the "Sources" part of `/work/utxo-architecture-research.md`. "M" marks a source the maintainer gave.

- The Delving thread "Libbitcoin for Core people" (delvingbitcoin.org/t/libbitcoin-for-core-people/1222) (M): post 34, the link the maintainer gave, is evoskuil's (existence and spentness need the block order, script validation only a partial order); sipa's reply, post 36, has the "negative UTXO" entry that cancels when the output arrives. Together the origin of the window and deferral (section 1).
- Libbitcoin's v4 database design: validation split from confirmation.
- Hornet Node, UTXO(1): a store written by age at a steady rate with nothing emptied (the young tier and lag runs).
- purpleKarrot, "Towards a Side-Effect Free Validation Library", parts 1 to 3 (purplekarrot.net/blog/bitcoin-validation-library-3.html) (M): the pure core and the script cache as a sequential, a parallel and a sequential phase; recorded in `/work/validation-refactor-plan.md`.
- Bitcoin Core PRs: 31645 (`-dbbatchsize`) for the size of runs and flush batches; 31875 (sorted batches) for the key-ordered flush (4.8); 25325 (the pool resource for the coins cache) as the precedent behind the pool-per-shard trial (7.3).
- Benchcoin PR 201 with the artifact of run 42992592996 (M), and a friend's mainnet sync log: 7.5. A friend's review report (gist willcl-ark/9fe44851f113830b361e6a1ebbddf709) (M): 4.11.
- Dice, "Malthusian Locks" (arXiv 1511.06035), and Karlin et al. on competitive spinning: a lock that always blocks does poorly for short holds, which is why the shard lock retried before it blocked; T3 removed the retries on 22 November after they measured as no gain here (5.2).
- BatchIt (ISMM 2024), the snmalloc and mimalloc papers, the glibc documentation: a free on a thread other than the allocating one is the hard case, and the answer is to hand objects back to their owner in batches (4.3).
- Lozi et al., "The Linux Scheduler: a Decade of Wasted Cores", and Gregg, "CPU Utilization is Wrong": read waiting for a core and sleeping apart from the on-CPU share (6.1).
- Ruben Somsen, "Out-of-Order Validation & The Single Spend Check" (gist RubenSomsen/21c45b1c57448881ce5007d884fb3f21) (M): read against the series in 7.6; its double-spend handling matches the store's conflict and retry rule, its single spend check needs a store that keeps spent outputs.
- Compared and not taken (7.6): UHS (Cory Fields, bitcoin-dev May 2018) (M), SwiftSync (gist RubenSomsen/a61a37d14182ccd78760e477c78133cd, Core PR 34004), Utreexo (M), Core issue 36476, DORA and Seastar (threads bound to data, the shape of the independent design).
- The survey of other implementations (5.4), all read by subagents, figures self-reported unless said: Lopp, "2025 Bitcoin Node Performance Tests" (blog.lopp.net; six cores, every script checked to height 928,000: Core 30.0 12 h 07, libbitcoin 4.0.0 20 h 51, Gocoin out of memory at 32 GiB, Mako and btcd days; the one independent comparison found); libbitcoin-node `chaser_download.cpp`, `chaser_validate_batch.cpp`, `block_arena.cpp`; Gocoin `client/network/data.go`, `lib/utxo`; rbitcoin at 94337954 (`confirm_run/scripts.rs`, `ibd/assign.rs`, `docs/ibd-memory.md`, `docs/external_findings`); the BSV node's `txn_grouper.cpp`, `DBCacheAllInputs()` and slow-fetch options; BU's `requestManager.cpp` and `ConnectBlockCanonicalOrdering()`; Flowee's `BlockValidation.cpp`; Teranode's `validOrderAndBlessed`; the BCHN bulletin of 2021-10-31 (LMDB); Zebra's RFCs 0002 and 0004 and its batch verifiers; reth's stages and prewarming, Erigon's ETL; Firedancer's tiles; Core 29491, 36000, 35195 and secp256k1 1134, 1789.
